L448: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L449: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L450: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L451: The Applicant seeks approval of INQOVI (ASTX727: decitabine and cedazuridine) for the treatment of adult patients with myelodysplastic syndromes (MDS) including previously treated and untreated, de novo and secondary MDS with the following French-American-British subtypes (refractory anemia, refractory anemia with ringed sideroblasts, refractory anemia with excess blasts, and chronic myelomonocytic leukemia) and intermediate-1, intermediate-2, and high-risk one International Prognostic Scoring System groups. The proposed INQOVI dosing regimen is one tablet (35 mg decitabine and 100 mg cedazuridine) taken orally once daily, on an empty stomach, on first five consecutive days of each 28-day cycle. Patients should avoid food 2 hours before and 2 hours after each INQOVI dose.
L452: The Clinical Pharmacology section of the NDA is supported by the evaluation and analysis of comparative pharmacokinetics (PK) and pharmacodynamics (PD) of INQOVI versus IV decitabine, multiple dose PK of decitabine and cedazuridine, and exposure-response relationships for efficacy and safety.
L453: Based on the efficacy and safety results in the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies in patients with MDS and the comparative PK and PD assessment of INQOVI and IV decitabine in the Phase 3 study, the proposed INQOVI dosing regimen of 35 mg decitabine and 100 mg cedazuridine as a single tablet administered orally once daily for Days 1-5 of each 28-day cycle with the dose modification schema is acceptable. The geometric mean ratio (90% CI) for the 5 day cumulative decitabine exposure between INQOVI and IV decitabine was 0.99 (0.93, 1.06). The high 5-day cumulative decitabine exposure was associated with a higher likelihood of adverse reactions, including any grade neutropenia and thrombocytopenia, that were mostly managed by dose delays and dose reductions.
L454: Analysis of the data provided by the Applicant showed that an increase in cumulative decitabine exposure with decrease in body surface area or body weight was not clinically meaningful. The cumulative decitabine exposures between INQOVI and IV decitabine were comparable for patients with mild to moderate renal impairment, mild hepatic impairment, and in the presence or absence of proton pump inhibitors. No dose adjustment is recommended for patients based on body size or in patients with mild to moderate renal impairment, mild hepatic impairment, and concomitant use of proton pump inhibitors. The results of the food-effect study were found to be unreliable, and the QT-concentration assessment did not adequately characterize the effect of cedazuridine on QT/QTc interval prolongation and post marketing clinical studies will be needed to address these issues.
L455: ## **Recommendations**
L456: The proposed INQOVI dosing regimen of 35 mg decitabine and 100 mg cedazuridine as a single tablet orally once daily for the first 5 consecutive days of each 28-day cycle in patients with MDS with the dose modification schema in the event of adverse reactions is acceptable. From a Clinical Pharmacology standpoint, the NDA is approvable provided the Applicant and the FDA reach an agreement regarding the labeling language.
L457: PMC or PMR	Key Issue(s) to be Addressed	Rationale	Key Considerations for Design Features

L459: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L460: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L461: ## Data:
L462: ## *Mechanism of Action:*
L463: ASTX727, a combination of cedazuridine (new molecular entity) and decitabine, is an oral hypomethylating agent (HMA).
L464: Decitabine is a nucleoside metabolic inhibitor that is believed to exert its antineoplastic effects after phosphorylation and direct incorporation into DNA and inhibition of DNA methyltransferase, causing hypomethylation of DNA and cellular differentiation and/or apoptosis. When administered orally, decitabine is degraded by cytidine deaminase (CDA) and has limited oral bioavailability.
L465: Cedazuridine (E7727) is a competitive inhibitor of CDA with a concentration giving half-maximal inhibition (IC_{50}) value of 281 \pm 58 nmol/L. The potency of the E7727-epimer to inhibit CDA is only 1/10^{th} of E7727.
L466: Oral administration of cedazuridine with decitabine enhances the oral bioavailability of decitabine via inhibition of first pass metabolism of decitabine in the gut and liver by CDA.
L467: ## *Pharmacodynamics:*
L468: - Administration of ASTX727 transiently increased plasma concentrations of cytidine as expected for the CDA inhibitor, cedazuridine.
- Administration of ASTX727 resulted in a similar reduction in long interspersed nucleotide element-1 (LINE-1) DNA methylation in peripheral blood mononuclear cells to that of
L469: administration of IV decitabine 20 mg/m2. At the recommended ASTX727 dose, a maximal or near maximal pharmacodynamic effect of LINE-1 demethylation was observed over the range of decitabine systemic exposures, based on PK-PD modeling.
L470: - The potential for QT prolongation effect of ASTX727 was evaluated in the Phase 3 study by performing limited electrocardiogram (ECG) assessment before and after dosing at the expected t_{max} of both decitabine and cedazuridine. No large mean changes (i.e., &gt;10 msec) in the heart rate-corrected QT interval (QTc) interval were observed. An exposure-QTc analysis suggested no concentration-dependent QTc interval prolongation for either decitabine or cedazuridine.
L471: ## *General Pharmacokinetics*
L472: - Cedazuridine exposures were dose proportional across the dose range of 40 to 100 mg.
- Decitabine 5-day AUC0-t over the dosing range of 20 to 40 mg was dose proportional.
- Both cedazuridine and decitabine reach steady-state on Day 2 of administration of ASTX727, with low accumulation ratios (1.3 to 1.6 for cedazuridine and 1.7 to 1.8 for decitabine).
- Steady-state concentrations of decitabine were reached on Day 1 of dosing with IV decitabine 20 mg/m2.
L473: ## *AUC equivalence:*
L474: The Phase 3 study showed that the extent of absorption (total cycle 5-day AUC0-24) of decitabine from oral dosing with ASTX727 was equivalent to IV administration of decitabine (Table 4).
L475: **Table 4. Comparison of Decitabine 5-day AUC0-24 Between ASTX727 and Intravenous Decitabine (Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02)**
L476: Treatment	N	Decitabine
5-day AUC0-24
Geometric LSM	Percentage Ratio (%) (Oral/IV) of
Geometric LSM
(90% CI)
Oral ASTX727	123	855.69	98.93
(92.66, 105.6)
IV Decitabine	123	864.94
L477: AUC0-24=area under the plasma concentration-time curve from time zero to 24 hours; CI=confidence interval; CSR=clinical study report; LSM=least squares mean; IV=intravenous; N=number of subjects.
L478: Source: CSR ASTX727-02, Table 22
L479: ## *Absorption:*
L480: - Following oral administration of ASTX727, cedazuridine was rapidly absorbed and the median time to maximum plasma concentration (t_{max}) was 3 hours (range, 0.52 to 7.9 hours). Approximately 47.5% of an oral dose of cedazuridine was absorbed and the oral bioavailability of cedazuridine was 20.7%.
- Following oral administration of ASTX727, decitabine was rapidly absorbed and t_{max} was 1 hour (range, 0.3 to 3). The t_{max} with IV administration was at the end of the infusion.
- When coadministered, cedazuridine (40, 60, or 100 mg) increased the oral bioavailability of decitabine (20, 30, or 40 mg) compared to that of oral decitabine alone by (R value) 3.68-12.0-fold.
- While administration of ASTX727 with a high-fat, high-calorie meal (approximately 800-1000 calories, with approximately 150 calories from protein, approximately 250 calories from carbohydrates, and approximately 500-600 calories from fat) had little effect on cedazuridine and cedazuridine-epimer exposures, decitabine exposure parameters were approximately 40% (AUC) to 54% (maximum plasma concentration [C_{max}]) lower compared to those following administration under fasted conditions. Administration with food delayed cedazuridine t_{max} slightly but had little effect on systemic exposures.
L481: ## *Distribution*
L482: - Cedazuridine was approximately 35% bound to human plasma proteins in vitro. The geometric mean (coefficient of variation [CV]) of apparent volume of distribution (V/F) for cedazuridine was 296 L (51%).
- Decitabine was approximately 5% bound to human plasma proteins in vitro. After administration of ASTX727, the geometric mean (CV) of decitabine V/F at steady state was 417 L (54%).
L483: ## *Elimination (Metabolism and Excretion)*
L484: ## Metabolism:
L485: - The primary metabolic pathway for cedazuridine was conversion to the epimer. There was also some dehydrogenation and some conjugation with urea.
L486: ## Elimination:
L487: - Renal clearance was the main route of excretion of orally absorbed cedazuridine and the cedazuridine-epimer.
- After oral administration of [^{14}C]-cedazuridine, 17.1% (range, 12.0% to 24.1%) of the administered dose was excreted in the urine as unchanged cedazuridine within 75 hours. The cedazuridine-epimer excreted in the urine was 17.5% of the orally administered cedazuridine dose.
L488: - After IV administration of [^{14}\text{C}]-cedazuridine, 80.9% (range, 73.5% to 88.4%) of the total radioactivity was recovered in the urine and 0.6% was recovered in the feces within 72 hours.
- After oral administration of [^{14}\text{C}]-cedazuridine, the mean total recovery was 96.9% (range, 93.2% to 100.2%) over the 120-hour collection interval with the majority of radioactivity recovered in urine and feces in the first 72 hours postdose (92.2%). The renal clearance (\text{CL}_r) was 4.46 L/h (range, 3.01 to 5.47 L/h).
- The major elimination pathway for decitabine is metabolic, by CDA, and physico-chemical degradation at physiological conditions.
- Following a single oral dose of ASTX727, the mean (CV) terminal elimination half-life (t_{1/2}) of cedazuridine was 6.3 hours (18%). The apparent clearance (CL/F) of cedazuridine was 30.6 L/h at Day 1 and 30.3 L/h at steady state.
- Following a single oral dose of ASTX727, the mean t_{1/2} (CV) of decitabine was 1.2 hours (23%) compared to 1.0 hours (47%) for IV decitabine. After administration of ASTX727, decitabine CL/F was 342 L/h at Day 1 and 197 L/h at steady state.
L489: ## Specific Populations
L490: - Age, sex, body weight, or mild to moderate hepatic impairment (by NCI criteria) did not have a clinically meaningful effect on the PK of decitabine or cedazuridine after administration of ASTX727. Mild or moderate renal impairment (NCI criteria) had an effect on cedazuridine exposures, but based on the relatively small magnitude of the effect this was considered not clinically meaningful.
- The effect of severe hepatic impairment or of severe renal impairment on the PK of decitabine and cedazuridine is unknown.
L491: ## Drug-Drug Interactions
L492: - The exposure of cedazuridine was unaffected by coadministration with decitabine.
- Model-based analyses of proton pump inhibitor (PPI) coadministration showed that PPI had no effect on the PK of decitabine or cedazuridine.
- Cedazuridine is a potent inhibitor of CDA, so it would affect the metabolism and exposure of any coadministered drug that is metabolized via this enzyme.
- In vitro, cedazuridine did not induce CYP1A, CYP2B6, CYP2C9, and CYP3A and did not inhibit any of the CYP isoforms tested (i.e., CYP1A, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, CYP2E1, and CYP3A).
- Cedazuridine was not an inhibitor of P-gp, BCRP, MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT2, CNT1, ENT1, or ENT2. A weak inhibition of CNT2 and CNT3 was observed, but only at the highest concentration tested.
- In in vitro studies, cedazuridine was not metabolized by human liver S9 fraction or liver cytosol and was not a substrate for the majority of transporters. Although cedazuridine was transported by CNT1, CNT3, and ENT2, the potential for drug-drug interactions with co medications is low.
L493: - Decitabine is not a substrate for CYP enzymes, and the plasma protein binding of decitabine is negligible. Decitabine is unlikely to inhibit or induce CYP enzymes.
L494: ## *Exposure-adverse event analysis:*
L495: - Model-based analyses of exposure-safety relationships using pooled data of Phase 2 part of Study ASTX727-01 and from Study ASTX727-02 (Phase 3) focused on mainly hematological parameters such as neutropenia and thrombocytopenia determined that none of the trends in these analyses were significant, suggesting a similar risk of thrombocytopenia or neutropenia with oral versus IV decitabine within or across exposure ranges and weight groups (quartiles) and that no dose adjustment is needed based on the subject's weight.
L496: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L497: Refer to Table 5 in Sections 6.3.1.
L498: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L499: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L500: ## Data:
L501: The selection of the dose and regimen is based on the decitabine 5-day AUC equivalence of ASTX727 with IV decitabine (20 mg/m2 Daily×5). Dose levels for the combination to achieve total cycle AUC that match the IV were confirmed in the Phase 2 study (Table 12 and Table 13) and the full AUC equivalence was established in the Phase 3 study (Table 6). In addition, ASTX727 treatment at the recommended dose and regimen achieved almost identical demethylation to IV decitabine, as measured by %LINE 1 demethylation (Table 8, Table 9, and Figure 3). Likewise, the safety data from the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies largely mirrors the expected safety profile of IV decitabine in the same patient population.
L502: Administration of ASTX727 with food (high-fat, high-calorie meal) had little effect on cedazuridine and cedazuridine epimer exposures; however, decitabine exposure parameters were approximately 40% (AUC) to 53.5% (Cmax) lower compared to those following administration under fasted conditions (Table 16).
L503: See Section 6.3.1 for more details.
L504: ## The Applicant's Position:
L505: The recommended dose of ASTX727 is 1 tablet containing 100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine taken orally once daily on Days 1 through 5 of each 28-day cycle for a minimum of 4 cycles. ASTX727 tablets should be taken with water on an empty stomach. (b) (4)
L506: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L507: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position regarding the proposed dose of one ASTX727 (35 mg decitabine and 100 mg cedazuridine) tablet once daily for first the 5-days of each 28-day cycle under fasted conditions, based on the demonstration of comparable PK and PD in Study ASTS727-02 between ASTX727 and IV decitabine and efficacy in Study ASTX727-01-B and Study ASTS727-02. FDA agrees with the Applicant's proposed strategy for dose modification for hematological toxicity, including the proposed schema for dose reductions.
L508: FDA does not agree with the Applicant's position regarding the food effect due to inconsistency in the PK results in Study ASTX727-04 (refer to Section 6.3.2.4). There is inadequate data evaluating the effect of food on ASTX727. Additional data regarding the effect of food on ASTX727 is needed as a post-marketing commitment. FDA recommends that patients should avoid taking food 2 hours before and 2 hours after administration of ASTX727, consistent with the fasted conditions followed in Studies ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02.
L509: ## 6.2.2.2. **Therapeutic Individualization**
L510: ## **Data:**
L511: Exploratory subgroup analyses by age (32 to 90 years), sex, body weight (41 to 158 kg), or mild to moderate hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt; upper limit of normal [ULN] up to \le 3 \times ULN) did not have a clinically meaningful effect on the PK of decitabine or cedazuridine after administration of ASTX727. Mild or moderate renal impairment (creatinine clearance [CRCL] \ge 20 to 59 mL/min/1.73\text{m}^2) had an effect on cedazuridine exposures, but this was not considered to be clinically meaningful. The effects of severe hepatic impairment or severe renal impairment on the PK of decitabine and cedazuridine is unknown.
L512: Drug-drug interaction studies have not been conducted with cedazuridine or decitabine. In vitro studies indicate that neither cedazuridine nor decitabine is likely to inhibit or induce major human cytochrome P450 enzymes. Neither cedazuridine nor decitabine is an inhibitor or a substrate of major human drug transporters. Cedazuridine is an inhibitor of CDA.
L513: Concomitant PPI medication was assessed as a potential covariate in the population PK (PopPK) model because of the possibility that such medications could affect the rate of conversion of cedazuridine into its much less active epimer. The assessment showed, however, that concomitant PPI medication usage did not have a significant impact on decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine-epimer parameters and subsequent exposures, suggesting that drugs modifying gastric pH are unlikely to affect the PK of cedazuridine or decitabine after ASTX727 administration.
L514: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L515: In regards to drug-drug interactions (DDIs), the following are recommended:
L516: - **Drugs metabolized via CYP450 pathways:** no modification
- **Drugs metabolized by CDA:** avoided concomitant use
- **Proton Pump Inhibitors (or other gastric pH modifying agents):** no modification needed
L517: In regards to special populations, the following are recommended:
L518: - **Renal Impairment:** no modification needed for patients with mild to moderate impairment. No data on patients with severe impairment
- **Hepatic Impairment:** (b) (4) No data on patients with severe impairment
L519: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L520: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position of no dose modifications based on age (32 to 90 years), sex, body weight (41 to 158 kg), body surface area, mild to moderate renal impairment (creatinine clearance \ge 30 mL/min), mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt; ULN to 1.5 \times ULN, or AST&gt;ULN). The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no dose modifications are necessary for the concomitant use of CYP modulators and that the concomitant use of drugs that are metabolized by CDA should be avoided. FDA agrees that no dosing modifications are needed with concomitant use of PPIs or other gastric pH modifying agents.
L521: There was only one patient with severe renal impairment (creatinine clearance 15 to &lt;30 mL/min) in the Phase 2 and 3 studies, and hence there is insufficient data to reliably demonstrate the effect of severe renal impairment on the PK and safety of ASTX727. Therefore, additional information regarding the exposure and safety of ASTX727 in patients with severe renal impairment and end-stage renal disease will be needed as a post-marketing requirement.
L522: We disagree with the Applicant's position on therapeutic individualization in patients with moderate hepatic impairment. There were only 3 patients with moderate hepatic impairment and no patients with severe hepatic impairment in the clinical studies. Hence, there is insufficient data to reliably demonstrate the effect of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on the PK and safety of ASTX727. Additional information regarding the exposure and safety of ASTX727 in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment will be needed as a post-marketing requirement.
L523: ## 6.2.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L524: ## **Data:**
L525: The studies included in the PopPK analysis (the Phase 2 parts of Study ASTX727-01 and the Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02) did not include subjects with severe renal impairment or with severe hepatic impairment. These studies also did not have sufficient racial diversity to inform potential correlations with regard to race or ethnicity. ECG evaluations with time-matched PK
L526: were included in Phase 3 but were limited to single tracing before treatment and another post-treatment at the expected t_{max} of decitabine and cedazuridine on Day 3 of Cycle 1 and 2. This was due to already demanding PK assessments in the study design to support the primary endpoint of the trial.
L527: A thorough QT study was not conducted with ASTX727 as these studies are challenging to conduct in this disease population with advanced age and often cardiac comorbidities and use of concomitant medications. (b) (4)
L528: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L529: Neither race nor ethnicity is expected to affect the clinical pharmacology of ASTX727, but this has not been verified.
L530: The effects of severe renal impairment or severe hepatic impairment on the clinical pharmacology of ASTX727 are unknown at this time.
L531: (b) (4)
L532: ## **Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L533: - FDA disagrees with the Applicant's assessment of the QT prolongation risk. FDA notes that the PK and ECG data from ASTX727-02, used in the Applicant's QT risk assessment, are not sufficient to adequately characterize the effect of cedazuridine from ASTX727 on the QTc interval (refer to 6.3.2.5 for details). Evaluation of the QT prolongation potential of cedazuridine will be required. This will need to be assessed as a post-marketing requirement.
- There is insufficient data to assess the exposure and safety of ASTX727 in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment (refer to 6.3.2.3 for details). This will need to be assessed as a post-marketing requirement.
- There is insufficient data to assess the exposure and safety of ASTX727 in patients with severe renal impairment and end-stage renal disease (refer to 6.3.2.3 for details). This will need to be assessed as a post-marketing requirement.
- The results in Study ASTX727-04 do not permit for an adequate assessment of the effect of food on the pharmacokinetics of ASTX-727 (refer to 6.3.2.4 for details). There is no data to adequately describe and label the effect of food on the pharmacokinetics of ASTX727. This will need to be evaluated as a post-marketing commitment.
L534: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L535: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L536: Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
The information in Table 5 below.
L537: **Table 5. Summary of General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics of ASTX727 (decitabine and cedazuridine)**
L538: Pharmacology
Mechanism of Action	Decitabine is a nucleoside metabolic inhibitor.
Cedazuridine is a competitive inhibitor of CDA with an IC50 of 281 nmol/L
Active Moieties	Cedazuridine and decitabine
QT Prolongation	The effect of cedazuridine on the QTc interval has not adequately characterized.
General Information
Bioanalysis	Validated bioanalytical assays (LC-MS/MS) methods to determine concentrations of decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine-epimer.
Healthy vs. Patients	Clinical studies of ASTX727, IV decitabine, or decitabine in combination with cedazuridine were conducted only in patients.
Single oral 100 mg cedazuridine: CL/F (L/hr): 28 (22, 48) vs. 31 (9-90) (subjects vs. patients).
Drug Exposure at Steady State Following the Therapeutic Dosing Regimen	At steady state following once daily dosing with ASTX727:Mean decitabine (%CV) AUCtau was 178 (53%) ng•h/mL, and Cmax was 145 (55%) ng/mL.
Mean cedazuridine AUCtau was 3291 (45%) ng•h/mL and Cmax was 371 (52%) ng/mL.

Dose Proportionality	Cmax and AUCtau of decitabine at 20 to 40 mg once daily in combination with 100 mg cedazuridine, and cedazuridine at 40 to 100 mg once daily in combination with 20 mg decitabine were approximately dose proportional.
Accumulation	The accumulation ratios based on AUCtau for decitabine was 1.7 (42%) and cedazuridine was 1.1 (63%).
Variability	Decitabine geometric CV was 53% to 56% for AUCtau and 52% to 66% for Cmax. Cedazuridine geometric CV was 46 to 47% for AUCtau and 49% to 54% for Cmax.
Absorption
Median Tmax	Decitabine: 1 hour (0.3, 3)
Cedazuridine: 3 hours (1.5 to 6.1)
Food effect (Fed/fasted)	The effect of food on the PK of ASTX727 has not been adequately assessed.
Distribution
L539: Volume of Distribution	Decitabine V/F at steady state is 417 L (54%)
Cedazuridine V/F at steady state is 296 L (51%)
Plasma Protein Binding	Fraction unbound for decitabine is 96% (4%) at 17 ng/mL to 94% (2%) at 342 ng/mL, and for cedazuridine is 66% (6%) at 1000 ng/mL to 62% (2%) at 50000 ng/mL.
Blood to Plasma Ratio	0.622 to 1.07 (cedazuridine)
As Substrate of Transporters	In vitro, cedazuridine was not a substrate for P-gp (up to 1000 µM), ENT1/4, MATE1/2-K, OAT1/3, OATP1B1/2B1, OCT1/2 (up to 50 µM). Although cedazuridine is transported by CNT1, CNT3, and ENT2, the potential for drug-drug interactions with comedication is low.
Elimination
Terminal Elimination Half-Life	After administration of ASTX727 in the Phase 3 study, the mean T_{1/2} at steady state of decitabine was 1.5 (27%) hours and cedazuridine was 6.7 (19%) hours.
Metabolism
Fraction Metabolized (% dose)	Cedazuridine accounted for 59% of total drug-related material in plasma. Cedazuridine-epimer and M266/1 metabolite accounted for 31% and 10%, respectively, of total drug-related material in plasma.
Primary Metabolic Pathway(s)	Decitabine is mainly metabolized via deamination by CDA found in the liver, and also in granulocyte, intestinal epithelium, and whole blood, and degradation via hydrolytic ring-opening.
The primary metabolic pathway for cedazuridine is physiochemical conversion to its epimer.
Excretion
Primary Excretion Pathways (% dose) \pmSD	Primary route of elimination of cedazuridine is renal: 99% in urine following IV dose. After an oral dose, 46% (21% unchanged) of the orally administered dose was recovered in the urine and 51% (27% unchanged) in feces.
Interaction liability (Drug as Perpetrator)
Inhibition/Induction of Metabolism	Cedazuridine is an inhibitor of CDA.
In vitro, cedazuridine is not an inducer of CYP1A, CYP2B6, CYP2C9, or CYP3A (up to 400 µM) or inhibitor of CYP1A, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6, 2E1, and 3A (IC_{50} >400 µM).
Inhibition/Induction of Transporter Systems	Cedazuridine was not an inhibitor of P-glycoprotein (P-gp), BCRP, MATE1/2-K, OAT1/3, OATP1B1/2B1, OCT2, CNT1 & ENT1/2 (up to 300 µM), and a weak inhibitor of concentrative nucleoside transporter (CNT)2 and CNT3 with ~30% inhibition at 300 µM.
L540: AUC_{\tau}= area under concentration-time curve during the dosing interval; CDA=cytidine deaminase; C_{max}=maximum plasma concentration; IC_{50}=concentration giving half-maximal inhibition; t_{1/2}=elimination half-life; t_{max}=time to maximum plasma concentration; CL/F=clearance; V/F=apparent volume of distribution.
L541: Note: PK parameters are presented as geometric mean (%CV) or median (minimum, maximum) unless otherwise noted
L542: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L543: ## 6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L544: ## **Data:**
L545: Yes. Supportive evidence of effectiveness is provided based on PK assessments in the Phase 2 (ASTX727-01-B) and the Phase 3 pivotal study (ASTX727-02) and based on pharmacodynamic effects in the Phase 2 and 3 studies.
L546: ## ***Pharmacology***
L547: In vitro, cedazuridine was a potent inhibitor of CDA (IC_{50} of 0.281 \mu M) and was able to increase the t_{1/2} of the CDA-mediated metabolism of gemcitabine from \le 36 minutes to \ge 66 hours.
L548: ## ***Pharmacokinetic Equivalence***
L549: In the Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02, ASTX727 administered Daily \times 5 was compared with IV decitabine 20 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 Daily \times 5. The primary analysis of the primary study endpoint showed that the 5-day AUC_{0-24} percentage ratio of geometric LSM for ASTX727 relative to IV decitabine was 98.93% (90% CI 92.66%, 105.6%). The 2-sided 90% CI was contained entirely within the prespecified range of 80% to 125% for the primary analysis (Table 6).
L550: Results of the secondary analyses further supported the primary analysis AUC_{0-24} (Table 7) in that all analyses show that 90% CI for the oral/IV ratio are contained entirely between 80% and 125%. The range of decitabine exposures after oral ASTX727 at the recommended doses (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine) largely overlapped with the range of exposures after IV decitabine at 20 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 (Figure 1).

L574: ## **Phase 1 Study:**
L575: Decitabine administration had no effect on plasma cytidine levels in Study ASTX727-01.
L576: As expected, on days when cedazuridine was administered in Cycle 1 of Study ASTX727-01, a consistent increase in plasma cytidine levels was observed. This appeared to be modest (approximately 2-fold) and transient, as evidenced by the trend of recovery toward baseline by 24 hours, and full recovery as seen at predose on Cycle 2 Day -3.
L577: ## **Phase 2 Study:**
L578: The results of the primary PD endpoint (LINE-1 demethylation) are presented below (Table 9 and Figure 3). There was no significant difference between the PD effect on DNA methylation measured by LINE-1 between oral and IV in Cycles 1 or 2.

L593: The Applicant's Position:
Yes.
L594: The demonstration of equivalent decitabine exposures between ASTX727 and IV decitabine based on total cycle AUC_{0-24} supports the PK bridging approach. Decitabine plasma AUC exposures are largely considered to be the principal PK determinant of both biological and clinical activity of decitabine and the ASTX727 5-day AUC was equivalent to the IV decitabine 5-day AUC (Table 6). Decitabine exerts its hypomethylating activity after incorporation into DNA during the S-Phase of the cell cycle, and AUC exposures would be consistent with driving decitabine activity as more cells enter into the S-Phase over the exposure window represented by AUC. This is also supported by similar PD effect of LINE-1 demethylation between oral ASTX727 and IV decitabine over the treatment cycle in Phase 3 (Table 8).
L595: The PK/PD analysis demonstrated that a maximal drug effect of LINE-1 demethylation was achieved for ASTX727 and IV decitabine 20 mg/m2 over the range of decitabine exposures in the Phase 2 parts of Study ASTX727-01 and in Study ASTX727-02 (Figure 2).
L596: Overall, the PK and biomarker response were similar between ASTX727 and IV decitabine 20 mg/m2, supporting the conclusion that ASTX727 has the same effectiveness as IV decitabine 20 mg/m2.
L597: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L598: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position on the effectiveness of ASTX727 tablet (35 mg of decitabine and 100 mg of cedazuridine) when administered orally, once daily for the first 5 days of each 28-day cycle under fasted conditions, i.e., 2 hours before and 2 hours after dosing. The Phase 3 study demonstrated comparable 5-day cumulative decitabine exposure (primary PK endpoint) between ASTX727 and IV decitabine as shown in Table 6, and that the 90% confidence intervals of the geometric mean ratio were within bioequivalence limits of 80% and 125%.
L599: Comparison of daily decitabine exposures (Table A) demonstrated that following the first dose of ASTX727, the Day 1 exposure compared to IV decitabine was 40% lower. However, following 5 consecutive once daily doses of ASTX727, the Day 5 decitabine exposure was comparable between ASTX727 and IV decitabine. Also, the decitabine exposures at Day 2 and Day 5 were comparable, indicating attainment of steady state at Day 2 for ASTX727. Nonetheless, the lower Day 1 exposure for ASTX727 is not likely to affect the efficacy, as
L600: - decitabine requires at least 4 cycles to show response per the Dacogen USPI, with a median time to CR+PR response of 162 days,
- the efficacy of ASTX727 in the Phase 2 study were comparable to those of Dacogen (see Sections 8.1.2 and 8.1.4), and
- maximum LINE-1 demethylation was observed at Day 8 suggesting a cumulative effect of exposure.

L613: ## 6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L614: ## Data:
L615: ## *Dose Selection Rationale*
L616: The decitabine AUC0-t after the single oral dose of decitabine alone on Day -3 of Study ASTX727-01 was lower than that for IV decitabine 20 mg/m2 at all oral doses tested in Phase 1 (20, 30, and 40 mg; Table 10). The oral bioavailability was consistently low. Decitabine exposures increased dramatically when administered orally with cedazuridine (Days 2 and 5 of Phase 1 Study ASTX727-01-A) compared with Day -3. The magnitude of increase in AUC0-t ranged from approximately 4- to 7-fold on Day 2 (first dose with the combination) and from 7- to 12-fold on Day 5 (Table 11).
L617: In the Phase 2 study (ASTX727-01-B), the selection of cedazuridine and decitabine doses of
L618: NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
L619: NDA 212576, Original
L620: INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
L621: 100 mg and 35 mg, respectively was confirmed to produce an oral/IV AUC ratio of ~94% in the Dose Confirmation stage using separate capsules (Table 12) and ~98% in the FDC stage using the FDC tablet (Table 13).

L639: The final confirmation of the dose selection came from the PK equivalence primary endpoint analysis in the pivotal Phase 3 study presented above in Table 6 (primary analysis), and Table 7 (sensitivity and secondary analyses). In addition, the levels and range of decitabine exposures after oral ASTX727 recommended dose largely overlapped with the range of exposures after IV decitabine standard dose (Figure 1).
L640: ## *Exposure-Safety*
L641: An exposure-safety analysis was performed on exposure and adverse event (AE) data from the Phase 2 part of Study ASTX727-01 and from Study ASTX727-02 (Phase 3). There was no statistically significant difference in the risk of thrombocytopenia or neutropenia with oral versus IV decitabine.
L642: The tables and figures below show model-based predictions of probability or the incidence of the most common adverse events of neutropenia and thrombocytopenia over a range of decitabine 5-day AUC exposures.

L658: ## The Applicant's Position:
L659: Yes. The PK data from Phase 2 and the pivotal PK data from Phase 3 support that decitabine exposures (mean, individual, and range) from ASTX727 recommended doses are largely equivalent or similar to IV decitabine standard dose of 20 mg/m2.
L660: This decitabine plasma exposure after oral ASTX727 recommended doses resulted in similar
L661: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## NDA 212576, Original
## INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
L662: pharmacodynamic effect on LINE-1 demethylation as IV decitabine 20 mg/m2.
L663: The proposed dosing regimen for ASTX727 is oral administration of 1 tablet (FDC of 100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine) each day for the first 5 days of each 28-day cycle. This regimen replicates the decitabine AUC exposures of IV decitabine 20 mg/m2 by 1-hr infusion each day for the first 5 days of each 28-day cycle. As such, this ASTX727 dosing regimen is appropriate to the general patient population for which the indication is being sought.
L664: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L665: The proposed dosing regimen of once daily dosing of ASTX727 tablet (35 mg of decitabine and 100 mg of cedazuridine) for the first 5 days of each 28-day cycle is supported by PK and efficacy results (Refer to Regulatory Authority Assessment under Section 6.3.2.1 and 8.1).
L666: There was a flat relationship between cumulative decitabine exposure and complete response (CR) based on pooled data from Studies ASTX727-01-B and -02 (Figure A)
L667: **Figure A: Relationship between 5-day Cumulative Decitabine AUC and Probability of Complete Response (left), and Responders by Exposure Quartiles (right)**
L668: Solid blue line represents a loess smooth of original data (tick marks along the x-axes), and the grey region indicates a 95% CI for the smooth. Error bars are 95% CI at quartiles of decitabine exposure.
L669: Source: Response to IR, Module 5.3.3.5, SDN39, 4/21/2020
L670: FDA notes that a higher incidence of adverse reactions for ASTX727 compared to IV decitabine were observed in Cycles 1 and 2. Additional details regarding these differences are provided in Section 6.3.2.3 and 8.2. Overall, for all cycles, the common adverse reactions with ASTX727 were cytopenias, specifically neutropenia, followed by infections including pneumonia and sepsis. Majority of these adverse reactions were Grade 3 or higher. Other common adverse
L671: reactions that were Grade 2 or less were gastrointestinal and general disorders.
L672: The exposure-response analyses for safety based on the observed PK data, indicated that 5-day cumulative decitabine AUC (for both ASTX727 and IV decitabine) had a positive relationship for any grade neutropenias and tended to be associated with any grade thrombocytopenias, particularly in Cycle 2 (see Figure B below). Further, higher 5-day cumulative decitabine AUC in Cycles 1 and 2 for ASTX727 appeared to be associated with any grade neutropenias in Cycle 3.
L673: **Figure B: Exposure-response for Safety in Studies ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02.** Relationship between 5-day Cumulative Decitabine exposure and any Grade Neutropenias (top left) and any Grade Thrombocytopenias in Cycle 2 (top right), and between 5-day cumulative Decitabine Exposure in Cycle 1 &amp; 2 versus any Grade Neutropenias in Cycle 3 (bottom)
L674: Refer to Appendix 19.4.4
L675: While the likelihood of increased cytopenias with increased cumulative decitabine exposure was observed, majority of these adverse reactions were managed by dose modifications in Studies ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02 (refer to “Dose Modification Strategy” subsection below). Further, comparison of the oral and IV formulations shows that the 5-day cumulative decitabine exposure tends to be similar with overlapping variability between ASTX727 and IV decitabine in patients with serious adverse reactions, neutropenia, and thrombocytopenia
L676: (Figure C below), indicating that ASTX727 is not expected to be more toxic than IV decitabine.
L677: **Figure C: Comparison of 5-day Cumulative Decitabine Exposure between Oral and IV Decitabine by Cycle (left) and Cycles 1 and 2 combined (right) in Patients with Serious Adverse Events (top), Neutropenias (middle) and Thrombocytopenias (bottom) in Studies ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02.**
L678: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## NDA 212576, Original
## INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
L679: **Dose Modification Strategy:** Dose modifications (dose delays/interruptions and dose reductions) due to hematologic toxicity were common with ASTX727.
L680: The dose delay strategy proposed by the Applicant for hematologic adverse reactions allows for dose delays if cytopenias persist after the first (b) weeks of treatment. Patients may continue dosing if counts recover within (b) weeks after dose delay. The Applicant's proposed dose delay strategy for hematological toxicity is reasonable. However, dose delays can be extended to 4 weeks, instead of (b) weeks, considering that majority of the dose delays were within 28 days with ASTX727 and to be consistent with dose delay criterion for IV decitabine.
L681: In Study ASTX727-02, 68% (91 of 133) patients had dose delays or interruptions. The adverse reactions leading to dose interruptions were mainly cytopenias, particularly neutropenia, followed by infections and infestations. Majority of the dose-delays occurred early in therapy, mostly in Cycles 2 to 4, with a short duration (see Table C). Dose delays greater than 14 days were observed in only 15% of the dose delayed cycles, however, majority were less than 28 days. The dose delays in majority of patients occurred in 1 to 5 cycles (median 2 cycles, range: 0, 15). The complete response (CR) rate in the patient subpopulation with dose delays were higher than in the overall population in Study ASTX727-02.
L682: **Table C: Dose Delays and Response in Study ASTX727-02**
L683: Event	Dose Delay Population
Patients	91
CR rate (vs. overall)	27% vs. 21%
Median TT first event	74 days (29, 353): 95% CI 80,105
L684: Median Duration	10 days (1, 63): 95% CI 10, 14
L685: Source: Reviewer analysis
L686: The Applicant's proposed dose modification strategy for hematological toxicity includes dose reductions if cytopenias persist for greater than 2 weeks. The Applicant proposes a dose reduction strategy that includes reduction to a 4 day regimen/cycle, and if toxicities persist, further reduction to a 3 day regimen/cycle and finally, to a 3 day, every-other-day regimen per cycle. The proposed dose reduction strategy is reasonable.
L687: In Study ASTX727-02, 50% (66 of 133) of the patients had dose reductions (DR), mainly due to adverse reactions, count recovery, and myelosuppression. Like dose delays, the adverse reactions leading to DR were mainly cytopenias, specifically neutropenia. DR occurred as early as the 3rd cycle (median time to first DR was 116 days, range: 59, 467) and a median duration of 138 days (range: 0, 467 days). The breakdown of the DR in Study ASTX727-02 is listed in Table D and Figure D. Majority of patients had first dose reduction to a 4-day regimen per cycle, with half of them continuing with the 4-day regimen per cycle. Thirty six percent of patients on the 4-day regimen/cycle had a second DR to 3-day regimen/cycle, and 14% of patients had second DR to an every alternating day 3-day regimen. The CR rate in the patient subpopulation with DR was similar to that in the overall population (23% versus 21%) in Study ASTX727-02. Considering the 3-day regimen has higher exposure than the every alternating day 3-day regimen, it would be beneficial to dose reduce first from a 4-day to 3-day regimen, and subsequently to an alternating 3-day regimen, if necessary. Although the decitabine exposure with an alternating day 3-day regimen is only about 40% of that with a 5-day regimen, it may be beneficial to continue decitabine treatment with the 3-day alternating DR regimen for patients who experience adverse reactions with the 3-day regimen.

L693: ## 6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L694: ## **Data:**
L695: No adjustment to the dosage of ASTX727 is recommended on the basis of age, sex, or weight.
L696: No adjustment to the dosage of ASTX727 is recommended in patients with mild or moderate renal impairment. A recommended dose has not been established for patients with severe renal impairment or patients on dialysis.
L697: (b) (4)
A recommended dose has not been established for patients with severe hepatic impairment.
L698: No clinical studies were conducted to specifically assess the impact of intrinsic factors on PK since the clinical program of ASTX727 included sufficient patient numbers to assess exposure effect based on most intrinsic factors.
L699: ## *Specific Populations*
L700: Age, sex, weight, renal function, and hepatic function were assessed as potential covariates in the PopPK analysis using data from the Phase 2 part of Study ASTX727-01 and from Study ASTX727-02. These studies did not have sufficient racial diversity to inform potential correlations with regard to race or ethnicity.
L701: ## Age
L702: Age (32 to 90 years; Figure 6) did not have a clinically meaningful effect on the PK of decitabine or cedazuridine after administration of ASTX727. Age was not found to be a significant covariate in the population PK model.
L703: **Figure 6. Phase 2/3 Cedazuridine 24-Hour and Decitabine 5-Day Exposures Against Age**

L706: Source: ASTX727-Report-PopPK, Figure 20.
L707: ## Sex
L708: Sex did not have a clinically meaningful effect on the PK of decitabine or cedazuridine after administration of ASTX727 (Figure 7).

L713: ## **Body Weight**
L714: Body weight (41 to 158 kg) did not have a clinically meaningful effect on the PK of decitabine or cedazuridine after administration of ASTX727 (Figure 8). Although there was a small effect of body weight on decitabine AUC following oral ASTX727, this was not clinically meaningful.

L729: ## *Renal Impairment:*
L730: Mild or moderate renal impairment (CRCL \ge 20 up to 59 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2) Figure 11 and Figure 12 had a relatively modest effect on cedazuridine exposures, but this was not considered to be clinically meaningful and did not result in clinically meaningful effect on decitabine exposure.
L731: The effect of severe renal impairment (CRCL &lt; 20 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2) or dialysis on the PK of decitabine or cedazuridine is unknown.

L740: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L741: Based on the above data age, sex, or mild to moderate hepatic impairment did not have a clinically meaningful effect on the PK of decitabine or cedazuridine after administration of ASTX727. Mild or moderate renal impairment had an effect on cedazuridine exposures, but this was not considered to be clinically meaningful.
L742: The effect of severe hepatic impairment or of severe renal impairment on the PK of decitabine and cedazuridine is unknown.
L743: As a result, dose adjustment is not necessary for intrinsic patient factors, but caution should be exercised with severe hepatic or severe renal impairment.
L744: Dose reduction of ASTX727 in response to drug-related myelosuppression, if severe and/or prolonged, could be achieved by reducing the total 5-day decitabine exposures by reducing the number of doses per cycle.
L745: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L746: **Age and Sex:** FDA agrees with the Applicant's position with regards to the effect of age and sex. Age did not correlate with the ratio of 5-day cumulative decitabine exposure between oral and IV decitabine formulation in Studies ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02 (see Figure E). Decitabine exposure was higher in females compared to males, however, this finding was confounded by body size, and is not clinically meaningful.
L747: **Figure E: Effect of Age (left) and Sex (right) on the Ratio of 5-day Cumulative Decitabine AUC between Oral and IV decitabine formulations in Patients from Studies ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02.**
L748: Dashed red line refers to a ratio of 1. Blue line represents the fit.
L749: Source: Reviewer analysis
L750: **Body Weight and Body Surface Area:** FDA agrees with the Applicant's position with regards to the effect of body weight and body surface area (BSA). Body weight and BSA correlated with the 5-day cumulative decitabine exposure ratio as shown in Figure F. An increase in decitabine exposure with a decrease in BSA was observed with ASTX727 compared with IV decitabine. Patients in the lowest BSA group had 25% higher exposures with ASTX727 compared to the patients in the median BSA group. However, a comparison of adverse reactions in Studies ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02, shown in Figure G, indicated that proportion of patients with neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, and serious adverse reactions in the lowest BSA quartile was comparable to that in patients in the median BSA quartile (1.78 - 1.99 m^2) group.

L756: Source: Reviewer analysis
L757: **Effect of Hepatic Impairment:** FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no dose adjustment for patients with mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt; ULN to 1.5 x ULN or AST &gt; ULN). FDA does not agree that there is sufficient information to address the effect of moderate (total bilirubin &gt;1.5 to 3 x ULN, any AST) and severe (total bilirubin &gt;3 x ULN, any AST) on decitabine and cedazuridine PK, as there were only 3 patients with moderate hepatic impairment and no patients with severe hepatic impairment in Studies ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02 (Figure 10). It should be noted that decitabine is mainly eliminated in the liver by CDA. A PMR to determine the dose recommendation for ASTX727 in moderate and severe hepatic impairment will be required.
L758: **Effect of Renal Impairment:** FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no dose modification is necessary for patients with mild (creatinine clearance (CLcr) 60 to &lt;90 mL/min) and moderate (CLcr) 30 to &lt;60 mL/min) impairment. As shown in Figure H, increased cedazuridine levels can increase decitabine exposure. Cedazuridine exposure is also inversely correlated with creatinine clearance, with increase in exposure with decreasing renal clearance (Figure H). However, the point estimates for cumulative decitabine exposure between oral and IV decitabine formulations were similar for patients with mild and moderate renal impairment (see Table E below). The mean 5-day decitabine exposures for ASTX727 was less than for IV decitabine for patients with normal renal function, however, the decrease was &lt;20%.
L759: **Figure H: Effect of Creatinine Clearance on the Ratio of 5-day Cumulative Decitabine AUC between Oral and IV decitabine formulations (left) and Cedazuridine AUC (right) in Patients from Studies ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02**

L770: ## 6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L771: ## Data:
L772: ## *Effect of Food*
L773: The food effect study, ASTX727-04, found that there was little effect on the PK of cedazuridine when ASTX727 was administered with a high-fat meal (approximately 800-1000 calories, with approximately 150 calories from protein, approximately 250 calories from carbohydrates, and approximately 500-600 calories from fat). Cedazuridine and cedazuridine-epimer exposure parameters were approximately 10% and 20% higher, respectively, with food.
L774: The same food effect study found that there was an effect on the PK of decitabine when ASTX727 was administered with a high-fat meal (Table 16). The extent and rate of decitabine exposures were both reduced. Exposure was reduced by approximately 40% (AUC) to 54% (C_{max}) and the t_{max} was delayed from 1.00 hour to 1.75 hours when administration was with food.
L775: **Table 16. Comparison of Decitabine Exposure Between Fed and Fasted States (Study ASTX727-04)**
L776: PK Parameter	Fed	Fasted	Percentage Ratio of Geo. LSM (% Fed/Fasted)	90% CI (%)
N	Geo. LSM	N	Geo. LSM
AUC0-8 (ng•h/mL)	16	114.97	16	191.75	60.0	(50.28, 71.50)
AUC0-24 (ng•h/mL)	14	135.54	15	205.85	65.8	(54.92, 78.94)
AUC0-inf (ng•h/mL)	13	132.38	15	202.72	65.3	(52.94, 80.55)

## NDA 212576, Original
## INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
L778: AUC0-8=area under the plasma concentration-time curve from time zero to 8 hours; AUC0-24=area under the plasma concentration-time curve from time zero to 24 hours; AUC0-inf=area under the plasma concentration-time curve from time zero extrapolated to infinity; AUC0-t=area under the plasma concentration-time curve from time zero to the last measurable concentration; CI=confidence interval; Cmax=maximum plasma concentration; Geo. LSM=geometric least squares mean; N=number of subjects; PK=pharmacokinetic.
L779: Source: CSR ASTX727-04 PK Report, Table 6.5-5.
L780: ## *Effect of ASTX727 on Other Drugs*
L781: No specific study of the effect of cedazuridine on the PK of other drugs has been conducted.
L782: Cedazuridine is a potent inhibitor of CDA (IC50=281 nmol/L), so it would affect the metabolism and exposure of any coadministered drug that is metabolized via this route.
L783: In vitro, cedazuridine (up to 400 µmol/L) did not induce CYP1A, CYP2B6, CYP2C9, and CYP3A. The fold induction (relative to vehicle) for the CYPs evaluated ranged from 0.8 to 1.5 over the cedazuridine concentration range tested. A
L784: In vitro, cedazuridine did not inhibit any of the CYP isoforms tested 1A, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6, 2E1, and 3A; in all cases, the IC50 was &gt;400 µmol/L.
L785: Cedazuridine was not an inhibitor of most of the transporters tested: BCRP, BSEP, P-gp, CNT2, ENT1, ENT2, MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OCT2 at concentrations up to 300 µmol/L. A weak inhibition of CNT2 and CNT3 by cedazuridine was observed at 300 µmol/L, but this is not considered clinically meaningful (cedazuridine clinical plasma Cmax is approximately 2 µmol/L).
L786: Drug interaction studies with decitabine have not been conducted. Decitabine is unlikely to cause DDI via CYP enzymes.
L787: ## *Effect of Other Drugs on ASTX727*
L788: No specific study of the effect of other drugs on the PK of cedazuridine has been conducted.
L789: In vitro, cedazuridine was not metabolized by human liver S9 fractions or liver cytosols and was not a substrate for the majority of transporters. Although cedazuridine was transported by CNT1, CNT3, and ENT2 (3.81-, 4.58-, 2.63-fold, respectively in MDCKII or HEK293 cells expressing the transporters), the potential for drug-drug interactions with comedication is low.
L790: Cedazuridine conversion to its major metabolite (cedazuridine-epimer) occurs prior to its absorption and it was postulated that it may be affected by gastric pH. A PopPK assessment of concomitant proton pump inhibitor medication as a potential covariate found that such comedication did not have a significant impact on decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine-epimer parameters and subsequent exposures, suggesting that drugs modifying gastric pH are unlikely to affect the PK of cedazuridine or decitabine after ASTX727 administration (Figure 13).
L791: Drug interaction studies with decitabine have not been conducted. Decitabine is not a substrate for CYP enzymes, and the plasma protein binding of decitabine is negligible.
L792: **Figure 13. Phase 2/3 Decitabine 5-Day Exposures Against Proton Pump Inhibitor Concomitant Medication**
L793: AUC=area under the plasma concentration-time curve; PPI=proton pump inhibitor.

L797: Yes. Administration of ASTX727 with food results in lower exposure to decitabine.
L798: The Applicant recommends that ASTX727 is administered on an empty stomach and patients should avoid eating

L801: There was no effect on decitabine exposure when ASTX727 was co-administered with PPI drugs. No drug-drug interactions are anticipated with comedication that are substrates or inhibitor of major CYP enzymes or major drug transporters. Drugs that are substrate for CDA should be avoided during dosing with ASTX727.
L802: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L803: **Effect of Food:** FDA does not agree with Applicant's position on the effect of food on the PK of ASTX727 due to inconsistency in the results in the 2 sequence, cross-over study ASTX727-04 in patients. The study design for ASTX727-04 is shown in Figure 1.
L804: **Figure 1: Study Schema for ASTX727-04**
L805: The mean steady state AUC24hr in patients administered ASTX727 initially in the fasted state on Day 2 (Sequence 2) was 2-fold higher than that observed in patients who received ASTX727 in the fasted state on Day 4 (Sequence 1) or in comparison with the mean Day 2 or Day 5 decitabine AUC24hr observed in ASTX727-02 (Table G). In contrast, the decitabine AUC24hr in the fed state was similar in both sequences. This increase in exposure in the fasted state in Sequence 2 confounds the food effect analysis and does not allow for an adequate assessment of the effect of food on decitabine and cedazuridine exposure. Similar results were obtained with AUC8hr.

L814: **Effect of Concomitant drug use:** FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no dose recommendation for ASTX727 in patients with concomitant drugs and to avoid concomitant drugs that are metabolized by CDA. In vitro studies suggest that cedazuridine is not a substrate or modulators of CYP enzymes or major transporters.
L815: FDA noted that there was an increase in dissolution of ASTX727 with increase in pH. The protocol specifies to avoid gastric acid reducing agents within 4 hours of ASTX727 administration, however, per the Applicant, no specific written instructions were provided to the patients regarding administration of acid reducing agents. Nonetheless, majority of patients on proton pump inhibitors (PPI) were on chronic PPI use in Study ASTX727-02.
L816: Given that cedazuridine conversion to its epimer is believed to occur prior to absorption in the stomach and roughly 30% of patients in the Phase 2 and 3 studies were concomitantly administered PPI, an independent analysis of patients in Study ASTX727-02 who were and were not concomitantly administered PPI that overlapped with administration of ASTX727 was conducted. The analysis (Table I) revealed that the cumulative decitabine exposures were not different between oral and IV decitabine formulations in patients coadministered with PPI and patients not taking PPI: the point estimates were close to 1.

L821: ## 6.3.2.5 Does this drug(s) prolong the QT or QTc interval?
L822: ## Data:
L823: An exposure-response analysis of decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine-epimer concentrations with the change from baseline in heart rate corrected QT interval (QTc) from Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02 was conducted.
L824: The ECGs were conducted at screening and predose on Day 3 (Cycles 1 and 2) and 3 hours postdose for ASTX727 treatment (time-matched with PK at tmax of cedazuridine) and within 10 minutes of the end of infusion for IV decitabine treatment (time-matched with PK at tmax of decitabine) on Day 3.
L825: ECG assessments used for determination of QTc interval were time-matched with the PK blood draws.
L826: For this analysis, a modified analysis was required because, for each subject, only 2 postdose ECG-plasma concentration were obtained (1 in each period). Therefore, due to the limited number of ECG and plasma concentration time point matches, a formal complete exposure-response analysis was not possible. For this analysis, a general linear modeling approach was used. Results of the linear regression model are presented in Figure 14 (cedazuridine concentration), Figure 15 (cedazuridine-epimer concentration), Figure 16 (decitabine concentration after oral ASTX727), and Figure 17 (decitabine concentration after IV decitabine).
L827: **Figure 14. Phase 3: QTcF Change from Baseline- QTcF vs Cedazuridine Plasma Concentration (ng/mL) - Estimates from Simple Linear Regression- ASTX727 Cycle**
L828: Source: ASTX727-02 CSR Appendix 16.1.13, Figure 2-1
L829: Figure 15. Phase 3: QTcF Change from Baseline- QTcF vs Cedazuridine-Epimer Plasma Concentration (ng/mL) - Estimates from Simple Linear Regression- ASTX727 Cycle
L830: Source: ASTX727-02 CSR Appendix 16.1.13, Figure 2-2
L831: **Figure 16. Phase 3: QTcF Change from Baseline- QTcF vs Decitabine Plasma Concentration (ng/mL) - Estimates from Simple Linear Regression- ASTX727 Cycle**
L832: Source: ASTX727-02 CSR Appendix 16.1.13, Figure 2-3
L833: Figure 17. QTcF Change from Baseline- QTcF vs Decitabine Plasma Concentration (ng/mL) - Estimates from Simple Linear Regression– IV Decitabine Cycle
L834: Source: ASTX727-02 CSR Appendix 16.1.13, Figure 2-4
L835: Within the limitation of the study, at the therapeutic exposures with the recommended dosing regimen, no large mean changes in the QTc interval (i.e., &gt;10 msec) were observed. The exposure-QTc analysis suggested no concentration-dependent QTc interval prolongation for either decitabine or cedazuridine.
L836: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L837: An assessment of ASTX727 on prolongation of the QT interval has been conducted in Phase 3.
L838: (b) (4)
L839: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L840: (b) (4)
L841: FDA does not agree with Applicant's position
L842: The FDA notes that the ECG collection times from Study ASTX727-02 do not allow for adequate characterization the QTc interval. Additionally, the PK/ECG data from ASTX727 do not support the proposed concentration-QTc analysis because the Applicant did not collect an appropriate pre-treatment/baseline ECG measurement, the exposure range was too narrow to support the assessment, and the sparse sampling schedule
L843: does not allow an evaluation of any delayed effects (e.g., PK/PD hysteresis). The QT-IRT concluded that the PK and ECG data from ASTX727-02 are not adequate to characterize the effect of cedazuridine from ASTX727 on the QTc interval (QT-IRT Review, DARRTS ID 4572035). Therefore, a PMR study will be requested to evaluate the QT prolongation potential of cedazuridine.

L867: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L868: Astex believes the studies included in the submission provide adequate data to support the clinical pharmacology, AUC equivalence, efficacy, and safety of ASTX727 in patients with MDS or CMML. Since the application is primarily based on a PK bridging approach the long-term effectiveness and safety of ASTX727 are provided from single-arm treatment with ASTX727 after the first 2 randomized cycles. In addition to the systemic decitabine AUC exposure equivalence and similarity of the biological DNA demethylation effect between ASTX727 and IV decitabine in the first 2 randomized cycles, we have also used prior decitabine approval (Dacogen USPI 2019) and the published single-arm study of IV decitabine (5-day regimen) from Steensma et al 2009 as a reference of the expected effectiveness and safety of IV decitabine compared to data from ASTX727 treatment in the clinical program.
L869: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L870: We agree with the summary of Studies presented in Table 17 above. It is notable that Study ASTX727-02 enrolled patients with all FAB classes as well, which included patients with low-risk IPSS score.
L871: On Study ASTX727-01-A, the Phase 1, single arm, dose escalation trial, prior azacitidine was allowed with no limitation on the number of prior cycles. On the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies (ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02) patients could have only received 1 prior cycle of azacitidine or decitabine.
L872: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L873: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L874: ## **Applicant's Position:**
L875: Extensive nonclinical and clinical information as well as postmarketing information are available for IV decitabine; therefore, the ASTX727 development program was designed to generate comprehensive pharmacokinetic (PK) data to bridge ASTX727 to safety and efficacy evidence for decitabine. This PK bridging approach is in accordance with FDA guidance for industry, "Providing Clinical Evidence of Effectiveness for Human Drugs and Biological Products," dated May 1998. The design of the clinical protocol for Phase 3 study ASTX727-02 was developed based on the end of Phase 2 agreement between Astex and the FDA.
L876: In addition to the PK bridging to efficacy and safety, the randomized crossover design of the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies in the first 2 treatment cycles allowed intra-patient comparison of ASTX727 (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine) and IV decitabine (20 mg/m2), both given once daily for 5 consecutive days (Daily×5), to also assess the PD effect on global DNA methylation (LINE-1 DNA) and safety, over the first 2 cycles. As designed, these studies included continuation of treatment with ASTX727 from Cycle 3 onwards, as long as the subject continued to benefit, in order to generate single-arm, long-term safety and efficacy data for ASTX727 in the treated disease population. The Phase 1 part of study ASTX727-01 (ASTX727-01-A CSR) was also conducted in MDS and CMML patients and had the longest follow up (minimum 18 months). All subjects were treated with ASTX727 in all cycles (except Cycle 1 Day 1 when IV decitabine at 20 mg/m2 was administered) but with different oral doses of cedazuridine (40, 60, and 100 mg) and decitabine (20, 30, and 40 mg). The main other difference between Phase 1 and the Phase 2 and 3 studies is that prior azacitidine was allowed in Phase 1 without limitation of number of prior azacitidine cycles. In Phase 2 and 3, prior decitabine or azacitidine treatment were limited to only one prior cycle.
L877: Eligible subjects in all 3 studies were males or females ≥18 years of age with de novo or previously treated (any number of cycles of prior azacitidine in Phase 1 but only up to 1 cycle of azacitidine or decitabine in Phase 2 and 3) Int-1, Int-2, or high-risk MDS or CMML. Subjects with Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) performance status (PS) 0-2 were allowed to participate in Phase 1 and 2 but ECOG PS 2 subjects were excluded from Phase 3 to minimize of number of subjects who may not have been able to receive at least the first 2 cycles of treatment, necessary for analysis of the primary endpoint (AUC exposure). In addition, subjects with French-American-British (FAB) classification of refractory anemia with excess blasts in transformation (bone marrow
L878: blasts 20% to 30%) were allowed in Phase 3, as these are included in the FDA approved Dacogen indication, but not in Phase 1 or 2. The duration of treatment and follow-up in the Phase 1 and 2 studies were also longer (median ~24 months) than in the Phase 3 study (median~5 months). We also note that since the initial studies presented in the Dacogen USPI only used the FAB classification and IPSS score, we did not collect data on the MDS and CMML WHO classification in the studies.
L879: ## 8.1.1. **Phase 1 and 2 Studies (ASTX727-01-A and B CSRs)**
L880: ## Trial Design
L881: ## The Applicant's Description:
L882: Study ASTX727-01 was a Phase 1-2 trial to evaluate ASTX727, for treatment of patients with MDS/CMML. The Phase 1 part of the trial (ASTX727-01-A CSR) was a single-arm, dose-finding, dose-escalation design. The Phase 2 part of the trial (ASTX727-01-B CSR) provided confirmation of the doses selected based on Phase 1 finding.
L883: ## *Phase 1:*
L884: The Phase 1 portion of Study ASTX727-01 (CSR ASTX727-01-A) was a first-in-human, open-label, non-randomized, single-arm, PK-guided, dose-escalation study to assess the safety, tolerability, and PK profile of oral doses of cedazuridine and decitabine administered concomitantly as separate capsules. The purpose of the study was to identify the doses of oral cedazuridine and oral decitabine that achieved decitabine AUC comparable to that of IV decitabine at the approved dose of 20 mg/m2 Daily×5. In this study separate cedazuridine capsules (20-mg capsules) and decitabine capsules (5- and 20-mg capsules) were used. The starting dose was 40 mg cedazuridine and 20 mg decitabine. Doses were escalated using a standard 3+3 design after review of PK and safety data (Figure 18).
L885: The study design included assessments of the PK of oral decitabine (Cycle 1 Day -3 [C1D-3]), IV decitabine (C1D1), oral cedazuridine and decitabine administered concomitantly (C1D2 through C1D5), and oral cedazuridine alone (C2D-3). Oral cedazuridine and decitabine were then administered in 5-day treatment cycles (Days 1 through 5) from Cycle 2 onwards at the same doses used in Cycle 1 (intrapatient dose escalation was allowed in subsequent cycles). Cedazuridine and decitabine were administered under fasted conditions (i.e., no food, alcohol, or non-clear liquids for 2 hours before and 2 hours after dosing).
L886: Clinical response was a secondary endpoint in this phase of the study. Efficacy from this study is supportive since it used different doses of cedazuridine and decitabine but also because almost half the subjects (46%) received prior azacitidine or decitabine with no limits in number of prior azacitidine cycles.
L887: Figure 18. ASTX727-01 Phase 1 Study Schema
L888: DLT=dose-limiting toxicity
L889: Source: Adapted from ASTX727-01-A CSR Figure 1
L890: ## *Phase 2:*
L891: Phase 2 portion of Study ASTX727-01 (CSR ASTX727-01-B) was a Phase 2, open-label, randomized, 2-cycle, 2-sequence crossover study followed by a single-arm continuation of treatment conducted in 2 stages to assess the PK, PD, safety, and efficacy of cedazuridine (100 mg) and decitabine (35 mg) as separate capsules (in the Dose Confirmation Stage) and as the ASTX727 FDC tablet (in the FDC Stage) administered Daily×5. At least 42 subjects were planned to be randomized in the Dose Confirmation Stage, and 18 to 24 subjects were planned to be randomized in the FDC Stage. Treatment continued until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, the subject withdrew consent, or the subject was withdrawn from the study. The study schema is shown in Figure 19.
L892: A standard 2×2 crossover design during the first 2 cycles of treatment was used for within-subject comparison of decitabine AUCs and LINE-1 demethylation after treatment with ASTX727 and IV decitabine. Masking the treatment assignment was not feasible due to the different routes of administration (oral vs IV).
L893: Figure 19. Phase 2 ASTX727-01 Study Schema
L894: Source: Adapted from ASTX727-01-B CSR Figure 1
L895: ## Trial Location
L896: Study ASTX727-01 was conducted at 15 centers in North America (11 centers in the United States and 4 centers in Canada).
L897: ## Choice of Control Group (Phase 2)
L898: The control group in the randomized crossover design was IV decitabine. IV decitabine (20 mg/m2 once daily for 5 days) is an approved therapy for MDS/CMML (Dacogen USPI 2019) and was therefore an appropriate active comparator.
L899: ## Diagnostic Criteria (Phase 2)
L900: The Phase 2 portion of Study ASTX727-01 enrolled adult patients with MDS with
L901: peripheral blasts or bone marrow blasts &lt;20% or CMML who had received no more than 1 prior cycle of an HMA. Diagnosis was based on local centers pathology assessment of peripheral blood and bone marrow including assessment of the IPSS category (int-1, int-2, or high-risk) to match the FDA-approved indication for IV decitabine (Dacogen USPI 2019). Data on WHO classification were not collected as part of the study.
L902: ## Key inclusion criteria:
L903: - Men or women \ge 18 years with IPSS intermediate-1, intermediate-2, or high-risk MDS with peripheral blasts or bone marrow blasts &lt;20% in all study stages, including subjects with CMML who are candidates for treatment with an HMA.
- Subjects with Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) performance status of 0 to 2.
- Subjects with adequate organ function defined as:Hepatic: Total or direct bilirubin \le 2 \times upper limit of normal (ULN); AST/SGOT and ALT/SGPT \le 2.5 \times ULN.
- Renal: serum creatinine \le 1.5 \times ULN or creatinine clearance &gt; 50 mL/min/1.73 m^2 for subjects with creatinine levels above institutional normal.
- For subjects with prior allogeneic stem cell transplant, no evidence of graft-versus-host disease and must be \ge 2 weeks off systemic immunosuppressive therapy.
- Able to swallow the number of capsules required for the treatment assignment within a 10-minute period and tolerate 4 hours of fasting.
L904: ## Key exclusion criteria:
L905: - Previous treatment with at least 2 courses of decitabine or azacitidine.Known or suspected hypersensitivity to decitabine or the components of cedazuridine.
- Diagnosis of AML with bone marrow or peripheral blast count \ge 20\% or other hematological malignancies.
- Active uncontrolled gastric or duodenal ulcer.
L906: The diagnosis of MDS and CMML was appropriate matching the IV decitabine FDA approval. The key inclusion and exclusion criteria were appropriate to ensure subjects safety (e.g., organ functions) and allow assessment of efficacy of ASTX727 (exclusion of more than one cycle of prior HMA treatment).
L907: ## Dose Selection (Phase 2)
L908: Dosing in the Phase 2 portion of Study ASTX727-01 was based on the decitabine AUC findings from the Phase 1 Dose Escalation portion of Study ASTX727-01. In the Phase 1 Dose Escalation (results reported in CSR ASTX727-01-A), decitabine exposure increased when decitabine was administered with cedazuridine, compared with decitabine administered alone, and approached or exceeded the decitabine AUC exposure of IV decitabine (128% oral/IV) at the highest oral decitabine dose of 40 mg. Oral/IV was 81% when the decitabine dose was de-escalated to 30 mg. It was therefore concluded from Phase 1 that an intermediate oral decitabine dose between 30 mg and 40 mg would best replicate the IV exposure and this was confirmed by PK modelling. The assessment led to the selection of the doses of the 2 components of ASTX727 to carry into the Phase 2 part of the trial: 100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine.
L909: ## Study Treatments (Phase 2)
L910: ## Dose Confirmation Stage:
L911: ## Test Product (cedazuridine and decitabine capsules)
L912: - Dose: 100 mg cedazuridine (five 20-mg capsules) administered concomitantly with 35 mg decitabine (one 20-mg capsule and three 5-mg capsules) orally once daily on Days 1 through 5 (Daily×5) in 28-day cycles.
L913: ## Reference Product (IV decitabine, 5-day regimen)
L914: - Dose: 20 mg/m2 decitabine by continuous 1-hour IV infusion Daily×5 in 28-day cycles.
L915: ## FDC Stage:
L916: ## Test Product (ASTX727 FDC tablet)
L917: - Dose: one ASTX727 FDC tablet (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine) administered orally Daily×5 in 28-day cycles.
L918: ## Reference Product (IV decitabine, 5-day regimen)
L919: - Dose: 20 mg/m2 decitabine by continuous 1-hour IV infusion Daily×5 in 28-day cycles.
L920: Study drugs (test and reference products) were administered at the study center under the supervision of study personnel during Cycle 1 and Cycle 2 of the Dose Confirmation and FDC Stages. In Cycles ≥3 of both stages, the test products (capsules or tablets) were administered on an outpatient basis. The study drugs (test and reference) were to be taken in the morning at the same time of day (±1 hour) on each dosing day, in at least the first 2 cycles of treatment.
L921: ## Assignment to Treatment (Phase 2)
L922: Individual subject treatment assignments in each study stage were determined through a computer generated randomization scheme and accessed through the electronic data capture system used for study data management. Subjects were randomized in a 1:1 ratio and stratified by IPSS risk level (IPSS Int-1 vs IPSS Int-2 and high-risk [subjects with CMML were randomized into the IPSS Intermediate-2/high risk stratum]) to one of the following treatment sequences:
L923: - Sequence A: cedazuridine + oral decitabine capsules (Dose Confirmation Stage) or the ASTX727 FDC tablet (FDC Stage) in Cycle 1 and then IV decitabine Cycle 2, or
- Sequence B: IV decitabine Cycle 1 and then cedazuridine + oral decitabine capsules (Dose Confirmation Stage) or the ASTX727 FDC tablet (FDC Stage) in Cycle 2.
L924: All subjects received ASTX727 from Cycle 3 until treatment discontinuation. The randomization code and treatment assignment for each subject are provided in the submission (ASTX727-01-B CSR, Appendix 16.1.7).
L925: ## Blinding (Phase 2)
L926: This was an open-label study.
L927: The primary PK and PD endpoints (total cycle AUC, LINE-1 methylation), were not susceptible to bias due to the objective nature of the measurements of PK and the LINE-1 assay. Efficacy response rate assessment is subject to bias; however, response assessments were done in Phase 2 by Astex medical personnel following the International Working Group (IWG) 2006 criteria by medical review of objective hematology data provided by the center (peripheral blood, bone marrow, and transfusion data). Efficacy survival data are not subject to bias.
L928: ## Dose Modification/Dose Discontinuation (Phase 2)
L929: Dose modification was not allowed during the first 2 cycles in order not to bias the PK and PD assessments. Subjects should receive the full intended dose of cedazuridine (100 mg) and decitabine (35 mg) as either separate capsules (Dose Confirmation Stage) or as the ASTX727 FDC tablet (FDC Stage) and IV decitabine dose (20 mg/m2) in the first 2 cycles. Cycle 2 can be delayed until blood count recovery from drug related hematological toxicity, to allow full dose in Cycle 2.
L930: Guidelines for dose delay or dose reduction in response to toxicity are detailed in the ASTX727-01 Protocol (Section 7.3). In summary, ASTX727 dose reduction in Phase 2 was recommended by reducing the number of dosing days from 5 to 4 then 3. There was no
L931: guidelines for dose modification of IV decitabine as it was administered for only one cycle.
L932: ## Administrative Structure (Phase 2)
L933: The Applicant was responsible for all aspects of the study, including oversight of the contract research organization responsible for study management and monitoring. An additional organization coordinated monitoring activities and support for Canadian study centers.
L934: A Data and Safety Review Committee (DSRC) met periodically, mainly during the Phase 1 part of the study, to discuss and agree on the dose escalation for each subsequent cohort after assessment of safety, PK, and PD data from the previous cohort.
L935: Response was assessed independently by 2 Astex medical monitors as described in Appendix 1 of the SAP for ASTX727-01-B using local center peripheral blood, bone marrow, and transfusion data and applying the IWG 2006 response criteria.
L936: ## Procedures and Schedule (Phase 2)
L937: The schedule of events is provided below in Table 18.
