L945: ## Dietary Restrictions/Instructions (Phase 2)
L946: Subjects were permitted to have only clear liquids for 2 hours before and 2 hours after administration of the oral test product (cedazuridine and decitabine capsules or the ASTX727 FDC tablet). No dietary restrictions were in place when subjects received IV decitabine.
L947: ## Concurrent Medications (Phase 2)
L948: Supportive treatment such as antiemetics, blood and platelet transfusions, antibiotics including antifungal treatment were permitted at the investigator discretion according to the institution's standard of practice.
L949: Hematopoietic growth factors were permitted in the event of a life-threatening septic event, at the discretion of the treating physician. Erythropoietin and/or Neupogen could be used after Cycle 1 and 2 in Phase 2.
L950: Prohibited concomitant therapies were radiation therapy, chemotherapy, immunotherapy, or any experimental therapy. Other nucleosides or drugs that are metabolized by cytidine deaminase were also prohibited when cedazuridine was being administered.
L951: Medications that may affect gastric pH were allowed, but were recommended not to be taken within \pm 4 hours of administration of oral ASTX727.
L952: ## Treatment Compliance (Phase 2)
L953: During the first 2 cycles, all treatments (oral and IV) were administered by study personnel at each study center. In Cycles \ge 3, test products (capsules or tablets) were self administered on an outpatient basis. Drug accountability was determined by counting the number of capsules or tablets dispensed and returned by the subject to the study center.
L954: ## Rescue Medications (Phase 2)
L955: No rescue medications per se were planned in the trial apart from the permitted concomitant medications and supportive treatments described above.
L956: ## Subject Completion, Discontinuation, or Withdrawal (Phase 2)
L957: Subjects may discontinue study treatment for reasons such as disease progression or unacceptable toxicity, but they should continue on study follow up to assess AML transformation and survival. Subjects were considered to have completed the study
L958: when they were withdrawn from the study for any of the following reasons:
L959: - Death.
- Withdrawal of consent.
- Lost to follow up.
L960: Subjects lost to follow up were included in the statistical analysis to the point of last evaluation. For analyses of survival time and time to progression to AML, subjects were censored on the last date of contact if lost to follow up prior to reaching a time-to-event endpoint.
L961: Enrollment continued until the required number of evaluable subjects was reached.
L962: For subjects who discontinued treatment, follow-up procedures and assessments were conducted at the 30-day follow-up visit and thereafter at approximately 3-month intervals to assess conversion to AML and to assess overall survival. See Table 18 for more details.
L963: ## **Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L964: We agree with the Applicant's description of the trial design of the Phase 1 and 2 Studies (ASTX727-01-A and -B).
L965: ## Study Endpoints (Phase 2)
L966: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L967: The objective of the study was to establish and then confirm the doses of the two components to be used in the final product (ASTX727 FDC tablet). To that effect Phase 2 described multiple primary endpoints (decitabine AUC for PK; and LINE-1 for treatment PD effect on DNA methylation both using randomized comparison of IV and oral in the first 2 cycles of treatment) and response rate from study treatment assessed as a single arm. The multiple primary endpoints were needed to ensure that the selected oral ASTX727 doses achieved similar decitabine exposure, similar DNA demethylation compared to IV decitabine, and that the response rate following multiple cycles of oral ASTX727 was consistent with expected response rate from IV decitabine. This assurance was deemed necessary before finalizing the dose for the Phase 3 study, which was discussed with FDA at the EOP2 meeting.
L968: ## **Primary Endpoints (Phase 2)**
L969: - Mean decitabine AUC (estimated from 5 days of dosing) and mean maximum %LINE-1 demethylation after oral decitabine + cedazuridine compared with IV decitabine 20 mg/m2.
L970: - Response rate in all subjects (based on IWG 2006 MDS response criteria).
L971: ## Secondary Endpoints (Phase 2)
L972: - Incidence and severity grades of adverse events and clinically significant abnormal laboratory values.
- Duration of response, hematological improvement, rate of transfusion independence, time to AML, and overall survival.
- Other PK parameters of oral decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine-epimer (if needed).
L973: ## Exploratory Endpoint (Phase 2)
L974: - Gene mutations and correlation with response.
L975: The endpoints in Phase 2 were standard endpoints and appropriate based on the main objective of confirming the final doses of ASTX727 to proceed to Phase 3. While PK, PD, and response rates were all primary in the Phase 2 part of the ASTX727-01 study, they have different emphasis for this NDA. The efficacy data in Phase 2 (80 patients randomized and received any amount of treatment) are considered the primary efficacy results in this application due to the long follow-up necessary to achieve mature efficacy assessment, which was achieved in this study (minimum follow-up of 12 months; median follow up of 24 months, and median number of 7 cycles with a range of 1 to 29 cycles). In contrast, the PK and PD endpoints in Phase 2 are considered supportive in the application since the sample size in Phase 2 was not designed with sufficient power to achieve AUC equivalence definition (oral/IV ratio with 90% CI between 80% and 125%). Phase 1 efficacy data are supportive since it is also a mature study with minimum follow-up of 18 months (median follow-up ~ 24 months, median cycle 5 (range 1-35). Response rate in both Phase 1 and 2 was based on the most recent and standard criteria for MDS response assessment (IWG 2006 criteria). Local center objective laboratory and clinical data from peripheral blood (PB), bone marrow (BM), and transfusions at baseline and subsequently were carefully reviewed by Astex medical personnel to assign response category and did not just rely on investigator judgment.
L976: ## Efficacy Assessments (Phase 2)
L977: Hematology with peripheral blood counts was assessed at baseline and on Day 1, 8, 15 and 22 during Cycles 1 and 2 and at least on Day 1 in Cycle \ge 3. Bone marrow was assessed at baseline and within 7 days before the start of Cycles 3, 5, and 7; then according to institution practice as long as the subject was responding.
L978: Evaluation of response was based on IWG 2006 MDS Response Criteria (Cheson et al 2006) and was conducted by 2 Astex medical monitors (independently of each other) by review of peripheral blood and bone marrow data from each cycle and assisted by programmed data listings as described in Appendix 1 of the SAP for ASTX727-01-B.
L979: The frequency of assessment (multiple PK samples during treatment days); LINE-1 assessment at baseline and weekly in cycles 1 and 2; the frequency of PB, and bone marrow assessments at baseline and subsequently were sufficient to capture response category, duration of response, and time to AML transformation. Subjects were followed monthly (every 4 weeks) during treatment and every 3 months after treatment discontinuation to adequately capture survival data. Transfusion requirements were assessed weekly in the first 2 cycles then on Day 1 of each subsequent cycle.
L980: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L981: We agree with the Applicant's description of the study endpoints of the Phase 2 Study (ASTX727-01-B).
L982: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments (Phase 2)
L983: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L984: The planned analyses for this Phase 2 study are described in two different documents, the Pharmacometric Analysis Plan (27 August 2018) and the Statistical Analysis Plan (08 August 2018). The Pharmacometric Analysis Plan (PAP) addressed the PK analyses of plasma decitabine, cedazuridine and cedazuridine-epimer following single and multiple once daily oral doses of decitabine and cedazuridine for dose-confirmation and fixed-dose combination stages. The planned analyses of AUC exposures of decitabine after treatment with ASTX727 (administered as individual capsules of cedazuridine and decitabine in the dose-confirmation stage and as a fixed dose tablet in the FDC stage) versus IV decitabine, as well as planned analyses of secondary PK parameters, were described in the PAP. The Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) described the planned analyses of all other clinical and PD endpoints.
L985: ## **Sample Size**
L986: In the Dose Confirmation Stage of the study, an intra-subject CV of 0.50 (estimated from a previous study described in the Dacogen Prescribing Information) was chosen to calculate the sample size to include in the 2 one-sided equivalence tests of the mean decitabine AUCs of ASTX727 (estimated from 5-days of dosing) compared with IV decitabine. A sample size of 42 subjects provides 86% power at the statistical significance level of 0.10, when the true ratio of geometric means is 1.0 and the equivalence limits for the mean ratio are 0.75 and 1.33. This same sample size of 42 in the same 2x2 cross-over design achieves 80% power to detect the non-inferiority, with
L987: respect to the mean maximum %LINE-1 demethylation from baseline, of ASTX727 compared with IV decitabine using a one-sided t-test when the margin of non-inferiority is -0.05, the true mean difference is 0, the standard deviation (SD) is 0.15 and the significance level is 0.10. The SD of the paired differences of 0.15 was estimated from a previous Astex study.
L988: For the FDC Stage, a total of 18-24 evaluable subjects included in the 2 one-sided equivalence tests for mean oral decitabine AUC compared with mean IV decitabine AUC provided 75%-88% power at a 10% significance level when the true ratio of means is 1.0, the coefficient of variation under an unlogged scale is 0.55, and the equivalence limits for the ratio of means are 0.65 and 1.539.
L989: The 18-24 subjects who completed Cycles 1 and 2 with sufficient LINE-1 data in the same 2x2 crossover design provided 78%-86% power to detect non-inferiority, with respect to the mean maximum %LINE-1 demethylation from baseline, of the ASTX727 FDC tablet compared with IV decitabine using a one-sided t-test when the margin of non-inferiority is -0.075, the true mean difference is 0, the SD is 0.15 and the significance level is 0.10.
L990: ## Analysis Populations
L991: Two Pharmacokinetic Analysis Populations were used for analysis of PK data and were defined as follows:
L992: The **Primary PK Analysis Set** was used for analyses of the primary study endpoint. This analysis set included all randomized subjects who receive 2 cycles of treatment and who had sufficient plasma concentration data to allow AUC_{0-t} determination for each cycle.
L993: The **Complete PK Analysis Set** included subjects who received at least one dose of study drug (cedazuridine + oral decitabine, or ASTX727, or IV decitabine) with sufficient plasma sample data to assess PK parameters (including unpaired AUC comparison) and with no protocol deviations affecting assessment of the primary study endpoint.
L994: The following Analysis Sets were used for analysis of all non-PK primary and secondary endpoints:
L995: The **All Subject Analysis Set** includes data from all randomized subjects including those who did not receive any study treatment. This dataset was used to summarize disposition.
L996: The **Efficacy Analysis Set** includes data from all randomized subjects who received any amount of study treatment.
L997: The **Safety Analysis Set** includes data from all randomized subjects who received any amount of study treatment. In the safety analysis, no data exclusion was allowed because of protocol deviations.
L998: The **PD LINE-1 Analysis Set** includes data from subjects if they received at least one cycle (Days 1 through 5) of study treatment and had LINE-1 methylation data at baseline (Day 1) and on either Day 8 or Day 15. Based on prior experience with decitabine treatment, maximum effect on LINE-1 % methylation is mostly achieved on Day 8 or Day 15 following in the 5-day dosing schedule.
L999: ## Methods for Handling Missing Data (Phase 2)
L1000: ## *Handling of Missing Plasma Concentrations*
L1001: For the PK parameter calculations, imputation of missing data may be done for:
L1002: - In instances where there is a missing plasma concentration at the end time of the predefined partial AUC (i.e., AUC_{0-24}, AUC_{0-8}), the following data extrapolation / interpolation was applied:If the predefined end time falls within the range of the available data but does not coincide with an observed data point, then the plasma concentration corresponding to the end time of the predefined partial AUC will be calculated (imputed) by performing a linear or logarithmic interpolation, as applicable.
- If the sample was collected before the end time of the predefined partial AUC (e.g., 23.5 h for AUC_{0-24}) and corresponds to the last sample collected for the predefined partial AUC, then the plasma concentration corresponding to the end time of the predefined partial AUC will be calculated by extrapolation using \lambda_z (i.e., concentration at 24 h for AUC_{0-24}).
- If \lambda_z is not estimable, the observed partial AUC up to the time of the last sample preceding the end time of the pre-defined partial AUC will be used only if the actual end time of the predefined partial AUC is within 20% time window of the nominal end time (i.e. &gt;6.4h for AUC_{0-8} and &gt;19.2h for AUC_{0-24}).
- Otherwise, the predefined partial AUC will not be calculated.
- Missing pre-dose values:The pre-dose concentration will be set to 0 for the first dose.
- After multiples doses, the pre-dose concentration will be set to C_{min} following the previous dose for cedazuridine and zero for decitabine.
L1003: Unless otherwise noted, data summaries will be performed only on observed data.
L1004: ## *Incomplete Dosing Information*
L1005: In the case where subjects were dosed with any of the study drugs but discontinued from the study (early termination), the concentration data will be reported in the concentration tables and included in all summary statistics and PK analysis if sufficient concentration data are available to permit PK analysis for that treatment in that subject.
L1006: ## *Missing Sampling Dates and/or Times*
L1007: For concentration-time profiles from subjects with missing sample dates and/or times, the nominal sampling day and/or time (as appropriate) will be assumed for the calculation of PK parameters.
L1008: ## *Unexpected Data*
L1009: A visual inspection of concentration-time profiles will be performed to determine dataset integrity and potential outliers. Descriptive statistics of PK parameters may be calculated with and without subjects who could be potential outliers. Subjects with potential outlier data will be reviewed by [REDACTED](b) (4) and the Sponsor for inclusion or exclusion from the descriptive statistics on a case-by-case basis.
L1010: Non-zero predose concentration values will be identified in the study report and included in the computation of PK parameters. Concentration profiles and PK parameters for subjects with non-zero predose concentrations greater than 5% of Cmax will be reviewed by [REDACTED](b) (4) and the Sponsor for inclusion or exclusion from the descriptive statistics on a case-by-case basis.
L1011: Any plasma concentrations that cannot be uniquely and unequivocally attributed to a particular subject, period and/or time point based on case report form (CRF) records or other observed data records in the study will be treated as incomplete data. Such data will be compiled, commented, and listed separately in the appendix of the report. Incomplete and/or non-compliant data, if any, will be excluded from analysis datasets; no analyses, summaries or graphs will be produced using these data.
L1012: ## *Vomiting*
L1013: For subjects receiving, oral decitabine or combination treatment of decitabine and cedazuridine who may experience emesis on the day or during the serial sampling, if vomiting occurs at or before 2 times median tmax of cedazuridine or decitabine (whichever median tmax occurs later), then subject data for decitabine, cedazuridine and its circulating metabolites (E7727-epimer) will be excluded from PK statistical analysis for the treatment in which this occurs.
L1014: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1015: We agree with the Applicant's description of the SAP of the Phase 2 Study (ASTX727-01-B).
L1016: ## **Protocol Amendments (Phase 2)**
L1017: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1018: Three of the four ASTX727-01 protocol amendments affected the Phase 2 portion of the study (Amendments 2 through 4). The key changes are shown in Table 19.
L1019: **Table 19. ASTX727-01 Phase 2 Protocol Amendments**
L1020: Protocol Amendment	Effective Date	Key Changes in Amendment
2	23 Sept 2015	Added PK assessments on Days 2, 3, and 4 during the ASTX727 cycle in the Dose Confirmation Stage to better estimate PK over 5 dosing days.
3	26 July 2016	Added “FDC stage” to confirm ASTX727 FDC tablet formulation yields PK and PD data similar to data for IV decitabine in addition to the “Dose Confirmation” stage of the study protocol, which assessed cedazuridine and decitabine given as separate capsules and to gather additional efficacy and safety data with the FDC tablet.
4	06 Apr 2017	Require transition of long-term ongoing subjects in the Dose Confirmation Stage from cedazuridine and oral decitabine capsules to the ASTX727 FDC tablet (due to expiry of the capsules).

We agree with the Applicant’s description of protocol amendments.

L1029: investigators participating in Study ASTX727-01, as recommended in the guidance for industry Financial Disclosure by Clinical Investigators.

L1031: - Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6) participated as a clinical investigator in Study ASTX727-01 and had a financial arrangement with Astex that was required to be disclosed. Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6) received "significant payments of other sorts" from Astex as he was also contracted to be [REDACTED](b) (6). The financial arrangement with Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6) does not raise any data integrity concerns, as the primary PK Analysis Assessment for Study ASTX727-02 was conducted by a third party and data were not accessible to Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6). In regards to the Response Assessment, Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6) did not conduct response assessment for Phase 2, [REDACTED](b) (6) thus reducing the potential for bias.

- Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6) participated as a clinical investigator in Study ASTX727-01 and had a financial arrangement with Astex that was required to be disclosed. Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6) received "significant payments of other sorts" from Astex [REDACTED](b) (6). As a result, payments were made to [REDACTED](b) (6), which included payments for Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6). The financial arrangement with Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6) does not raise any data integrity concerns, as the PK Analysis Assessment conducted by a third party and data were not accessible to Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6). In regards to the Response Assessments, the response data were assessed by internal Astex medical reviewers, not by Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6).

L1034: disclosed a proprietary interest in the product or a significant equity holding in Astex; and 3) no listed investigator was the recipient of significant payments of other sorts. As listed above, the financial arrangements made with both Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6) and Dr. [REDACTED](b) (6) do not raise any concerns with data integrity.

L1036: ## Patient Disposition (Phase 2)
L1037: Data:
A total of 86 subjects were randomized in the Phase 2 portion of the study from 11 centers in the US and 4 in Canada. The Efficacy Analysis Set and the Safety Analysis Set were composed of the 80 randomized subjects who received any amount of study treatment, no treated subjects were excluded. Six subjects were randomized but did not receive any treatment (1 died before receiving treatment, 1 because of pathologist change of diagnosis from MDS to myelofibrosis, 1 withdrew because of disease progression, 1 withdrew consent before treatment, and 2 withdrew due to elevated liver function tests). It is to be noted that appendix 16.2.3 of the submitted Phase 1 and 2 CSRs (ASTX727-01-A and ASTX727-01-B) was mislabeled "subjects excluded from efficacy analysis" when in fact they are listing of subjects who failed screening and were not enrolled/randomized to the study.
L1038: As of the Phase 2 CSR data cutoff date (05 June 2018), 13 subjects were alive and ongoing treatment. The median duration of follow up was 729 days (~24 months) with a minimum of 12 months (range, 365 to 876 days). The median duration of treatment was 7 treatment cycles (range, 1 to 29 cycles).
L1039: As the FDC stage began after enrollment in the Dose Confirmation Stage had finished, the median follow up time in the Dose Confirmation Stage (809.5 days) was longer than in the FDC Stage (460 days).

L1047: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1048: None of the subjects who received treatment were excluded from planned efficacy or safety analyses. Since efficacy in Phase 2 was assessed as a single arm, it is appropriate to conduct the efficacy assessment based on subjects who actually received treatment.
L1049: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1050: FDA performed an analysis of disposition on the Phase 2 Study ASTX727-01-B using data from the 120-day safety update received on 4/6/2020. The 120-day safety update used a cutoff date of 11/1/2019. For the purposes of determining reasons for treatment discontinuation, FDA considers death under the primary reason for discontinuation (i.e., either primary disease or adverse event). Thus, the reason “adverse event” included 9 deaths due to an adverse event, in addition to the disposition events coded as adverse event. The reason “primary disease” included 2 deaths due to primary disease, in addition to the disposition events coded as progressive disease.
L1051: Of the 17 treatment discontinuations for “Other” in the overall treated population, 5 were “rollover,” 4 appeared to be due to the primary disease, 4 were physician decision, 2 were moved to palliative/hospice care, one was considered not a good candidate for the trial, and one was the subject’s decision.

L1056: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations (Phase 2)
L1057: ## Data:
L1058: Categories of important protocol deviations were identified by the study team during protocol deviation review throughout the study.
L1059: **Important Protocol Deviations:** A subset of protocol deviations that might significantly affect the completeness, accuracy, and/or reliability of the study data or that might significantly affect a subject's rights, safety, or well-being.
L1060: For this study, important protocol deviations were further defined as those that potentially affected evaluation of the study endpoints, particularly the PK primary endpoint in each study stage. Deviations such as out-of-window assessment/procedures were not considered important; in addition, PK sampling out-of-window was not considered significant, as the actual (not the scheduled) PK sampling time was used to compensate and was considered sufficient for assessment of the primary study endpoint.
L1061: Six deviations were considered to be important and are described by deviation category as follows:
L1062: - Safety: 2 occurrences of study center failure to report an SAE to the sponsor with 24 hours and to the local IRB.
- Study procedures: PK sampling was not collected per protocol for 1 subject.
- Informed consent: the screening informed consent process was not in the study source documentation at 1 study center.
- Randomization/enrollment: 1 subject was treated prior to being randomized to a treatment sequence (Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) described below).
L1063: Astex subject matter experts and the medical monitor reviewed the listings for confirmation of the assignment of importance and for deviation trends across study centers.
L1064: No subjects were randomized who did not meet study entry criteria. Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) was not randomized prior to treatment; this subject was subsequently manually assigned a treatment sequence corresponding to the actual treatment received; this was documented in the EDC and submitted to the study center's IRB.
L1065: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1066: These deviations did not affect the study conclusions.
L1067: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1068: We agree with the Applicant's assessment. None of the deviations were likely to bias the study in favor of the study drug. Therefore, all patients, including those with important protocol deviations, were included in our analysis of efficacy endpoints.

L1091: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1092: The baseline disease characteristics of subjects in this study are representative of the patient population intended to receive the marketed product. L1093: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1094: The mean time since diagnosis was 299.3 days and median time since diagnosis was 52.5 days. Of note, this is shorter than the mean and median time since diagnosis noted in the IV decitabine label (mean 86 weeks and median 29 weeks on the decitabine arm of the randomized trial and mean 444 days and median 154 days on the single-arm trial).
L1095: Only 7 (9%) received up to 1 cycle of prior HMA therapy prior to enrolling on the trial.
L1096: FDA obtained further information from the Applicant regarding prior therapies in a response to information request received on 3/10/2020. Results of FDA's subsequent analysis of prior therapies are displayed in the Table below.

L1101: Of note, the USPI for IV decitabine states that 30% of patients on the decitabine arm of the randomized trial and 27% of patients on the single arm trial received previous MDS therapy. Subject (b) (6) received lenalidomide for 1827 days.
L1102: Of note, the 17 (21%) of patients without IPSS results documented had CMML. Cytogenetic risk categories for the 80 patients were better in 27 (34%), intermediate in 22 (28%), poor in 15 (19%), and missing/unknown in 16 (20%).
L1103: FDA found that 40 (50%) of patients had bone marrow blasts &gt; 5% at baseline, which is slightly different from 41 (53%) reported by the Sponsor.
L1104: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use (Phase 2)
L1105: ## **Data:**
L1106: During the 2-way crossover, 73 of the 80 subjects treated (91.3%) received the first 2 cycles of treatment.
L1107: The median number of cycles received overall in Phase 2 was 7 (range, 1 to 29) and 50 subjects (62.5%) received 6 or more cycles. Overall in Phase 2, 51.3% of subjects had delayed cycles and 40% of subjects had dose-reduced cycles (Table 23).

L1114: Therapeutic classes of concomitant medications most commonly used were antibacterials (93.8%), analgesics (76.3%), and antimycotics for systemic use (67.5%). Rescue medications were not used in this study.
L1115: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1116: Treatment compliance was well controlled/managed at the study centers during the first 2 cycles as all treatments (oral and IV) were administered by study center personnel. Compliance with treatment was adequate based on the median number of 7 cycles (range 1, 29), which is consistent with prior IV decitabine 5-day regimen study: median of 5 cycles (range 1, 17) (Steensma et al 2009).
L1117: Dose reduced cycles and treatment cycle delays were permitted for management of cytopenias. Overall, 60% of subjects did not have a dose reduced cycle during the Phase 2 study. For the primary PK endpoint, only subjects who received both treatments (oral and IV) and had adequate PK samples to be able to calculate 5-day AUC for both treatments were included in the primary paired PK analysis. No formal statistical comparisons between treatments (oral and IV) were performed for efficacy endpoints since subjects only received one cycle of IV decitabine by study design.
L1118: Concomitant medications used during the study were mainly used as supportive treatment or treatment for other comorbidities so they did not affect the efficacy results.
L1119: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1120: FDA performed a separate analysis of exposure based on months on therapy (instead of number of treatment cycles) (see Table below). Note that this data was obtained using the original cutoff date of 6/5/2018 included in the original NDA submission. Updated efficacy data was not submitted for the Phase 2 trial with the 120-day safety update.

L1150: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1151: **PK:** The primary PK objectives of this study were achieved and demonstrated that the dose of 100 mg cedazuridine combined with 35 mg decitabine produced decitabine exposures (5-day AUC) that were highly comparable with 20 mg/m2 IV decitabine, regardless of the formulation administered (Table 12 and Table 13). Therefore, the efficacy results presented here include the overall Phase 2 population (N=80). The 80% CI of the oral/IV AUC were within the limits defined in the study analysis plan for each of the Dose Confirmation stage and the FDC stage.
L1152: **PD:** Oral administration of ASTX727 achieved LINE-1 DNA demethylation similar to IV decitabine at 20 mg/m2 (Table 9, Figure 3)
L1153: **Efficacy:** The efficacy endpoint of response rate in this study was not compared between treatment sequences (Sequence A and B). There was no clinical reason to conduct such a comparison since all patients in the trial only received ASTX727 except for one cycle of IV decitabine, and all patients received the same doses of cedazuridine and decitabine in ASTX727.
L1154: The CR and overall response rates of 21% and 60%, respectively, overall in Phase 2 (Table 24), were consistent with historical results observed with IV decitabine using the same IWG 2006 criteria (CR of 17% and overall response of 51% in Steensma et al 2009). Additional analysis of response by age, sex, country, race, disease type (MDS and
L1155: CMMI), and IPSS score showed that ASTX727 was clinically active in all patient subgroups within the limitation of patient numbers in certain subgroups.
L1156: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1157: See Section 6 of the Assessment Aid for FDA's assessment of PK and PD results from the Phase 2 trial.
L1158: The acceptable response rate for MDS appropriate for regulatory decision making is overall response rate (ORR), defined as CR+PR responses. ORR served as the basis for approval of IV decitabine. There were no PR responses on the Phase 2 trial of ASTX727, and therefore, only CR responses will be considered for demonstration of clinical benefit. Lesser responses than CR/PR do not convey clear clinical benefit to patients and will not be included in labeling.
L1159: We disagree with the Applicant's assessment of the CR rate. FDA adjudicated CR responses based on evaluation of bone marrow and laboratory results in the submission. We could not confirm CR responses in the following subjects:
L1160: - Subject USUBJID ASTX727-01(b) (6): This patient was noted to have a best response of CR on (b) (6). However, the date of the bone marrow assessment for response was listed as (b) (6), the date of the screening marrow assessment. It is not appropriate to use the screening bone marrow for an assessment of response. Furthermore, the ANC on (b) (6) was &lt; 1 Gi/L. At the time of the next marrow assessments (on (b) (6) and (b) (6)), the ANC was still &lt; 1. ANC was normal on (b) (6), but there was no bone marrow evaluation at that time to confirm response. Furthermore, the patient had 0.01 peripheral blasts on their next count check on (b) (6). Then, on (b) (6) (the last assessment on trial), peripheral blasts were back down to 0, but hemoglobin, ANC, and platelets were below the CR thresholds again. Given 1) the last bone marrow assessment several months prior and 2) the lack of a "robust" CR response (Cheson requires responses to last at least 4 weeks), we are unable to confirm CR response in this patient and consider them to be a non-responder.
- Subject USUBJID ASTX727-01(b) (6): This patient was noted to have a best response of CR on (b) (6). However, the date of the bone marrow assessment was 3 months earlier on (b) (6), at which time there was erythroid and myeloid dysplasia on the marrow and peripheral count recovery was not achieved. Furthermore, the patient only achieved peripheral count recovery on one date ((b) (6)). All subsequent count checks were below the required thresholds for CR response, up until the next marrow assessment on (b) (6), which showed 9% blasts. The marrow on (b) (6) showed 2% blasts, but with persistent dysplasia
L1161: and lack of peripheral blood count recovery. In addition to the bone marrow assessment months before the count recovery, the peripheral blood response did not last at least 4 weeks (per Cheson guidelines). Therefore, we are unable to confirm CR response in this patient and consider them to be a non-responder.
L1162: - Subject USUBJID ASTX727-01 (b) (6) This patient was noted to have a best response of CR on (b) (6). However, the date of the bone marrow assessment was a year earlier on (b) (6), at which time the patient did not have peripheral count recovery. The patient did not have any subsequent bone marrow assessments on the trial. The patient only had peripheral count recovery on (b) (6), with no further count checks meeting all CR criteria. No CR response was confirmed due to the yearlong interval between the last marrow assessment and peripheral blood CR criteria and lack of maintenance of peripheral count recovery per Cheson guidelines. Therefore, we consider this patient to be a non-responder.
L1163: Taking out the 3 patients above, we calculate the ORR (CR+PR) rate = CR rate to be 14/80 (18%; 95% CI 10-28%) for the overall population. This remains similar to the ORR in the USPI for IV decitabine, which was 17% for the randomized trial and 16% for the single arm trial (CR rates of 9% and 15%, respectively).
L1164: The Applicant confirmed FDA's analysis of the CR results and provided updated efficacy data including the FDA-adjudicated CR responses with the 120-day safety update.
L1165: Of note, the protocol used IWG 2006 MDS Response Criteria, which differs from that used in the IV decitabine prescribing information (IWG 2000 MDS Response Criteria) (Cheson, Bennett et al. 2000). The major differences are that the IWG 2000 criteria required an ANC of 1.5 Gi/L or more with no dysplasia for CR responses, whereas the 2006 criteria only required an ANC of 1 Gi/L or more with allowance of persistent dysplasia. Therefore, the CR responses reported in the decitabine USPI of 9-15% would likely be higher using the updated response criteria.
L1166: The following subgroup analyses were performed in patients with FDA-adjudicated CR
L1167: ## responses:
L1168: - CR rate &lt; 65 years 3/17 (18%) and &gt;= 65 years 11/63 (17%)
- CR rate females 2/19 (11%) and males 12/61 (20%)
- CR rate Canada 2/16 (13%) and US 12/64 (19%)
- CR rate White 14/74 (19%) and Non-White 0/6 (0%)
- CR rate MDS 9/63 (14%) and CMML 5/17 (29%)
- CR rate Int-1 IPSS 5/35 (14%) and Int-2 and high-risk 4/28 (14%)
- CR rate Int-2 2/19 (11%) and high-risk 2/9 (22%)
L1169: Overall, the responses seen on Phase 2 mirror that observed with IV decitabine. Interpretation of the subgroup analyses is limited by the small sample sizes.

L1172: The Astex Quality Assurance group audited 7 study centers, with a total of 8 investigator audits performed from the 15 centers that randomized subjects (one center was audited twice). No critical audit findings were identified. Appropriate corrective and preventive actions were taken for all audit findings.

The data sets are well documented and include definition files. L1181: ## **Transfusion Independence (Phase 2)**

Of the 12 subjects who were platelet transfusion dependent at baseline, 50% were platelet transfusion independent post-baseline. L1207: ## Dose/Dose Response (Phase 1-2)
L1208: ## Data:
L1209: A fixed dose was used in the Phase 2 study; assessment of response by dose was not evaluated in that study. Study ASTX727-01 also had a dose-escalation stage that included 44 subjects with MDS or CMML who were treated with various doses of cedazuridine/decitabine (in mg): 40/20, 60/20, 100/20, 100/40, and 100/30 (ASTX727-01-A CSR). The number of patients were relatively small to assess dose/response as the study was not designed with that objective. However, responses were observed in each cohort. It is worth noting that in the Phase 1 study, a larger proportion of patients had
L1210: prior HMA treatment mostly azacitidine without limitation of number of cycles (46%) compared to only 9% of patients with prior one cycle HMA in Phase 2. The Phase 1 study had the longest follow-up (minimum 18 months, median 24 months) and responses were observed in all doses (Table 29).

L1215: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1216: The ASTX727 clinical program was designed to support PK bridging of ASTX727 at the selected doses of 100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine with IV decitabine dose of 20 mg/m2 in a 5-day regimen. However, data from Phase 1 supports clinical activity at a broad range of doses (cedazuridine 40 to 100 mg) and decitabine (20 to 40 mg) within the limitation of the small number of patients at each dose level and the large percentage of patients with prior HMA treatment in the Phase 1 study.
L1217: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1218: We agree with the Applicant's overall assessment. FDA did not independently adjudicate responses on the Phase 1 trial given the exploratory nature of the efficacy findings on this trial.
L1219: ## **Durability of Response (Phase 2)**
L1220: ## **Data:**
L1221: Duration of response was analyzed based on the subjects who achieved a CR or mCR
L1222: using both K-M method and simple descriptive statistics. In the K-M method, subjects' data were censored on the last date of disease assessment in the absence of progressive disease.
L1223: The median duration of response from the K-M method was 399 days for subjects with a best response of CR (IQR 195, 413) and was 397 days (IQR 170, 560) for subjects with a best response of mCR.
L1224: Using descriptive statistics, the median (min, max) duration of response was 286 days (range 32, 553) for subjects with a best response of CR and was 143 days (range 49, 662) for subjects with a best response of mCR.

L1230: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1231: As expected with IV decitabine and HMA treatment in general, responses are often
L1232: durable. Median duration of CR was 9.5 or 13 months depending on the method used to calculate duration of response. This duration is consistent with previously reported median duration of response of 10 months with IV decitabine 5-day regimen (Steensma et al 2009).
L1233: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1234: In addition to the three CR responses that FDA could not verify above, FDA determined several discrepancies in the ADEFF.xpt dataset with regards to the date of best response, which will affect calculations of duration of response and time to response. These discrepancies were noted in the following patients:
L1235: - Subject USUBJID ASTX727-01 (b) (6): This subject was noted to have a best response of CR on (b) (6). However, the date of the bone marrow assessment for response was listed as (b) (6), the date of the screening marrow assessment. It is not appropriate to use the screening bone marrow for an assessment of response. We note that this patient had 3% blasts on their Cycle 3 Day 1 marrow on (b) (6) (the next marrow). Therefore, the date of achievement of CR should be (b) (6).
- Subject USUBJID ASTX727-01 (b) (6): This patient was noted to have a best response of CR on (b) (6). However, the date of the bone marrow assessment for response was listed as (b) (6), the date of the screening marrow assessment. It is not appropriate to use the screening bone marrow for an assessment of response. We note that this patient had 2% blasts on their next marrow on (b) (6). The patient met all CR criteria at that time. Therefore, the date of achievement of CR should be (b) (6).
- Subject USUBJID ASTX727-01 (b) (6): This patient was noted to have a best response of CR on (b) (6). However, the date of the bone marrow assessment for response was listed as (b) (6), the date of the screening marrow assessment. It is not appropriate to use the screening bone marrow for an assessment of response. Furthermore, the patient had 8% bone marrow blasts at that time. We note that this patient had 1% blasts on their next marrow on (b) (6), but hemoglobin was 10.6 at the time. Count recovery did not occur until (b) (6). Therefore, the date of achievement of CR should be (b) (6).
- Subject USUBJID ASTX727-01 (b) (6): This patient is noted to have a best response of CR on (b) (6). However, the date of the bone marrow assessment for response was listed as (b) (6), which was the screening bone marrow. It is not appropriate to use a screening bone marrow for assessment of response. We note that this patient had 2% blasts on their next marrow on (b) (6). The patient met CR criteria at that time. Therefore, the date of achievement of CR should be (b) (6).
L1236: (b) (6)
L1237: The Applicant submitted an updated ADEFF.xpt file with the 120 day safety update, which included FDA's adjudicated dates of response in the four patients noted above. Using the updated efficacy data, we determined a median duration of CR of 265 days (range 35 – 553 days). This is equivalent to 8.7 months (range 1.1 – 18.2 months).
L1238: ## Persistence of Effect (Phase 2)
L1239: ## **Data:**
L1240: Median duration of response for Phase 2 subjects with best response of CR was &gt;1 year (399 days) and was 397 days for subjects with best response of mCR. Twelve subjects (15%) proceeded to transplant (bone marrow/stem cell transplant). Median overall survival time (as of the data cutoff date) was 549 days (18.3 months).
L1241: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1242: As with most anticancer therapies, loss of therapeutic effect eventually occurs as resistance to the drug's mechanism of action eventually develops; however, responses to ASTX727 were durable, as noted above.
L1243: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1244: As noted above, we determined that median duration of response for phase 2 subjects with best response of CR was 8.7 months (range 1.1 – 18.2 months).
L1245: We confirm that 12 (15%) of patients proceeded to transplant and that median OS was 549 days.
L1246: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints (Phase 2)
L1247: ## **Data:**
L1248: There were no study endpoints evaluating patient-reported outcomes in this study.
L1249: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1250: Not applicable
L1251: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1252: We agree with the Applicant's position.
L1253: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial (Phase 2)
L1254: ## **Data:**
L1255: The median (range) time to response for CR was 147 days (29-571) and was 64 days
L1256: (49-218) for mCR; mCR was therefore achieved earlier but reaching a complete response requires multiple cycles (approximately 4 to 6 cycles consistent with median time to CR of 147 days).
L1257: Exploratory analysis for the presence of mutations in 7 relevant genes (CBL, IDH2, ASXL1, DNMT3A, TP53, RUNX1, and SF3B1) in the baseline bone marrow of 33 subjects was performed, and the presence of mutations or suspicious variants were correlated with response. Of the 14 subjects who had the RUNX1 mutation, 6 (42.9%) had a best response of CR. None of the mutations for the remaining 6 genes was correlated with response.
L1258: A K-M analysis of OS by gene mutation status for the 3 genes (RUNX1, ASXL1, and TP53) with the highest number of subjects with identified mutations was performed. No definitive conclusions can be made due to the small sample size, although median survival time appeared to be lower for the mutant vs. wild type for the 3 genes shown (particularly for the TP53 mutation).
L1259: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1260: Achieving a best response of CR usually requires 4 to 6 cycles (median time to CR 147 days). The exploratory analysis of response and survival by gene mutation is inconclusive due the small number of patients with available mutation data in the study. It does seem to confirm the known poor survival prognosis of TP53 mutation.
L1261: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1262: Using the updated response files submitted with the 120-day safety update (correcting the dates of CR response per FDA adjudication), the median time to CR was unchanged at 147 days (range 51 - 303 days). This is equivalent to 4.8 months (range 1.7 - 10.0 months).
L1263: We performed an analysis of FDA-adjudicated CR responses in the 33 patients with available genomic data at baseline:
L1264: - RUNX1 (n=14): CR 5 (36%)
- ASXL1 (n=13): CR 2 (15%)
- TP53 (n=5): CR 0 (0%)
- SF3B1 (n=2): CR 0 (0%)
- DNMT3A (n=1): CR 0 (0%)
- IDH2 (n=1): CR 0 (0%)
L1265: These analyses are considered exploratory only. The small sample size of the subgroups explored limits the statistical power of such inferences.
L1266: ## 8.1.3. **Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02**
L1267: ## Trial Design
L1268: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1269: Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02 (CSR ASTX727-02) design was identical to Phase 2, and was an open-label, randomized, 2-cycle, 2-sequence crossover study followed by a single-arm continuation of treatment to assess the PK, PD, safety, and efficacy of the ASTX727 FDC tablet (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine) Daily\times5 and IV decitabine 20 mg/m^{2} Daily\times5. At least 118 subjects were planned to be randomized. Treatment continued until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, treatment discontinuation for other reasons, or withdrawal of consent. The study schema is the same as Phase 2 (Figure 19), all patients received ASTX727 as FDC tablet.
L1270: ## Trial Location (Phase 3)
L1271: Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02 was conducted at 37 centers in North America (30 centers in the United States and 7 centers in Canada).
L1272: ## Choice of Control Group (Phase 3)
L1273: The purpose of this study was to establish the total 5-day cycle decitabine AUC equivalence of ASTX727 and IV decitabine in adult patients with MDS or CMML.
L1274: Intravenous decitabine (20 mg/m^{2} once daily for 5 days) is an approved therapy for MDS/CMML (Dacogen USPI 2019) and was therefore the appropriate active comparator.
L1275: ## Diagnostic Criteria (Phase 3)
L1276: Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02 enrolled adult patients with MDS or CMML who had no more than 1 prior cycle of an HMA. Eligibility criteria were similar to Phase 2 with few exceptions. Eligible MDS patients were eligible by either FAB or IPSS criteria to match the Dacogen US label. ECOG PS 2 and other poor general condition criteria (recent hospitalization for infection, proliferative disease, or life expectancy &lt;12 weeks) were excluded in Phase 3 in an effort to minimize the number of patients who may not be able to receive 2 complete cycles of treatment with sufficient PK assessment to be evaluable for the primary endpoint of AUC equivalence.
L1277: ## Key inclusion criteria:
L1278: - Men or women \ge 18 years who are candidates to receive IV decitabine, i.e., subjects with MDS previously treated or untreated with de novo or secondary MDS, including all French-American-British subtypes (refractory anemia, refractory anemia with ringed sideroblasts, refractory anemia with excess blasts, refractory anemia with excess blasts in transformation, and CMML), and subjects with MDS IPSS int-1, -2, or high-risk MDS.
- Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group performance status of 0 to 1.
- Adequate organ function defined as follows:Hepatic: Total or direct bilirubin \le 2 \times ULN; AST/SGOT and ALT/SGPT \le 2.5 \times ULN.
- Renal: serum creatinine \le 1.5 \times ULN or calculated creatinine clearance or glomerular filtration rate &gt; 50 mL/min/1.73 m^2 for subjects with creatinine levels above institutional normal.
- Life expectancy of at least 3 months.
L1279: ## Key exclusion criteria:
L1280: - Prior treatment with more than 1 cycle of azacitidine or decitabine.
- Hospitalization for more than 2 days for documented febrile neutropenia, pneumonia, sepsis, or systemic infection in the 30 days before screening.
- Treatment with any investigational drug or therapy within 2 weeks of study treatment, or 5 half-lives, whichever is longer, before the first dose of study treatment, or ongoing clinically significant AEs from previous treatment.
- Cytotoxic chemotherapy or prior azacitidine or decitabine within 4 weeks of first dose of study treatment.
- Concurrent MDS therapies, including lenalidomide, erythropoietin, cyclosporine/tacrolimus, granulocyte-colony stimulating factor, granulocyte-macrophage colony-stimulating factor, etc. (Prior treatment with these agents is permitted, provided that completion is at least 1 week before the first dose of study treatment.)
- Rapidly progressive or highly proliferative disease (total white blood cell count of &gt; 15 \times 10^9/L) or other criteria that render the subject at high risk of requiring intensive cytotoxic chemotherapy within the next 3 months.
L1281: Other than the relatively small differences between Phase 2 and 3 eligibility, the diagnostic criteria and eligibility criteria were appropriate for assessment of the treatment effect.
L1282: ## Dose Selection (Phase 3)
L1283: Dose selection for Phase 3 was the same dose tested in Phase 2 and discussed above in Section 8.1.1.
L1284: ## Study Treatments (Phase 3)
L1285: - Test Product: ASTX727 FDC tablet (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine).Dose: one ASTX727 tablet administered orally once daily on Days 1 through 5 (Daily×5) in 28-day cycles.
- Reference Product (IV decitabine 5-day regimen).Dose: 20 mg/m2 continuous 1-hour IV infusion Daily×5 in Cycle 1 or 2.
L1286: Study drugs (ASTX727 FDC tablets and IV decitabine) were administered by study center personnel in Cycles 1 and 2. The actual doses ASTX727 and IV decitabine administered were recorded in the CRF.
L1287: In Cycles ≥3, ASTX727 was administered at the study center on Day 1 of each cycle, and subjects were given the remaining 4 ASTX727 tablets in a 5-count bottle for home dosing; subjects were also given IRB approved home dosing logs (diaries) to record home dosing of ASTX727.
L1288: ## Assignment to Treatment (Phase 3)
L1289: Individual subject treatment assignments were determined through a computer-generated randomization schedule and accessed through an interactive web response system. No stratification was performed. Subjects were randomized in a 1:1 ratio to one of the following treatment sequences:
L1290: - Sequence A: ASTX727 tablet in Cycle 1 and then IV decitabine in Cycle 2, or
- Sequence B: IV decitabine in Cycle 1 and then ASTX727 tablet in Cycle 2.
L1291: The randomization and treatment assignment for each subject are provided in the submission (ASTX727-02 CSR, Appendix 16.1.7).
L1292: ## Blinding (Phase 3)
L1293: This is an open-label study.
L1294: ## Dose Modification / Dose Discontinuation (Phase 3)
L1295: Guidelines for adjusting or withholding study treatment are detailed in the protocol for
L1296: ASTX727-02 (Section 7.6). In summary, dose reductions were not allowed in Cycles 1 and 2. If a dose reduction was necessary in Cycles \ge 3, the dose was reduced by decreasing the number of dosing days in a cycle to 4; if further reduction was necessary, the dose could have been reduced to 3 days. Dose delays up to 2 weeks were allowed for recovery of blood counts from drug-related myelosuppression.
L1297: ## Administrative Structure (Phase 3)
L1298: The Applicant was responsible for all aspects of the study, including oversight of the contract research organizations overseeing study management activities in the US and Canada.
L1299: An Independent Review Committee composed of 3 disease experts (who did not participate as investigators in the study) was established for independent assessment of clinical response.
L1300: ## Procedures and Schedule (Phase 3)
L1301: The schedule of events for the study is shown below in Table 31.

L1310: ## Dietary Restrictions/Instructions (Phase 3)
L1311: The ASTX727 tablet was to be taken at the same time on each dosing day, with water. Subjects were instructed to fast from food and non-clear liquids for at least 2 hours before and 2 hours after dosing. No dietary restrictions were in place when subjects received IV decitabine.
L1312: ## Concurrent Medications (Phase 3)
L1313: Antibiotics were permitted to prevent or manage febrile neutropenia. Hematopoietic growth factors were not to have been routinely used. However, short-term use of G-CSF for febrile neutropenia was permitted.
L1314: Drugs that alter gastric pH were allowed but subjects instructed not to take them within \pm 4 hours of ASTX727 administration. Other prohibited concomitant therapies were chemotherapy, immunotherapy, or any experimental therapy. Other nucleosides or drugs that are metabolized by CDA were not to have been administered on ASTX727 dosing days.
L1315: ## Treatment Compliance (Phase 3)
L1316: Study treatment was administered by study center personnel in Cycles 1 and 2. In Cycles \ge 3, ASTX727 was administered at the study center on Day 1 of each cycle, and subjects were given the remaining 4 ASTX727 tablets for home dosing; subjects were also given IRB-approved home dosing diaries to record home dosing of ASTX727. At their next visit, subjects returned the home dosing log and any unused tablets (if a dose reduction occurred) to the study center and study staff entered dosing information into the CRF.
L1317: ## Rescue Medications (Phase 3)
L1318: No rescue medications per se were planned in the trial apart from the permitted concomitant medications and supportive treatments described above.
L1319: ## Subject Completion, Discontinuation, or Withdrawal (Phase 3)
L1320: Subjects were considered to have completed the study when they were withdrawn from the study for any of the following reasons:
L1321: - Death.
- Withdrawal of consent.
- Lost to follow up.
L1322: Subjects lost to follow up were included in statistical analyses to the point of the data cut-off date for the CSR.
L1323: ## Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1324: We agree with the Applicant's description of the trial design of the Phase 3 Study (ASTX727-02).
L1325: ## Study Endpoints (Phase 3)
L1326: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1327: ## Primary Endpoint (Phase 3)
L1328: Comparison between ASTX727 and IV decitabine:
L1329: - Total 5-day AUC exposures of decitabine after treatment with ASTX727 versus IV decitabine (based on AUC_{0-24}).
L1330: ## Secondary Endpoints
L1331: - Safety as assessed by AEs, concomitant medications, physical examination, clinical laboratory tests (hematology, serum chemistry, and urinalysis), vital signs, ECOG performance status, and electrocardiogram.
- Maximum %LINE-1 demethylation.
- Additional secondary PK parameters.
- Clinical response (CR, mCR, PR, and HI) based on International Working Group (IWG) 2006 MDS response criteria.
- Red blood cell (RBC) or platelet transfusion independence (TI).
- Leukemia-free survival, defined as the number of days from the date of randomization to the date when bone marrow or peripheral blood blasts reach \ge 20\%, or death from any cause.
- Overall survival, defined as the number of days from the date of randomization to the date of death from any cause.
L1332: The primary endpoint for the study was based on agreement with FDA at EOP2 meeting on using a PK bridging approach to primarily establish efficacy. The 5-day total cycle AUC_{0-24} was agreed upon during the pharmacometric analysis plan assessment by FDA. PD assessments of DNA methylation by LINE-1 assay is appropriate for quantitative
L1333: comparison of treatment effect on global DNA methylation. Safety assessment from both the randomized first 2 cycles with IV decitabine and long-term from continued ASTX727 treatment is appropriate to assess ASTX727 safety. Finally, efficacy endpoints were secondary in the trial to assess consistency with efficacy previously reported with IV decitabine. At the time of the data cutoff for the CSR, the Phase 3 trial did not have sufficient follow-up (minimum 2 months, median 5 months) to assess response in all treated patients or to assess long-term efficacy such as duration of response, time to AML, or overall survival. These endpoints will be later updated after a minimum follow-up of 12 months.
L1334: ## Efficacy Assessments (Phase 3)
L1335: Efficacy assessments were almost identical to assessments previously summarized in Phase 2 (Section 8.1.1 above).
L1336: Evaluation of response was based on IWG 2006 MDS Response Criteria (Cheson et al 2006) like in Phase 2; however, response evaluation in Phase 3 was independently conducted by a panel of 3 external expert medical reviewers who did not participate as investigators in the study based on database listings of hematology results (peripheral blood and bone marrow) and transfusion data.
L1337: Additional details regarding assessment of clinical response is provided in the Independent Clinical Response Assessors Charter (ASTX727-02 CSR, Appendix 16.1.13.7).
L1338: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1339: We agree with the Applicant's description of the study endpoints of the Phase 3 Study (ASTX727-02).
L1340: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments (Phase 3)**
L1341: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1342: The analyses for this Phase 3 study are described in two different documents, the Pharmacometric Analysis Plan (06 December 2018) and the Statistical Analysis Plan (27 February 2019). The Pharmacometric Analysis Plan addressed the PK analyses of plasma decitabine, cedazuridine and cedazuridine-epimer following single and multiple once daily oral doses of decitabine and cedazuridine. The planned analyses of the primary endpoint, total 5-day AUC0-24 exposures of decitabine after treatment with ASTX727 versus IV decitabine, were described in the PAP as well as all planned analyses of the secondary PK parameters. The Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) described the planned analyses of all other secondary endpoints.
L1343: ## Sample Size
L1344: The sample size calculation was based on preliminary analysis of 5-day AUC in study ASTX727-01, which showed an estimate of intra-subject CV of 0.5. A conservative CV value of 0.55 was chosen to calculate the sample size to include in the 2 one-sided equivalence tests for the geometric mean ratio of ASTX727 5-day AUC relative to IV decitabine 5-day AUC to provide 90% power at the statistical significance level of 0.05, when the true ratio of geometric means is 1.0, the CV under an unlogged scale is 0.55, and the 90% CI equivalence limits for the ratio of geometric means are 0.8 and 1.25. It was assumed that 10% of subjects may not be evaluable in for the primary PK analysis.
L1345: ## Analysis Populations
L1346: Two Pharmacokinetic Analysis Populations were used for analysis of PK data and were defined as follows:
L1347: The **Primary Endpoint PK Analysis Population** was used for calculating decitabine 5-day (total cycle) AUC0-24 and equivalence statistical analyses. This analysis set included decitabine daily AUC0-24 from subjects who received 5-day dosing of ASTX727 and IV decitabine in Cycles 1 and 2 and who met the following criteria for both ASTX727 and IV decitabine dosing and PK assessments:
L1348: - ASTX727: received full dose of ASTX727 (i.e., Days 1 through 5) within 3 hours of the intended dosing time, and no vomiting occurred within 6 hours of dosing.
- IV decitabine: received the full dose as a 1-hour infusion.
- Subjects in this analysis set had at least 1 evaluable day of decitabine AUC0-24 measurement in the IV decitabine cycle, either Day 1 or Day 5. When both days were available, the average value was used to calculate total 5-day AUC0-24. If data from 1 of the 2 available IV days were deemed to be extreme outlier and excluded, the data from the other IV day (Day 1 or Day 5) was used for analysis.
L1349: The **Overall PK Analysis Population** included additional subjects who may not have been included in the Primary Endpoint PK Analysis Set and who:
L1350: - Received any amount of study treatment (including subjects who received only 1 cycle of treatment).
- Complied with the protocol sufficiently to ensure PK samples were collected as intended.
- Provided sufficient samples to measure available plasma concentrations for decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine-epimer.
L1351: The Overall PK Analysis Population was used for calculating PK parameters, including decitabine daily AUC0-24, which was used to derive the primary PK endpoint of
L1352: decitabine cumulative 5-day AUC_{0-24} exposures and other secondary PK parameters for decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine-epimer.
L1353: Two additional analysis populations were used for sensitivity analyses of the PK endpoints:
L1354: **Evaluable unpaired PK population:** the overall sensitivity population, defined as the subjects who contributed PK data, excluding subjects whose samples had integrity issues.
L1355: **Paired sensitivity population:** the subjects in the paired analysis, excluding subjects who received only 1 cycle of treatment.
L1356: The following Analysis Sets were used for analysis of all non-PK secondary endpoints:
L1357: The **Efficacy Analysis Set** includes data from all subjects who received any amount of study treatment. All data was included, and no subjects excluded because of protocol deviations. Subjects were analyzed according to their randomly assigned treatment sequence.
L1358: The **Safety Analysis Set** includes data from all subjects who received any amount of study treatment. In the safety analysis, no data exclusion was allowed because of protocol deviations. Subjects were analyzed according to the treatment sequence received.
L1359: The **PD LINE-1 Analysis Set** includes data from subjects if they received any amount of study treatment and had LINE-1 methylation data at baseline (Day 1) of Cycle 1 or 2 and on either Day 8 or Day 15 of the respective cycle.
L1360: ## Methods for Handling Missing Data (Phase 3)
L1361: ## *BLQ Data*
L1362: All plasma concentrations that were below the lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) or missing were labeled as below the limit of quantification (BLQ) or missing, respectively, in the concentration data listings. Considering the number of PK samples to estimate AUC_{0-24}, BLQ values were set to 0 for statistical summary of concentrations and calculation of PK parameters.
L1363: Unless otherwise noted, data summaries were performed on only observed data.
L1364: ## *Missing Incomplete Dosing Information*
L1365: In cases in which subjects were dosed with any of the study drugs but discontinued treatment early, the concentration data were reported in the concentration tables and included in all summary statistics and PK analysis if sufficient concentration data were available to permit PK analysis for that treatment in that subject.
L1366: ## *Missing Sampling Dates and/or Times*
L1367: For concentration-time profiles from subjects with missing sample dates and/or times, the nominal sampling day and/or time (as appropriate) were assumed for the calculation of PK parameters.
L1368: ## *Unexpected Data and Potential Scenarios for Exclusion*
L1369: Every effort was made to include as many of the subjects as possible in the Primary Endpoint PK Analysis Population for the primary analysis. Exclusions of subjects from the primary analysis set were expected to be rare and could have happened under the following circumstances:
L1370: - The subject did not complete two full cycles of treatment and PK assessments to include both IV decitabine and ASTX727 cycles.
- The PK profile for the subject manifests as an extreme outlier versus profiles of the remaining population, with an atypical profile possibly a result of contamination during collection of blood samples (i.e., collection from the second lumen of the peripherally inserted central catheter, instead of contralateral arm, following IV infusion).
- Atypical and unexplained PK profile for a subject rendering extrapolation of AUC to 24 hours not possible due to inability to calculate \lambda_z.
- Atypical decitabine PK profile influenced by vomiting after ASTX727 dosing.
L1371: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1372: We agree with the Applicant's description of the SAP of the Phase 3 Study (ASTX727-02).

L1398: ## Patient Disposition (Phase 3)
L1399: ## Data:
L1400: A total of 138 subjects were randomized in the Phase 3 study from 30 study centers in the US and 7 in Canada. The Efficacy Analysis Set and the Safety Analysis Set were composed of the 133 treated subjects. All randomized and treated subjects were included in the efficacy and safety analyses datasets. Five subjects were randomized but not treated (3 subjects withdrew prior to treatment, 1 withdrew due to elevation of bilirubin and creatinine before treatment, and 1 subject was lost to follow up after randomization).
L1401: As of the CSR data cutoff date (19 March 2019), 94 subjects were alive and ongoing treatment. The median duration of follow-up was 155 days (~5 months; range 57 to 397 days). The median duration of treatment was 4 cycles (range, 1 to 11 cycles).

L1409: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1410: None of the treated subjects were excluded from planned preliminary efficacy or safety analyses.
L1411: ## **Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1412: FDA performed an updated analysis of subject disposition based on updated efficacy data from Study ASTX727-02 received with the 120 day safety update. The 120-day update used a cutoff date of 11/1/2019. For the purposes of determining reasons for treatment discontinuation, FDA considers death under the primary reason for discontinuation (i.e., either primary disease or adverse event). The reason "adverse event" included 8 deaths due to an adverse event, in addition to the disposition events
L1413: coded as adverse event. There were no deaths in the category for “primary disease.”
L1414: Of the 12 treatment discontinuations for “Other,” 7 appeared to be due to the primary disease, 3 were physician decision, one was due to prolonged myelosuppression, and one was due to withdrawal of consent.

L1418: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations (Phase 3)
L1419: ## Data:
L1420: Important deviations as determined by the Astex medical monitor and PK expert were those that affected a subject's rights, safety or welfare, and/or the integrity of the study data. A total of 15 subjects had at least 1 important protocol deviation. Categories of important deviations, numbers of subjects in each category, and brief descriptions are as follows:
L1421: - Eligibility criteria: 2 (1 subject had a 27-day washout from prior azacitidine treatment instead of 28-day and 1 subject was hospitalized more than 2 days within 30 days of screening)
- Study drug administration: 1 (94 mL of IV decitabine infusion was given rather than the full 108 mL)
- Study procedures: 9 (various PK and LINE-1 sample collection time points missed)
- Safety: 3 (SAEs not reported to Astex with 24 hours)
L1422: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1423: These protocol deviations did not affect the study outcome or conclusions.
L1424: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1425: We agree with the Applicant's assessment. None of the deviations were likely to bias the study in favor of the study drug. Therefore, all patients, including those with important protocol deviations, were included in our analysis of efficacy endpoints.

L1450: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1451: Baseline disease characteristics of subjects in this study are representative of the patient population intended to receive the marketed product. All IPSS risk categories were represented and patients with CMML were also included.
L1452: Baseline disease characteristics were generally balanced between the treatment sequences, but there were some imbalances that may confer worse prognosis for patients randomized to Sequence A vs Sequence B. Sequence A (ASTX727 in Cycle 1) compared with Sequence B (IV decitabine in Cycle 1) had a notably lower representation of CMML subjects (7.6% vs 16.4%), a higher representation of IPSS High-Risk MDS subjects (21.2% vs 10.4%), more subjects with low baseline neutrophils (42.4% vs 31.3% &lt;1000/µL), and fewer subjects with high baseline neutrophils (37.9% vs 49.3% &gt;1500/µL).
L1453: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1454: In the Phase 3 trial, the mean time since diagnosis was 548.9 days (78.4 weeks) and median time since diagnosis was 83 days (11.9 weeks). This is more comparable to the mean and median duration of therapy noted in the IV decitabine label (mean 86 weeks and median 29 weeks on the randomized trial and mean 444 days and median 154 days on the single-arm trial).
L1455: Only 10 (8%) received up to 1 cycle of prior HMA therapy prior to enrolling on the trial. FDA obtained further information from the Applicant regarding prior therapies in a response to information request received on 3/10/2020. Results of FDA's subsequent analysis of prior therapies are displayed in the Table below. Our analysis differed from the Sponsor's only in that we identified an additional patient who received G-CSF (n=3; Subjects (b)(6)). The Sponsor had only reported 2 (2%) subjects with prior G-CSF.

L1460: Again, the USPI for IV decitabine states that 30% of patients on the decitabine arm of the randomized trial and 27% of patients on the single arm trial received previous MDS therapy. The percentage here (23%) is slightly lower. No subjects were reported as
L1461: having prior HSCT in the Phase 3 database. The median duration of treatment of prior anticancer therapy (excluding azacitidine and decitabine which were given for only one cycle) was 124.5 days (range 1 to 1158 days). Subject (b) (6) received lenalidomide for 1158 days.
L1462: Of note, the 16 (12%) of patients without IPSS results documented had CMML. IPSS cytogenetic risk classification was missing/not evaluable in 12 (9%). Median baseline ANC was 1.21 in Sequence A, 1.50 in Sequence B, and 1.36 in all subjects. Of note, the Applicant excluded missing values when calculating the number of patients with bone marrow blasts &gt; 5% at baseline. FDA found that 26 (39%) of patients in Sequence A, 27 (40%) of patients in Sequence B, and 53 (40%) of patients overall had bone marrow blasts &gt; 5% at baseline.
L1463: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use (Phase 3)
L1464: ## **Data:**
L1465: During the 2-way crossover, 129 subjects (97%) received both cycles of treatment (oral and IV).
L1466: The median number of cycles received was 4 (range, 1 to 11) and 39 subjects (29.3%) received 6 or more cycles. Eighty-two subjects (61.7%) had dose delayed cycles, and 27.1% had dose reduced cycles. However, the study is ongoing and the median follow up at the time of data cutoff was only 5 months.

L1473: The most commonly used concomitant medication classes were Antibacterials for Systemic Use (78.2%); Analgesics (74.4%), and Antivirals for Systemic Use (68.4%). Rescue medications were not used in this study.
L1474: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1475: Treatment compliance was well controlled/managed at the study centers during the first 2 cycles as all treatments (ASTX727 and IV decitabine) were administered by study center personnel.
L1476: Dose reduced cycles and treatment cycle delays were permitted for management of cytopenias. Overall, most (72.9%) subjects did not have a dose reduced cycle during the Phase 3 study as of the CSR data cutoff. For the primary PK endpoint, only subjects who received both treatments (ASTX727 FDC tablets and IV decitabine) and had adequate PK samples to be able to calculate 5-day AUC for both treatments were included in the primary paired PK analysis. No formal statistical comparisons between treatments (oral and IV) were performed for efficacy endpoints.
L1477: Concomitant medications used during the study were mainly for supportive treatment or treatment for comorbidities, so they did not affect the efficacy results.
L1478: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1479: FDA performed a separate analysis of exposure based on months on therapy using the updated efficacy data from the 120-day update (see Table below).

L1483: At the time of the initial submission, only 21% of patients were treated for more than 6 months, but at the time of the efficacy update, this increased to 59%. Furthermore, the maximum duration of therapy was initially just over 12 months and is now up to 19.7 months. A total of 22% now have remained on therapy for more than a year. This should provide a more accurate representation of efficacy than what was provided with the initial submission.
L1484: ## **Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses) (Phase 3)**
L1485: ## **Data:**
L1486: The primary study endpoint for this study was a PK endpoint (total cycle 5-day decitabine AUC). The results of the comparison of 5-day decitabine AUC (oral vs IV), including sensitivity analyses, are presented above in Section 6.3.1 (Table 6). In this Phase 3 study, efficacy endpoints were secondary (Table 37).
L1487: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1488: The primary objective of this study was achieved and demonstrated that the decitabine exposures (5-day AUC) achieved from ASTX727 FDC tablets was equivalent to IV decitabine. The preliminary efficacy results in this study are supportive of the efficacy results achieved in the Phase 2 study and are presented below.
L1489: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1490: See Section 6 of the Assessment Aid for FDA's assessment of PK results from the Phase 3 trial.
L1491: ## Data Quality and Integrity (Phase 3)
L1492: ## **Data:**
L1493: The Astex Quality Assurance group audited 5 centers from the 37 centers that randomized subjects. No critical audit findings were identified at 4 of the centers audited. Appropriate corrective and preventive actions were taken for all audit findings. Data integrity concerns were identified at one center (Site 217) as part of a for-cause audit. These findings were reported to the OSI on 21 November 2019.
L1494: The data in this submission are in eCTD format, in accordance with FDA guidance on electronic submission. The data sets are well documented and include definition files. The data sets, clinical study reports, summary of clinical safety, and summary of clinical efficacy are located in Modules 5 and 2 of the eCTD. |\CDSESUB1\evsprod\NDAnnnnn.
L1495: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1496: The PK data collected at Site 217 was not useable and the PK data collected from the 2 subjects randomized at this site were not included in the primary PK analysis.
L1497: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1498: No issues were identified with the data quality or integrity from the pivotal study which could affect the efficacy results, aside from the issues with the response assessments noted above. The submitted datasets are generally consistent, and variables are clearly labeled and/or explained.
L1499: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints (Phase 3)
L1500: ## **Data:**
L1501: Efficacy was not a primary endpoint in Phase 3. Efficacy results presented in this section are considered preliminary at this time, as almost half the subjects had not completed 6 months of follow-up as of the data cutoff date for the CSR (median duration of follow-up was ~5 months). As of the cutoff date for the primary CSR, only response rates and transfusion independence were evaluated and reported in the Phase 3 CSR.
L1502: Long-term efficacy endpoints (duration of response and time to event endpoints) were not assessed or reported in the CSR. Exploratory analysis of these events based on the cutoff date for the CSR are reported in this document and will be updated when all subjects complete a minimum of at least 12 months of follow-up.

L1513: ## Transfusion Independence (Phase 3)
L1514: Transfusion independence post-treatment was defined as no transfusions for ≥56 consecutive days while maintaining hemoglobin ≥8 g/dL and platelets ≥20×109/L.
L1515: Overall, of the 55 patients who were RBC or platelet transfusions dependent at baseline, 32.7% were transfusion independent post-baseline as of the cutoff date for the CSR.
L1516: One-third (32.7%) of the 52 subjects who were RBC transfusion dependent at baseline were RBC transfusion independent post-baseline. A 30% rate of platelet transfusion independence post-baseline was observed for the 10 subjects with platelet transfusion dependence at baseline.
L1517: Transfusion independence data by each response category are summarized in Table 39. Despite the limitation of the short follow-up to assess transfusion independence all patients with CR (100%) were transfusion independent post-treatment.

At that time, the median duration of follow-up was 12.6 months and the median duration on treatment was 8.1 months. L1529: We were in agreement with the Applicant's assessment of the complete remission rate in the updated efficacy data set. FDA adjudicated CR responses based on evaluation of bone marrow and laboratory results in the submission.
L1530: Again, the acceptable response rate for MDS for regulatory decision making is ORR, defined as CR+PR responses. There were no PR responses on the Phase 3 trial of ASTX727, and therefore, only CR responses were considered for demonstration of clinical benefit. Lesser responses than CR/PR do not convey clear clinical benefit to patients. Overall, the CR responses seen on Phase 3 mirror that observed with IV decitabine.
L1531: We again note that the Phase 3 trial used IWG 2006 MDS Response Criteria, which differs from that used in the IV decitabine prescribing information (IWG 2000 MDS Response Criteria) (Cheson, Bennett et al. 2000).
