L1541: ## Dose/Dose Response (Phase 3)
L1542: ## **Data:**
L1543: A fixed dose was used in this study to produce decitabine exposure equivalent to IV decitabine at the approved dose of the 5-day regimen, and assessment of response by dose was not evaluated. The recommended dose and regimen for ASTX727 is 100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine given orally (as the ASTX727 FDC tablet) once daily on Days 1 through 5 of each 28-day cycle. In addition, ASTX727 treatment at the recommended dose and regimen achieved almost identical demethylation to IV
L1544: decitabine, as measured by % LINE-1 demethylation (Table 8).
L1545: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1546: No studies were conducted to assess dose-response relationship.
L1547: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1548: We agree with the Applicant's assessment.
L1549: ## **Durability of Response (Phase 3)**
L1550: ## **Data:**
L1551: The efficacy results presented for the Phase 3 study are preliminary and are based on available data as of the cutoff date for the primary CSR. Overall survival was not assessed in the first analysis for this study. Responses are ongoing in the study and with the K-M method median duration of response was not estimable. At the data cutoff for the first analysis in this study, all CR patients have not yet progressed and only one patient with mCR has progressed, so the duration of response for patients in CR or mCR are preliminary underestimates. With that caveat, the median (range) duration of response was 113+ days (29+, 306+) for subjects with a best response of CR, and 63+ days (range 4+, 320+) for subjects with best response of mCR.
L1552: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1553: Duration of response and overall survival for the Phase 3 study will be assessed in the second analysis of efficacy after all subjects have completed a minimum of 12 months of follow up. Almost all patients with CR or mCR have not yet progressed at the data cutoff of the first analysis in Phase 3. Durability of response is presented above in Section 8.1.1 for the Phase 2 study.
L1554: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1555: FDA performed an assessment of duration of CR based on the updated efficacy data from the 120 day update (see FDA assessment Table above for Study ASTX727-02 Phase 3). The median duration of response is now 7.5 (range 1.7-17.5) months. This is similar, but slightly lower than that observed on the Phase 2 trial. It is likely that the median will increase over time with longer follow-up. Of the 28 CR responses, there were only 4 relapses to date, with most responses ongoing at the data cutoff date.
L1556: Of note, the duration of CR+PR in the IV decitabine label was 288 days (range 116-388), which is equivalent to 9.4 months (range 3.8-12.8) months on the controlled trial and 443 days (range 72-722) days or 14.6 months (range 2.4-23.7) months on the single-arm trial.
L1557: ## Persistence of Effect (Phase 3)
L1558: ## **Data:**
L1559: The preliminary data from Phase 3 support persistence of effect. Persistence of effect is described above in Section 8.1.1 for the Phase 2 study.
L1560: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1561: Preliminary data from Phase 3 are supportive of durable effect but will be updated with longer-term follow-up.
L1562: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1563: As noted above, we determined that median duration of response for phase 3 subjects with best response of CR was 7.5 months (range 1.7 – 17.5 months) using the updated efficacy data with the 120-day safety update.

L1568: Not applicable
L1569: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1570: We agree with the Applicant's assessment.
L1571: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial (Phase 3)
L1572: ## **Data:**
L1573: Assessment of response is ongoing in the Phase 3 study. As of the cutoff date, the median (range) time to response for all treated subjects (N=133) was 99 days (42-158) for CR and was 63 days (42-227) for mCR.
L1574: No additional efficacy analyses were conducted for this study.
L1575: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1576: Median time to best response of CR was approximately 3 months supporting the need for at least 3-4 cycles.
L1577: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1578: Using the updated efficacy data, we determined the median time to CR was 132 days
L1579: (range 65 – 462 days), i.e., 4.3 months (range 2.1 – 15.2 months). Of note, this is similar to that observed with IV decitabine. In the USPI for IV decitabine, the median (range) time to CR+PR was 3.1 (1.8-8.9) months on the randomized trial and 5.3 (1.6-8.8) months on the single arm trial.
L1580: ## 8.1.5. **Integrated Review of Effectiveness**
L1581: **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
See below sections for our integrated assessment of effectiveness.
L1582: ## 8.1.6. **Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials**
L1583: **The Applicant's Position:**
The approach to establish the safety and effectiveness of ASTX727 FDC tablets (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine) was based primarily on a pharmacokinetic (PK) bridging approach to IV decitabine. In this application, the primary efficacy results are from the Phase 2 (ASTX727-01). Supportive efficacy results from the Phase 3 study (ASTX727-02) are provided.
L1584: All long-term efficacy endpoints, such as survival, are summarized from the Phase 2 study results only (Section 8.1.2). Due to the shorter duration of follow-up in Phase 3, only preliminary results for response rate and transfusion independence are presented here.
L1585: Complete blood counts (CBC) were completed prior to every cycle of treatment in both studies. Bone marrow was assessed every other cycle at least up to Cycle 7 until disease progression or treatment discontinuation. Transfusion needs were assessed continuously during the treatment period. In Phase 2, response was evaluated by medical review and statistical programming conducted by Astex (see implementation guide in the SAP). In Phase 3, response was assessed by an Independent Review Committee (IRC) composed of 2 disease experts (and adjudicated by a third, when necessary) who did not participate as investigators in the trial (see IRC charter). The IRC also assessed the categories of Stable Disease and Progressive Disease in the Phase 3 study, while in Phase 2 these categories were presented collectively as No Response.
L1586: **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. In addition, FDA notes that the follow-up efficacy data from the Phase 3 trial received with the 120-day safety update added assurance of the clinical benefit of ASTX727, based on comparable efficacy results to the Phase 2 trial and that of IV decitabine.
L1587: ## Primary Endpoints
L1588: ## Data:
L1589: **PK:** Decitabine total cycle 5-day AUC was a primary endpoint in both the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies. The results of the primary PK endpoint for Phase 2 (5-day AUC_{0-t}) and Phase 3 (5-day AUC_{0-24}) are presented in Section 6.3.1 (Table 12 and Table 13 for Phase 2 and Table 6 and Table 7 for Phase 3).
L1590: **PD:** LINE-1 methylation was a primary endpoint in Phase 2 and a secondary endpoint in Phase 3. The LINE-1 methylation results are presented in Section 6.3.1 (Table 8 and Table 9).
L1591: **Clinical efficacy:** Response rate was a primary endpoint in Phase 2 and secondary endpoint in Phase 1 and 3. Response rates for all Phases are summarized below and compared with published data from IV decitabine 5-day regimen (Steensma et al 2009), which also used IWG 2006 criteria for response (Table 40).

L1598: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1599: Overall, evidence of clinical activity with ASTX727 was observed in all phases of ASTX727 studies. Response rate was relatively low for Phase 1 due to a large percentage of subjects who received prior HMA (46%). In Phase 2, the response rate was high with 21% reporting CR, and median survival of ~18 months. The data from Phase 3 are preliminary, but it is worth noting the CR rate of 9% in all treated subjects and 12% in evaluable subjects. There was also a high percentage of mCR (34.6%) that it is likely that some of them will become CR with further follow up. This would be consistent with the observation that median time to CR is usually relatively longer than time to mCR as reported above for Phase 2 and 3. The primary PK endpoint of similar or equivalent systemic decitabine exposures was consistently met in Phase 2 and Phase 3 within the limitation of the smaller sample size in Phase 2. Also, Phase 2 and 3 produced consistent data of similar effect on DNA methylation from ASTX727 and IV decitabine.
L1600: The overall response rate was 60% in Phase 2 and 49% to 64% in Phase 3 in all treated and evaluable subjects, respectively.
L1601: The response rates observed for ASTX727 in the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies are similar to those reported for 5-day IV decitabine dosing (Steensma et al 2009; Dacogen USPI 2019), suggesting that the equivalent AUC exposures and similar effect on DNA methylation observed for ASTX727 and IV decitabine produce the same meaningful clinical activity.
L1602: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1603: FDA disagrees with the Applicant's assessment of the efficacy results from the Phase 2 trial, as noted in Section 8.1.2 above. FDA's adjudicated CR rate was 14/80 (18%; 95% CI 10-28%) with a median duration of response of 8.7 (range 1.1-18.2) months. The CR rate on the Phase 3 trial using updated efficacy data from the 120-day safety update was 28/133 (21%; 15-29%) with a median duration of response of 7.5 (range 1.7-17.5) months. Both are similar to the ORRs (CR+PR) reported in the USPI for IV decitabine, which were 17% for the randomized trial and 16% for the single arm trial.
L1604: Again, the acceptable response rate for MDS appropriate for regulatory decision making is overall response rate (ORR), defined as CR+PR responses. ORR served as the basis for approval of IV decitabine. There were no PR responses on either the Phase 2 or Phase 3 trials of ASTX727, and therefore, only CR responses are considered for demonstration of clinical benefit. Lesser responses than CR/PR do not convey clear clinical benefit to patients.
L1605: ## Secondary and Other Endpoints
L1606: ## **Data:**
L1607: **Transfusions:** Data on transfusions were available from both Phase 2 and 3. In Phase 2, 50% of subjects who were RBC or platelet transfusion dependent at baseline were transfusion independent for \ge 56 consecutive days. In Phase 3, RBC and platelet transfusion independence for \ge 56 consecutive days was achieved in 32.7% and 30.0% of subjects, respectively, despite the insufficient follow-up in the study at the time of this analysis. Transfusion independence for both RBCs and platelets in those who were dependent at baseline ranged from 33% in Phase 3 (short follow-up) to 49% in Phase 2. The rate of transfusion independence with IV decitabine for RBCs was 33% in the published data (Steensma et al 2009).

L1616: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1617: The secondary efficacy endpoint of transfusion independence was supportive of clinical benefit from ASTX727 that is consistent with IV decitabine previously reported data.
L1618: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1619: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment regarding the transfusion benefit of ASTX727. On the Phase 2 trial, of 41 patients who were transfusion dependent (TD) of RBCs or platelets at baseline, 49% became transfusion independent (TI) of both RBCs and platelets for at least 56 days post-baseline. Of 39 patients who were TI of RBCs and platelets at baseline, 64% remained TI of both RBCs and platelets for at least 56 days post-baseline. Using the updated efficacy data on Phase 3, the numbers were very similar. Of 57 patients TD of RBCs or platelets at baseline, 53% became TI of both RBCs and platelets for at least 56 days post-baseline and of 76 patients who were TI at baseline, 63% remained TI for at least 56 days post-baseline. This data supports a transfusion benefit of ASTX727 in patients with MDS and CMML.
L1620: ## **Subpopulations**
L1621: ## **Data:**
L1622: Due to the differences in duration of treatment and follow-up between the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies, efficacy data were not pooled for subgroup analyses. The evaluation of the effects of major demographic factors (age, sex) and other relevant intrinsic and extrinsic factors on the PK of decitabine or cedazuridine was performed and results are presented above in Section 6.2.1. Assessment of response by different subgroups based on age, sex, race, country, disease (MDS and CMML), and different MDS IPSS categories were performed for Phase 2 and reported above in Table 25 to Table 26.
L1623: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1624: Subgroup analyses were performed for efficacy endpoints (e.g., response rate) by different subpopulations and clinical activity was observed in all subgroups within the limitation of the sample size. In addition age, sex, body weight, renal function, and hepatic function were assessed as potential covariates in a PopPK analysis and found no clinically meaningful effects that would require dose adjustments (see Section 6.2.1 and 6.3.2). The effects of severe hepatic impairment or severe renal impairment on the PK of decitabine and cedazuridine is unknown. Studies ASTX727-01 and ASTX727-02 did not have sufficient racial diversity to inform potential correlations with regard to race or ethnicity.
L1625: The assessment of response by disease type showed that ASTX727 was clinically active in both MDS and CMML patients. Patients with CMML tended to have better response (Table 27).
L1626: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1627: In general, interpretation of the subgroup analyses is limited by the small sample sizes.
L1628: The Sponsor noted a better response rate in CMML patients, which was observed in Phase 2 (CR rate 5/17 [29%] CMML vs 9/63 [14%] MDS). However, the opposite was observed using the updated efficacy data from Phase 3 (CR rate 2/16 [13%] CMML vs 26/117 [22%] MDS). This indicates that the efficacy of ASTX727 is likely similar in patients with MDS and CMML.
L1629: ## Additional Efficacy Considerations
L1630: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1631: Regarding considerations on benefit in the post-market setting, while patients who enroll on clinical trials tend to differ in various ways from the broader population of patients with cancer, there is no indication that efficacy would be lower in patient populations underrepresented on the Phase 2 or Phase 3 trials. Given that the therapy is oral, it will be important for prescribers to monitor compliance and adverse events closely.
L1632: ## 8.1.7. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L1633: ## **Data:**
L1634: Substantial evidence of effectiveness of ASTX727 was established primarily through the PK-bridging approach. Evidence of decitabine AUC equivalence between ASTX727 and IV decitabine was established in the Phase 3 study (ASTX727-02). The primary evidence of clinical benefit (CR) and durability of response was demonstrated in the long-term Phase 2 study with complementary clinical benefit based on preliminary efficacy results from the Phase 1 and 3 studies.
L1635: Results from the clinical development program demonstrated that ASTX727 is associated with the following benefits:
L1636: - Oral ASTX727 resulted in decitabine 5-day AUC exposure that was equivalent to IV decitabine with high certainty. The oral/IV 5-day decitabine AUC of the FDC in the Phase 3 was approximately 99% with a narrow 90% CI (92.66%, 105.6%). The oral/IV AUC comparison was robust, with all sensitivity and secondary oral/IV AUC comparisons were consistent with the primary analysis, including all 90% CIs entirely contained between 80% and 125%.
- Data from the development program also showed a similar biological effect of ASTX727 compared with IV decitabine in the first 2 cycles. Differences of DNA demethylation as measured by LINE-1 assay was approximately 1% or less between ASTX727 and IV decitabine in Phase 2 and 3.
- Since efficacy from HMA treatment requires multiple (at least 4 to 6) treatment cycles (Table 1), the long-term efficacy outcomes in the ASTX727 program were mainly driven by ASTX727, as patients received only 1 cycle of IV decitabine. While
L1637: ASTX727 would be expected to result in similar efficacy to IV decitabine based on the above equivalent decitabine systemic exposure and similar biological effect, it is reassuring that the mature data from the Phase 2 study also support that conclusion, as follows:
L1638: - CR rate of 21% with ASTX727 compared to 9% to 15% in the Dacogen USPI 2019 (3-day and 5-day regimen respectively) and 17% as reported in Steensma et al 2009.
- RBC and platelet transfusion independence in about 50% of patients who were transfusion-dependent at baseline compared to 33% reported in Steensma et al 2009 for RBC transfusion independence.
- Median survival of 18 months compared to 19 months reported in Steensma et al 2009.
L1639: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1640: While decitabine plasma AUC exposures are largely considered to be the principal PK determinant of both biological and clinical activity of decitabine, long interspersed nucleotide element-1 (LINE-1) demethylation and safety data were collected in a randomized design in the first 2 cycles of Phase 2 and 3 to confirm similar biological activity and safety to further support the PK bridging approach to IV decitabine. In addition, subjects continued to receive ASTX727 after the first 2 randomized cycles so that long-term efficacy and safety data were also collected in Studies ASTX727-01 and ASTX727-02. This provided additional support that clinical data from ASTX727 are consistent with the expected clinical profile of IV decitabine from published data. LINE-1 demethylation was similar between ASTX727 and IV decitabine in the first 2 cycles. The clinical safety was also generally comparable between ASTX727 and IV decitabine in the first 2 cycles. Long-term efficacy and safety results after multiple cycles of ASTX727 were consistent with the efficacy and safety reported with decitabine IV using the 5-day regimen (Steensma et al 2009). Results from studies with ASTX727 therefore support the conclusion that 5-day decitabine AUC exposure is appropriate to use as a bridge to clinical effects.
L1641: The availability of an oral HMA alternative will alleviate a significant burden to patients and patient care givers by eliminating the need for monthly 5-day IV infusions in a clinic or hospital setting that may need to continue for several months or even years in responding patients. This may also avoid premature treatment discontinuation that could occur as a result of the burden of multiple-day IV infusions every month, particularly for the most elderly patients and/or for those who live far from a treatment center. Premature HMA treatment discontinuation has been reported to result in early relapse (Cabrero et al 2015). An oral treatment will also avoid potential safety issues due to multiple subcutaneous injections or multiple IV infusions that may require the establishment and maintenance of a central IV line, which could also result in additional
L1642: complications.
L1643: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1644: The efficacy of ASTX727 has been established, based on the results of the Phase 2 and Phase 3 trials in adult patients with MDS and CMML (for which IV decitabine is currently approved). Patients who received ASTX727 (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine) achieved a CR rate of 18-21%. Duration of CR responses were sufficiently durable and roughly comparable (although slightly lower) than that reported for IV decitabine. However, many of the responses were ongoing at the time of data cutoff, and thus, the median duration of CR would be expected to increase with longer follow-up.
L1645: The CR benefit was supported by a transfusion benefit on both trials, which demonstrated that 49-53% of patients dependent on transfusions at baseline achieved at least 56 days transfusion independence post-baseline and 63-64% of patients transfusion independent at baseline were able to maintain that transfusion independence for at least 56 days post-baseline.
L1646: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1647: ## **Data:**
L1648: The safety profile of ASTX727 in patients with MDS and CMML was primarily assessed based on safety results from the Phase 2 portion of ASTX727-01 study (ASTX727-01-B) and the Phase 3 study (ASTX727-02) where subjects were treated with the recommended dose of ASTX727 (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine). The safety data of all patients who received ASTX727 from the Phase 2 (78 patients) and Phase 3 (130 patients) studies were pooled (N=208). The key findings from the pooled analyses are provided in the following sections.
L1649: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1650: The safety profile of ASTX727 in patients with MDS and CMML was consistent with the known safety profile for IV decitabine, and no new safety concerns were identified.
L1651: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1652: FDA agrees with the Applicant's approach of pooling safety data across Studies ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02 given the similar study designs and patient populations. Two patients from Phase 2 and three patients from Phase 3 were only treated with IV decitabine during cycle 1 (i.e. never received ASTX727), and therefore, were excluded from the safety set. This led to the total of 208 patients in the pooled population.
L1653: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L1654: ## **Data:**
L1655: The primary studies used to describe the safety of ASTX727 in patients with MDS or CMML include the Phase 2 portion of Study ASTX727-01 (ASTX727-01-B CSR) and the Phase 3 Study ASTX727 02.
L1656: Results from each trial are included in this submission through the data cutoff dates established for the respective clinical study reports (CSRs) (Table 42).
L1657: In addition, this submission includes relevant safety findings following medical review of all serious adverse events (SAEs) in the safety database that were reported in ongoing studies, from the CSR data cutoff dates through 12 October 2019 (approximately 60 days before the planned NDA submission).

L1664: Comparison of AE incidence rates were limited to comparison of ASTX727 to IV decitabine during the first 2 cycles of treatment only (during the crossover portion of
L1665: the study). In Cycle 3 and beyond, subjects only received ASTX727. IV decitabine was only administered for 1 cycle in both the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies.
L1666: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1667: The safety review focuses on safety based on overall treatment exposure of ASTX727 in patients with MDS or CMML from 208 patients. Safety is expected to be similar between ASTX727 and IV decitabine based on equivalent decitabine systemic exposure and similar biological effect on DNA methylation from the 2 treatments as assessed in the randomized comparison of PK and PD in the first 2 cycles of Phase 2 and 3. In addition, a limited randomized comparison of ASTX727 vs IV decitabine in the first 2 cycles provided a useful assessment of potential of safety issues as a result of oral decitabine administration (mainly GI effects) or for detecting safety signals that may be attributed to cedazuridine. Safety review issues were not identified during drug development and there were no clinical holds for safety during the development program.
L1668: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1669: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of median duration of exposure across Studies ASTX727-01-A, ASTX727-01-B, ASTX727-02, ASTX727-04, and E7727-01. FDA performed an updated analysis of exposure based on the data submitted with the 120-day safety update received on 4/6/2020. The 120-day safety update included updated safety data from Studies ASTX727-01-A, ASTX727-01-B, ASTX727-02, and ASTX727-04, with a cutoff date of 11/1/2019. FDA utilized the safety data from the 120-day safety update for all safety analyses.

L1674: ## Overall Exposure
L1675: ## Data:
L1676: Overall, 208 subjects were exposed to ASTX727 in the Phase 2 (78 subjects) and Phase 3 (130 subjects) studies and 69 subjects were exposed to cedazuridine and/or decitabine in the Phase 1 studies (Table 42 and Table 43). In the controlled clinical trials a total of 208 subjects were exposed to at least 1 dose of 100 mg cedazuridine plus 35 mg decitabine administered concomitantly as separate capsules or as the ASTX727 FDC tablet and a total of 159 subjects were exposed to the ASTX727 FDC tablet (Table 44).
L1677: It is important to note that the first 2 cycles of the Phase 2 and Phase 3 controlled clinical trials consisted of a 2-way crossover. Subjects received both ASTX727 and IV decitabine in these trials. A total of 207 subjects received IV decitabine (Table 48).

L1703: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1704: The number of patients exposed to the study drug in the integrated analysis (208 patients) and the total number of cycles (1342 cycles) with median of 5 cycles (range 1 to 29) is adequate for safety review. The duration of follow-up in the Phase 2 (minimum: 12 months; median: ~24 months) and Phase 3 (minimum: 2 months; median: ~5 months) studies and the extent of exposure (i.e., number of cycles) are sufficient to assess the long-term safety of ASTX727. In the Phase 2 study with the longer follow-up, dose intensity was 80% or higher in over 90% of the patients in each cycle up to cycle 12. The data from Phase 3 show lower dose intensity beyond 5 cycles, but the number of patients beyond 5 cycles are small due to the shorter follow-up.
L1705: This pooling strategy was chosen because it provides the largest dataset for safety assessment and includes subjects who received multiple cycles of the same dose of cedazuridine and decitabine, and because there were similar exposure profiles (based on decitabine AUC) and adverse event (AE) profiles in ASTX727-01 Phase 2 and ASTX727-02 Phase 3. In addition, both studies provided a safety comparison between ASTX727 and IV decitabine in the first 2 cycles of treatment. Because Study ASTX727-01 Phase 2 was started earlier, the duration of treatment and follow up (median 7 cycles and 24 months, respectively) is longer than in Study ASTX727-02 (median 4 cycles of treatment and 5 months follow up) (Table 42).
L1706: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1707: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of the pooled safety analysis set (SAS) of 208
L1708: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## NDA 212576, Original
## INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
L1709: subjects exposed to ASTX727 in the Phase 2 (n=78) and Phase 3 (n=130) studies, excluding the 5 subjects treated with IV decitabine in cycle 1 who never received ASTX727. However, given that the one cycle of IV decitabine was part of the larger treatment regimen on these studies, FDA analyzed safety during all cycles of therapy, including the IV decitabine cycles. This differed from the Applicant's analyses, where TEAEs with onset during the IV decitabine cycle were not included in the "All Cycles" columns.
L1710: Furthermore, FDA focused its review of comparative safety between ASTX727 and IV decitabine in the first cycle of therapy, so as to avoid the possibility of overlapping toxicities between the first and second cycles of therapy. In these comparative analyses, we included the 5 subjects treated with IV decitabine in cycle 1 only. A total of 106 subjects received IV decitabine during Cycle 1 and a total of 107 subjects received ASTX727 during Cycle 1.
L1711: FDA performed an analysis of exposure in the pooled SAS based on the data from the 120-day safety update.

L1730: All MDS IPSS types were represented in the study, and 15.5% of subjects had CMML. IPSS low-risk MDS was included in Study ASTX727-02 as it corresponded to eligibility by FAB disease criteria from the Dacogen USPI indication. The median time since diagnosis was 66 days (range 5 days to 15 years). Just over 20% of subjects had had prior anticancer therapy, with under 5% having had 1 previous cycle of HMA therapy. Just over 40% of subjects were dependent on RBC transfusions at baseline and just over 10% were dependent on platelet transfusions at baseline. Low hemoglobin (&lt;80 g/L) was present at baseline in 17.8% of subjects, 14.6% had low platelets (&lt;25\times 10^9/L), and 22.5% had low neutrophils (&lt;0.5\times 10^9/L). Half of the subjects had &gt;5\% blasts in baseline bone marrow.
L1731: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1732: The demographic and disease characteristics of the integrated population were representative of the general disease population for MDS of all IPSS scores and CMML.
L1733: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1734: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of baseline demographics, except for the statement that under 5% had 1 previous cycle of HMA therapy. Reviewing the data in the submission, we determined that 17 (8%) of patients in the pooled SAS received up to 1 prior cycle of HMA therapy.

L1742: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L1743: ## Data:
L1744: The safety profile of cedazuridine has been evaluated in 208 subjects treated in the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies and in 69 subjects treated in the Phase 1 studies (Table 48). The integrated population included 208 subjects with MDS or CMMI who were exposed to at least 1 dose of 100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine administered concomitantly as separate capsules or as the ASTX727 FDC tablet, and 159 subjects were exposed to at least 1 dose of the ASTX727 FDC tablet.
L1745: A summary of the duration of treatment and follow-up by study is provided in Table 42 and the
L1746: duration of treatment for the integrated population is provided in Table 44. Treated subjects in the Phase 1 and Phase 2 portions of ASTX727-01 were followed for a minimum of 18 months and 12 months, respectively, with a median follow-up duration of approximately 24 months in both Phase 1 and Phase 2. The Phase 3 study had a shorter duration of follow-up (minimum: 2 months; median: approximately 5 months), in order to assess the primary endpoint of decitabine area under the plasma concentration-time curve (AUC) equivalence after all treated subjects completed assessments in Cycles 1 and 2.

L1767: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L1768: The Applicant's Position:
The definitions for AEs and SAEs are provided in the individual study protocols. Disease progression was not considered to be an AE or serious adverse event (SAE).
L1769: - AEs that occurred during screening (prior to dosing) were recorded. Any new AEs from the start of study treatment to the earliest of the following were recorded: Until 30 days after the last dose of study treatment or
- Until the subject starts new MDS/CMML treatment (including new investigational treatment)
L1770: AEs were collected spontaneously and were elicited at scheduled visits using an open-ended non-leading question such as "Have you had any new symptoms, injuries, illnesses since your last visit?".
L1771: AEs and SAEs that occurred during the protocol-specified reporting period were followed until 1.) resolution, 2.) the investigator assesses the subject as stable and the event is following a clinically expected outcome, or 3.) the subject is lost to follow-up or withdraws consent.
L1772: Verbatim descriptions of all AEs were mapped to MedDRA thesaurus terms for all studies in the individual CSRs. For the integrated analyses, AEs from Phase 2 Study ASTX727-01 were re-mapped using MedDRA version 22.0 (up-versioned from the versions used in the CSR) to be
L1773: NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
L1774: consistent with the version used for coding AEs in Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02. Verbatim terms as well as the preferred terms are provided in by-subject listings of Adverse Events.
L1775: Adverse event severity was graded using NCI Common Toxicity Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) version 4.0.3 for Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02. For Study ASTX727-01, CTCAE version 4.0 was used to grade AE severity. No re-grading of AE severity was performed.
L1776: All AEs summarized in the submission and summarized below are TEAEs. A treatment-emergent adverse event (TEAE) was defined as any new AE or any worsening of an existing condition with an onset date on or after the first study drug administration date (C1D1) and up to 30 days after the last dose of study treatment or the start of an alternative anticancer treatment (for MDS/CMML or subsequent leukemia), whichever occurred first. Events that occurred more than 30 days after the last dose of study treatment or the start of an alternative anticancer treatment (for MDS/CMML or subsequent leukemia) that were both serious and related to the study treatment were also considered treatment emergent. AEs with unknown onset date were considered to be treatment-emergent unless the stop date/time was known to be before first administration of the study drug (C1D1).
L1777: All AE data collected in the clinical study database with an AE start date on or before the CSR data cutoff dates were included in the data listings. However, only TEAEs are included in the AE summary tables. The number and percentage of subjects who had AEs are summarized by MedDRA system organ class (SOC) and preferred term (PT). AEs were not summarized based on per patient-year).
L1778: Summary tables that provide the causality of AEs are based on the investigator's assessment of relationship to study drug.
L1779: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1780: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. FDA evaluated the mapping of verbatim AE terms to MedDRA terms and is in agreement with the mapping.
L1781: We created a grouped terms dictionary based on common related terms (see Appendix 19.5). The grouped terms are included in safety analyses throughout the review and will be indicated with a "*" in safety tables and the text of the review.
L1782: ## **Routine Clinical Tests**
L1783: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1784: Chemistry and hematology laboratory parameters were assessed prior to each cycle (at a minimum); hematology parameters were assessed more frequently in the first 2 cycles. The schedule of assessments is provided in Table 18 for Phase 2 and in Table 31 for Phase 3.
L1785: **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.

L1810: ## Phase 1 Studies
L1811: As of the 01 June 2017 data cutoff date for the Phase 1 stage of Study ASTX727-01, 4 of 44 subjects died from AEs occurring up to 30 days from the last study treatment, all were considered unrelated to treatment by the investigator. TEAEs with an outcome of death in this study included:
L1812: - cardiac arrest (2 patients): died C7 D20; and C12 D10
- multiple organ dysfunction syndrome (died C1 D31), and sepsis (died C1 D20)
L1813: In Study ASTX727-04 (food effect), 1 of 17 treated subjects had an AE with an outcome of death as of the 08 July 2019 CSR data cutoff. TEAEs with an outcome of death in this study included:
L1814: - atrial fibrillation (died C1 D25) considered unrelated to study treatment
L1815: In the E7727-01 ADME study in healthy volunteers there were no deaths or SAEs during the study.
L1816: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1817: In general, AEs with an outcome of death were primarily infections that likely occurred as a result of prolonged neutropenia, which are typical for this patient population. A few other events, such as cardiac arrest, respiratory failure, and cancer were also observed, which are not unusual given the age of the subjects in the study population. Fatal AEs reported in &gt;1 subject were sepsis and septic shock. In Cycles 1 and 2, there was no difference in the incidence of fatal AEs between treatments. In the IV decitabine 5-day published study (Steensma et al 2009), 11 patients of 99 treated died within 30 days of study treatment (11%). The 6.7% deaths within 30 days of treatment in the pooled safety data set is consistent with data from IV decitabine.
L1818: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1819: Using the data from the 120-day safety update, FDA identified a total of 94 deaths among 277
L1820: subjects exposed to ASTX727 in the ISS dataset listed in the Table below. Most deaths occurred more than 30 days after the last dose of ASTX727 (73 subjects, 26%), leaving 21 (8%) on-treatment deaths.

L1842: As per the Table above, two cases that the Applicant designated as due to adverse reaction (pneumonia) (n=1) and unknown (n=1) were deemed related to primary disease by FDA. These case narratives are described in detail below.
L1843: **Subject** (b) (6): This was an 86-year-old man with CMML enrolled on the Phase 2 trial in Stage 2, Sequence A. On Study day 57, 24 days after the last dose of IV decitabine, the patient presented to the hospital for bilateral pneumonia. The patient had pneumonia prior to starting on the study, as well. He was found to have leukocytosis of 139.9 and was started on antibiotics. He clinically deteriorated and was transferred to the intensive care unit for respiratory failure and hyperleukocytosis and was intubated. Acute kidney injury, tumor lysis syndrome, and severe coagulopathy were also noted. White blood cell count further increased to 225.7. The patient died on Study day 57. The investigator assessed the event of bilateral pneumonia as Grade 5 and not related. The Applicant concurred but noted that the cause of death was the AE in the NDA dataset. FDA deemed the root cause of death to be the underlying disease, as evidenced by the hyperleukocytosis and tumor lysis syndrome.
L1844: **Subject** (b) (6): This was a 66-year-old man with CMML enrolled on the Phase 2 trial in Stage 3 Sequence B. He was hospitalized on study day 264, 5 days after the last dose, with an unrelated SAE of abdominal pain. On day 267, 12 days after the last dose, while still hospitalized, bone marrow showed progression of CMML. He was transitioned to comfort care and remained in the hospital for pain management. On Day 274, the patient died, with an immediate cause of death reported as disease progression. Increasing peripheral blasts and pancytopenia may have contributed. The Applicant listed the cause of death as unknown, but given the report of suspected disease progression in the narrative along with increasing peripheral blasts, FDA considered this death to be related to the primary disease.
L1845: Lastly, FDA reviewed the two deaths that occurred in patients treated with IV decitabine during Cycle 1 (Subjects (b) (6) and (b) (6)) who never received ASTX727. The first patient died of an adverse reaction of septic shock on Day 28, 23 days after the last dose of IV decitabine and the second patient died of an adverse reaction of pneumonia on Day 48, 43 days after the last dose of IV decitabine.
L1846: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1847: ## Data:
L1848: The most common non-fatal SAEs in the integrated population across all cycles were febrile neutropenia, pneumonia, and sepsis (Table 52).
L1849: In Cycles 1 and 2, the incidence of non-fatal SAEs was higher in the ASTX727 cycle (31.7% for the overall pooled ASTX727 population) compared to IV decitabine cycle (21.3%). There was no single AE responsible for this difference; however, small differences in the AEs of febrile neutropenia (11.5% for overall ASTX727, 7.7% for IV decitabine), pneumonia (5.8% for overall ASTX727, 3.9% for IV decitabine), and sepsis (3.4% for overall ASTX727, 1.0% for IV decitabine) were observed. All 3 AE terms are associated with one another, since pneumonia and sepsis are common in subjects with febrile neutropenia. In addition, the incidence of SAEs of pneumonia and sepsis on ASTX727 during Cycles \ge 3 decreased compared with the incidence on ASTX727 in Cycle 1 or 2, to a comparable incidence with IV decitabine (3.8% for SAE of pneumonia in ASTX727 Cycles \ge 3 vs 3.9% for IV decitabine; 2.7% for SAE of sepsis in ASTX727 Cycles \ge 3 vs 1% for IV decitabine).

L1855: Source: ISS Table 5.5.1
L1856: Due to the crossover design of the Phase 2 and 3 studies, there was a potential for AEs that began and were reported in Cycle 2 to be a consequence of Cycle 1 treatment. This made the Cycle 1 comparison more reliable as it is not affected by prior treatment. Therefore, an analysis was performed to summarize SAEs by treatment for Cycle 1 and Cycle 2 separately (Table 53). This allowed the assessment of AEs for Cycle 1, which was the principal randomized comparison before the crossover, and the potential for carryover effects. The data suggest that the overall higher incidence of SAEs reported in Cycles 1 or 2 for ASTX727 compared with IV decitabine was mainly driven by a difference in the incidence of SAEs in Cycle 2 (35.6% for ASTX727, 19.8% for IV decitabine) with a much smaller difference in SAE incidence in Cycle 1 (29.9% for ASTX727, 23.6% for IV decitabine). The small difference in SAE incidence in Cycle 1 may simply reflect small differences in the population of subjects randomized to Sequence A (ASTX727 in Cycle 1) compared with Sequence B (IV decitabine in Cycle 1). In Study ASTX727-02, subjects randomized to ASTX727 in Cycle 1 (Sequence A) had a higher proportion with high risk MDS and higher proportion with low baseline neutrophil count (&lt;1000 µL) compared to Sequence B (Table 35).

L1865: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1866: Non-fatal SAEs from oral ASTX727 are mainly related to myelosuppression and infections related to neutropenia, which is consistent with IV decitabine data from Steensma et al 2009 and Dacogen USPI 2019, and also consistent with expected complications of the disease itself. The incidence was higher in ASTX727 Cycle 1 or 2 vs IV decitabine Cycles 1 or 2 in some of those events such as febrile neutropenia, pneumonia, and sepsis (Table 52). However, the difference was small and mostly in Cycle 2 for febrile neutropenia (Table 53). The differences were mainly driven by Phase 3 population and may be related to lower baseline neutrophil count and higher % of high-risk MDS in Sequence A (ASTX727 in Cycle 1) compared to Sequence B (IV decitabine in Cycle 1) in the Phase 3 population. In Phase 2, the 2 most common SAEs of febrile neutropenia and pneumonia occurred at similar or lower incidence in ASTX727 compared to IV decitabine (Table 54). Only sepsis SAEs occurred at higher incidence in both Phase 2 and Phase 3.
L1867: The most common SAEs (febrile neutropenia, pneumonia, and sepsis) were identical in both Phase 2 and Phase 3 and highly consistent with both the expected complications of the disease population and the mechanism of action of decitabine. Myelosuppression and infections are common complications in MDS and CMML and are also expected pharmacological effects from decitabine. Since ASTX727 achieves equivalent systemic exposure and the same DNA demethylation effect compared to IV decitabine, it is unlikely that the incidence of these events are substantially different between ASTX727 and IV decitabine.
L1868: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1869: FDA performed an analysis of all treatment-emergent SAEs (TESAEs) and TESAEs in the first cycle of therapy between ASTX727 and IV decitabine based on the data from the 120-day safety update.
L1870: A TESAE occurring within 30 days after the last dose of ASTX727 or IV decitabine in the pooled SAS (N=208) was reported for 142 (68%) of patients. Considering the first Cycle of therapy, TESAEs occurred in 32 (30%) of patients treated with ASTX727 and 25 (24%) patients treated
L1871: with IV decitabine. The most frequently occurring (\ge 2\%) TESAEs are presented in the Table below.

L1876: SAEs were slightly lower in patients treated with IV decitabine during Cycle 1 for unclear reasons. It is possible that variations in baseline characteristics may have contributed. Sepsis was slightly more common in patients treated with ASTX727 during Cycle 1, while febrile
L1877: neutropenia was slightly less common. The numbers are sufficiently low that firm conclusions cannot be made. Overall, the SAEs observed are in line with the known safety profile of IV decitabine.
L1878: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L1879: ## **Data:**
L1880: Overall, the incidence of AEs leading to discontinuation of treatment in the integrated population was low (3.8%). During Cycles 1 and 2, two subjects each permanently discontinued treatment with ASTX727 or IV decitabine due to an AE. Across all cycles, the only AE leading to treatment discontinuation of ASTX727 that was reported in &gt;1 subject was febrile neutropenia.
L1881: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1882: Discontinuation of treatment due to AEs in ASTX727 is low and do not represent an impediment to treatment compliance.
L1883: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L1884: FDA performed an analysis of all TEAEs leading to withdrawal of study therapy in the pooled SAS and by treatment arm during Cycle 1 using the data from the 120-day safety update.
L1885: TEAEs leading to withdrawal occurred in 11 (5%) of patients, including 3 (3%) of patients treated with ASTX727 and 1 (1%) of patients treated with IV decitabine during Cycle 1. The only TEAEs leading to discontinuation in more than one subject were febrile neutropenia (n=2) and pneumonia (n=2). Overall, permanent discontinuation due to adverse reactions was quite low.
L1886: ## Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects
L1887: ## **Data:**
L1888: The most common AEs that led to drug interruption or dose reduction of ASTX727 were expected myelosuppression events, including neutropenia, febrile neutropenia, and thrombocytopenia.
L1889: During IV decitabine treatment, there was one subject in Phase 2 who received 2 of the 5 days of IV treatment during Cycle 2; dosing was interrupted due to an AE of sepsis.

L1907: Overall, the number of treatment interruptions are as expected for the patient population. The most common interruptions were for cytopenia-related complications. Interruptions in the first treatment cycle were uncommon with both ASTX727 and IV decitabine.
L1908: ## **Dose reduction**

L1910: ## **Significant Adverse Events**
L1911: ## **Data:**
L1912: ## *Myelosuppression*
L1913: The most common Grade \ge 3 AEs across all cycles in the overall ASTX727 integrated population (N=208) were the expected myelosuppression-related events that could be related to either disease or treatment with ASTX727, including thrombocytopenia (41.8%), neutropenia (42.3%), anemia (28.4%), febrile neutropenia (23.6%), and leukopenia (20.7%) (Table 59). In Cycles 1 or 2 the incidence was generally similar between IV decitabine and ASTX727 (Table 59 and Table 60).
L1914: ## *Infections*
L1915: The most common Grade \ge 3 infection AEs in the integrated population (N=208) was pneumonia (10.1%) and sepsis (7.7%) for all ASTX727 cycles (Table 59). The incidence of pneumonia was slightly higher for ASTX727 than IV decitabine in Cycle 1 but not in Cycle 2 (Table 60). Grade \ge 3 sepsis was higher in ASTX727 Cycles 1 or 2, which is consistent with data from SAEs.
L1916: ## Phase 1
L1917: In Study ASTX727-01 Phase 1 (N=44), Grade \ge 3 myelosuppression AEs included thrombocytopenia (40.9%), neutropenia (29.5%), anemia (25.0%), febrile neutropenia (15.9%), and leukopenia (15.9%). Most common Grade \ge 3 infection AEs in Study ASTX727-01 Phase 1 included pneumonia (15.9%), and sepsis (6.8%).
L1918: In Study ASTX727-04 (N=17), Grade \ge 3 myelosuppression AEs included neutropenia (58.8%), thrombocytopenia (35.3%), anemia (29.4%), leukopenia (29.4%), and febrile neutropenia (11.8%). There was 1 patient in this study with Grade \ge 3 sepsis but no cases of Grade \ge 3 pneumonia.
L1919: In Study E7727-01 (N=8), there were no myelosuppression AEs.
L1920: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1921: The significant AEs of Grade \ge 3 were the expected events of myelosuppression and infections. They were consistent in all studies and as expected from the disease patient population and the pharmacological effect of decitabine. The incidences were generally similar between IV decitabine and ASTX727 in Cycles 1 or 2. A slightly higher incidence on ASTX727 mainly in cycle 1 was observed in some events, which may have also been related to the imbalance between Sequence A and B in Phase 3. As discussed above patients randomized to Sequence A (ASTX727 vs IV decitabine in Cycle 1 in Phase 3) had worse baseline characteristics than Sequence B (higher % of high risk MDS, lower % of CMML, and higher % of low baseline neutrophils &lt;1000/\mu\text{L}) (See Table 35 of patients characteristics in Phase 3). Many cases with sepsis occurred in patients with Grade 3 or 4 neutropenia at baseline (Appendix 19.6, Table 86).
L1922: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1923: FDA performed an analysis of AESIs of cytopenias, infections, and hemorrhage in the pooled SAS, including the comparison of ASTX727 and IV decitabine during Cycle 1 using the data from
L1924: the 120-day safety update (see Table below).

L1927: FDA analysis
L1928: *Grouped term (see Appendix 19.5)
L1929: 1Cytopenias: defined as hematopoietic cytopenias broad SMQ term
L1930: 2Infections: defined as the infections and infestations SOC
L1931: 3Hemorrhage: defined as the haemorrhage terms (excl laboratory terms) narrow SMQ
L1932: To put things in perspective with these AESIs, the USPI for IV decitabine using the 15 mg/m2 q8h x 3 days every 6 weeks dosing regimen reports an incidence of neutropenia of 90%, thrombocytopenia 89%, anemia 82%, febrile neutropenia 29%, and leukopenia 28%. Results in the USPI for the 20 mg/m2 qday x 5 days every 4 weeks dosing regimen were neutropenia 38%, thrombocytopenia 27%, anemia 31%, febrile neutropenia 20%, and leukopenia 6%. However, it was noted that in this single-arm study, investigators reported AEs based on clinical signs and symptoms rather than predefined laboratory abnormalities. Given that laboratory AEs often under-report laboratory abnormalities, it is difficult to make the comparison across trials. However, the cytopenias seen with ASTX727 are roughly the same (in terms of febrile neutropenia and leukopenia) or less (neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, anemia) than what was observed with the 15 mg/m2 regimen of decitabine.
L1933: For the 3-day and 5-day regimens, the IV decitabine USPI describes pneumonia in 22% and 20% of patients and cellulitis in 12% and 9%, respectively. With the 3-day regimen, 7% had Staphylococcal infection and 5% had bacteremia and with the 5-day regimen, 8% had Staphylococcal bacteremia. These infectious AEs are of similar incidence to what is reported here with ASTX727. In terms of hemorrhage, the IV decitabine USPI notes petechiae in 39%, ecchymosis in 22%, and hematoma in 5% for the 3-day regimen and epistaxis in 13%, petechiae in 12%, contusion in 9%, and ecchymosis in 9% for the 5-day regimen. Therefore, the numbers
L1934: for hemorrhage also appear similar to what is reported here for ASTX727.
L1935: Overall, the AESIs appear to be more common in patients treated with ASTX727 compared to IV decitabine in the first cycle of therapy. It is possible, again, that this may have been due to imbalances in baseline demographics. It will be important for clinicians to closely monitor patients while on this oral form of decitabine given the possibility for significant cytopenias, infection, and hemorrhage. Despite this being an oral therapy that will allow patients to spend more time at home and away from the clinic, it will be important to have close follow-up for laboratory testing and clinical monitoring.
L1936: ## Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions
L1937: ## Data:
L1938: ## *Common AEs (All Grades)*
L1939: The most common AEs across all cycles in the overall ASTX727 integrated population were consistent with the known AE profile of decitabine and included thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, anemia, fatigue, nausea, constipation, leukopenia, febrile neutropenia, diarrhea, and dyspnea (Table 57). The incidence of these events was similar during randomized treatment with ASTX727 and IV decitabine in Cycles 1 and 2. There were also no clinically notable differences in the proportion of subjects with AEs in the different System Organ Classes (SOCs), with the exception of the Infections and Infestations SOC. However, the most common AE in this SOC (pneumonia) occurred at a similar incidence for ASTX727 and IV decitabine.

L1950: ## Grade ≥3 AEs
L1951: In Cycles 1 and 2, the overall incidence of Grade ≥3 AEs was generally similar in the ASTX727 cycle and the IV decitabine cycle in Cycles 1 or 2 (Table 59). This was also generally true for all SOCs with the exception of Infections SOC, but no large differences in the incidence of individual infection events, with the exception of sepsis (1.0% IV decitabine, 3.8% ASTX727). Of note is that GI AEs of Grade ≥3 were &lt;2% and therefore not represented in the table. The most common myelosuppression AEs Grade ≥3 tended to be higher on ASTX727 in Cycle 1 (thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, and anemia), but higher on IV decitabine in Cycle 2 (Table 60). However, febrile neutropenia Grade ≥3 occurred at a higher incidence on IV decitabine in Cycle 1 and higher on ASTX727 in Cycle 2 (Table 60).

L1969: Common all grades AEs in supporting study ASTX727-04 (food effect) included neutropenia (58.8%), anemia (41.2%), thrombocytopenia (35.3%), fatigue (29.4%), leukopenia (29.4%), nausea (29.4%), and constipation (23.5%).
L1970: Grade ≥3 AEs in supporting studies ASTX727-01 Phase 1 and ASTX727-04 were generally similar to those in the integrated population. Overall (N=44) the most common Grade ≥3 AEs in ASTX727-01 Phase 1 included thrombocytopenia (40.9%), neutropenia (29.5%), and anemia (25.0%). In ASTX727-04 (food effect), the most common Grade ≥3 AEs (N=17) were neutropenia (58.8%), thrombocytopenia (35.3%), anemia (29.4%), and leukopenia (29.4%).
L1971: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1972: The common all grade AEs and Grade ≥3 AEs were consistent with the expected AEs from IV decitabine and are the common complications of the disease population. The overall incidence of all grade AEs and Grade ≥3 AEs was generally similar between IV decitabine and ASTX727 in Cycles 1 or 2 with few exceptions.
L1973: GI AEs either as a system organ class or individually were very similar between IV decitabine and ASTX727 in the first 2 randomized cycles and the GI SOC incidence did not increase with subsequent cycles (Cycles ≥3). GI AEs of Grade ≥3 were &lt;2%.
L1974: In Phase 1, there was no obvious difference in AEs particularly mechanism related myelosuppression between the lowest dose cohort 1 in Phase 1 (cedazuridine 40 mg/ decitabine 20 mg) and the highest dose cohort 4 in Phase 1 (cedazuridine 100 mg/ decitabine 40 mg) suggesting a broad therapeutic index across all doses tested.
L1975: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L1976: FDA performed an analysis TEAEs based on the data from the 120-day safety update, focusing
L1977: on the pooled SAS and the comparative safety data between ASTX727 and IV decitabine in Cycle 1 of the pooled Phase 2 and Phase 3 trials.
L1978: A total of 208/208 (100%) of patients experienced a TEAE in the pooled SAS. In Cycle 1, 106/107 (99%) treated with ASTX727 and 105/106 (99%) treated with IV decitabine experienced a TEAE. A total of 195/208 (94%) experienced a grade 3 or higher TEAE, including 79/107 (74%) treated with ASTX727 in Cycle 1 and 76/106 (72%) treated with IV decitabine in Cycle 1.
L1979: TEAEs occurring either on study treatment or within 30 days after discontinuation of study treatment in the pooled SAS and in the comparative safety population between ASTX727 and IV decitabine during Cycle 1 are summarized by PT/grouped term in the Table below. TEAEs occurring in at least 10% of patients are included in the Table.

L1986: FDA analysis
L1987: *Grouped term (see Appendix 19.5)
L1988: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment that the common all-grade and Grade \ge 3 TEAEs are consistent with the expected AEs from IV decitabine and are common complications in the disease population. TEAEs \ge 5\% more common with ASTX727 in the first cycle of therapy included thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, nausea, dyspnea, diarrhea, dizziness, headache, leukopenia, transaminase increased, contusion, and hypoalbuminemia. TEAEs \ge 5\% more common with IV decitabine in the first cycle included arthralgias, myalgias, mucositis, and hypokalemia. Again, the increased cytopenias seen with ASTX727 during Cycle 1 may have been due to some baseline imbalances between the two treatment sequences. The increased nausea, diarrhea, and transaminase elevation with ASTX727 could be associated with the oral administration. However, almost all of the gastrointestinal toxicity was Grade 1-2 in severity.
L1989: FDA conducted an analysis of cases of acute febrile neutrophilic dermatosis (AFND, also known as Sweet's syndrome) given that this TEAE has been reported in the postmarketing setting for Dacogen. There were two cases of Grade 2 AFND noted in the submission – one in an 80-year-old man with MDS on the Phase 2 trial on Cycle 2, Day 24 of ASTX727 (Subject (b) (6)) and one in a 66-year-old man with MDS on the Phase 3 trial on Cycle 2, Day 1 of IV decitabine (Subject (b) (6)). Therefore, FDA agrees with the Applicant to include this adverse reaction in Section 6 of labeling.
L1990: Lastly, FDA evaluated TEAEs on the healthy volunteer Study E7727-01. Of the 8 healthy volunteers enrolled and treated with two single doses of cedazuridine 100 mg separated by a washout, 5 (63%) experienced a TEAE. Two (25%) experienced nasopharyngitis, and one each experienced anal hemorrhage, back pain, diarrhea, and headache. All were of Grade 1 severity, except for back pain, which was Grade 2.
L1991: ## Laboratory Findings
L1992: Data:
L1993: In both Study ASTX727-01 Phase 2 and Study ASTX727-02 Phase 3, hematology was evaluated on Days 1 (Screening and/or Day 1 of Cycle 1), 8, 15 and 22 for the first 2 treatment cycles; and at least on Day 1 of all following treatment cycles; and at study treatment discontinuation. Baseline values of neutrophils, hemoglobin, and platelets were defined as the average of all values obtained during screening up to and including the first dosing date. If there was only one value available before treatment, it was used as the baseline value. In both studies, serum chemistry was evaluated at Screening (for Cycle 1 treatment), then predose on Day 1 of Cycles \ge 2, and at treatment discontinuation.
L1994: There were no clinically notable trends over time in central tendency for alanine aminotransferase (ALT), aspartate aminotransferase (AST), serum creatinine, bilirubin, alkaline phosphatase, or electrolytes (potassium, sodium, and magnesium).
L1995: Analysis of central tendency for selected hematology parameters (Figure 22 through Figure 24) showed a decrease in mean neutrophils over the first 2 cycles followed by an increase, an increase in mean platelets over the first 3 cycles followed by stable levels, and an increase in mean hemoglobin over the first 8 cycles.

L2006: The incidence of Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) Grade\ge3 laboratory abnormalities for renal and hepatic function parameters was low, typically occurring in 1-3 subjects (&lt;2%). Shifts of 2 or more CTCAE grades for renal and hepatic function parameters were infrequent during treatment. Only 2 subjects had Grade 4 abnormalities (increased ALT and AST), both of which occurred in the setting of fatal AEs.
L2007: Laboratory data were also evaluated for potential Hy's Law cases. Three subjects met the Hy's Law laboratory criteria, but all 3 cases also occurred in the setting of fatal AEs of myocarditis or sepsis. Narratives for each of these subjects were provided in the submission.

L2015: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2016: No clinically meaningful trends in laboratory values were observed other than the trends in hematology parameters as a result of the effects of continued treatment and also response to treatment (increased platelets and hemoglobin). Laboratory abnormalities of any grade occurred in both IV and oral cycles and in all ASTX727 cycles with no clinically meaningful trend as incidence was somewhat higher on ASTX727 cycles for some laboratory abnormalities (decrease albumin and sodium, increase glucose and alkaline phosphatase) but somewhat higher on IV decitabine for others (bilirubin). Grade \ge 3 abnormalities \ge 5\% were all hematological with the exception of increase glucose in all ASTX727 cycles which was not present in the first 2 cycles for ASTX727. This is probably reflective of the elderly disease population and underlying comorbidities. The hematological laboratory abnormalities of Grade \ge 3 were generally similar for IV and ASTX727 in Cycles 1 and 2.
L2017: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L2018: The Applicant's analyses of platelets, hemoglobin, and neutrophils over time indicates that treatment with ASTX727 improved hematologic parameters with continued treatment. Of course, this assessment is somewhat biased, as patients on continued therapy are more likely to have responded to the therapy. However, the data is in-line with the demonstrated transfusion benefit of ASTX727 in a subset of patients (see Sections 8.1.2 and 8.1.4).

L2024: *The denominator used to calculate the rate varied from 103 to 107 for ASTX727 Cycle 1, from 102 to 106 for IV decitabine Cycle, and from 203 to 208 for All Cycles based on the number of patients with a baseline value and at least one post-treatment value.
L2025: As expected, the predominate laboratory abnormalities were cytopenias. Again, the Grade 3-4 cytopenias were slightly more pronounced during Cycle 1 with ASTX727 compared to IV decitabine, with the exception of platelet count decreased. The fact that the opposite was true for the AE of thrombocytopenia (i.e. Grade 3-4 thrombocytopenia appeared worse for ASTX727 during cycle 1), and that the incidences of cytopenias were considerably lower using AE data, illustrates that the AE data incompletely captures the incidence of cytopenias compared to the more accurate and complete laboratory results. Therefore, cytopenias will be indicated in labeling with the laboratory data.
L2026: A total of 3 patients (1%) in the pooled SAS experienced ALT and/or AST values &gt; 3 \times \text{ULN} combined with a total bilirubin value \ge 2 \times \text{ULN} and alkaline phosphatase &lt; 2 \times \text{ULN}. FDA reviewed narratives for all of the potential Hy's law cases and ruled out drug-induced liver injury based on plausible alternative causes, including sepsis (n=2) and myocarditis (n=1).
L2027: ## Vital Signs
L2028: ## Data:
L2029: In Study ASTX727-01, assessment of vital signs was performed at Screening, before dosing on Day 1 of all treatment cycles, and at study treatment discontinuation. In Study ASTX727-02, assessment of vital signs was performed at Screening, before dosing on all dosing days (Days 1-5), on Days 8, 15 and 22 of the first 2 treatment cycles, on Day 1 of all following treatment cycles, and at study treatment discontinuation. Vital signs were summarized for the integrated population by visit using proportion of subjects with each vital sign being above or below conventionally accepted vital sign normal ranges. In addition, for each vital sign parameter, a figure of mean values by visit was provided.
L2030: There were no clinically notable trends in the central tendency for vital signs parameters over time, including heart rate, diastolic blood pressure, systolic blood pressure, respiration rate, or temperature.
L2031: The most frequently occurring post-baseline vital signs finding outside the normal limits was respiration rate, which was \ge 20 breaths per minute on one or more occasions for approximately 75% of subjects, and diastolic blood pressure, which was \le 55 mmHg on one or more occasions for approximately 50% of subjects.
L2032: The most common vital signs AEs were dyspnea and pyrexia (20.7% and 11.5%, respectively, across all cycles in the overall ASTX727 population), which were likely associated with the occurrence of pneumonia and other infections.
L2033: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2034: The vital signs findings are not unexpected in a relatively sick population with myelosuppression who are receiving treatment for infections.
L2035: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L2036: FDA agrees with the Sponsor's assessment.

L2044: (b) (4)
L2045: ## QT
L2046: ## Data:
L2047: A dedicated thorough QT study to evaluate the QT prolongation potential of ASTX727 has not been conducted. Analyses of QTc from the pooled population and the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies individual are presented in Table 65.
L2048: The pooled population (N=208) in Table 65 below includes QTcF interval data from both the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies. It is important to note that ECGs were collected at different time points in the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies. In the Phase 2 study ECGs were collected at Screening and predose on Day 1 of each cycle (i.e., more than 3 weeks after last dose), and at the Treatment Termination Visit. In the Phase 3 study, ECGs were collected at Screening and on Day 3 (predose and 3 hours after ASTX727 and within 10 min before end of IV infusion for decitabine) of Cycle 1 and 2. Due to the differences in ECG collection times between the studies, an analysis of QTcF was also provided below for each study individually.
L2049: Maximum post-baseline absolute QT findings in Phase 2 and 3 presented in Table 65 probably reflect the background noise of the disease population (elderly age, comorbidities, concomitant medication) rather than a treatment effect as they do not indicate which cycle or day of cycle they were recorded. Table 66 shows adverse events of QT prolonged in Phase 2. All patients, except 2 had the QTc increase on Day &gt;15 from start of cycle and almost all continued treatment with no recurrence of events supporting that it is not related to drug exposure. The two patients who had the event recorded on Day 1 of a cycle received multiple cycles (12 and 16) and the event has not been recorded in other cycles.
L2050: A summary of the QTcF analysis from the Phase 3 study is provided below in Table 67. This table shows the subjects with a new QTcF outlier value above 480 msec and above 500 msec and the maximum post-baseline increase in QTcF done at the time of maximum drug exposure on Day 3 of Cycles 1 and 2.

L2061: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2062: (b) (4)
L2063: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L2064: As of the 120-day safety update, the incidence of all-grade PTs in the broad SMQ for Torsade de pointes/QT prolongation was 16/208 (8%). This included 7 (3%) patients with electrocardiogram QT prolonged, 7 (3%) with syncope, and one patient each with cardiac arrest, multiple organ dysfunction syndrome, and sudden death. Of note, there were no Grade 3 or higher TEAEs of electrocardiogram QT prolonged. No further ECG data were available from the 120-day safety update.
L2065: Overall, the data presented are not adequate to determine whether there is an effect of ASTX727 on post-baseline QTcF. Given the similar exposures to IV decitabine, the decitabine component is unlikely to contribute to increases in QTcF, but there are no clinical data to conclude that cedazuridine does not have an independent effect on QTcF. A PMR study will be requested to evaluate the QT prolongation potential of cedazuridine.

L2067: ## **Data:**

L2069: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2070: No immunogenic reactions are expected with decitabine and were not observed with ASTX727.
L2071: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L2072: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.

L2087: ## 8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L2088: ## Data:
L2089: Adverse events were analyzed by subgroups for a variety of intrinsic (age, sex, race, renal impairment, hepatic impairment, BSA, study disease) and extrinsic factors (country) in the integrated population.
L2090: ## Age
L2091: There was no consistent and clinically notable pattern of increasing AE incidence either overall or in the different SOCs from between the younger age group (&lt;65 years) to the oldest age group (\ge75 years) particularly in the SOCs of interest such as Blood and Lymphatic System Disorders and Infections and Infestations (Table 68).
L2092: Individual AEs that were notably higher (more than double) in the oldest age group (\ge75 years) compared to the youngest age group (&lt;65 years) included:
L2093: - sepsis (12.2% vs 3.8%)
- contusion (14.9% vs 3.8%), which were reported as bruises, some of which may have been due to multiple venipuncture for PK draws
- blood creatinine increase (12.2% vs 3.8%)
L2094: Individual AEs that were notably higher (more than double) in the youngest age group (&lt;65 years) compared to the oldest group (\ge75 years) included:
L2095: - nausea (48.1% vs 17.6%)
- vomiting (19.2% vs 5.4%)
- headache (26.9% vs 10.8%)
- insomnia (13.5% vs 5.4%)
- oropharyngeal pain (15.4% vs 4.1%)

L2101: ## **Sex:**
L2102: There was no overall difference in the incidence of AEs based on sex but for most SOCs the incidence of AEs was slightly higher in females than males (Table 69).
L2103: Individual AEs that were notably higher (more than double) in females compared with males:
L2104: - vomiting (14.1% vs 6.3%)
- urinary tract infection (15.6% vs 1.4%)
- hypokalemia (15.6% vs 5.6%)
- headache (26.6% vs 12.5%)
- insomnia (14.1% vs 6.3%)
- myalgia (10.9% vs 4.9%)

L2115: Source: ISS Table 5.1.2.2
L2116: ## ***Body Surface Area:***
L2117: In addition, since ASTX727 contains a fixed dose of decitabine (35 mg), it is important to note that the safety analysis by BSA quartiles showed no consistent trend of higher AEs for ASTX727 in subjects in the smallest BSA quartile compared with BSA-based dosing of IV decitabine. While subjects in the smallest BSA quartile had a higher incidence of certain AEs with ASTX727 in Cycles 1 or 2, the results were similar to IV decitabine, indicating that the higher AE incidence is probably related to other intrinsic baseline patients characteristics factors in that cohort compared with subjects in the larger BSA quartiles. Subjects in the smallest BSA quartile had lower baseline neutrophils and a higher incidence of transfusion dependence at baseline.
L2118: ## ***Other***
L2119: There was no consistent clinically notable pattern of a higher incidence of AEs in one race group versus the other (White vs non-White).
L2120: There were no notable safety differences in subjects with mild to moderate renal or hepatic impairment. Anemia and febrile neutropenia tended to have similar or lower incidence in the moderately impaired versus normal subjects while neutropenia, leukopenia, and thrombocytopenia tended to be higher in the moderately impaired compared to normal subjects. No clinically notable increase in AEs in other SOCs was observed in hepatically impaired subjects compared to normal subjects. The interpretation is limited due to the small sample size of hepatically impaired subjects (N=22) and the fact that most of them had only mild impairment. There was also no notable difference in AEs between MDS and CMML patients; however, there was a general trend toward lower incidence of AEs in CMML subjects compared to MDS subjects in most SOCs.
L2121: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2122: The subgroup analyses of safety data did not show marked or notable differences in the safety profile of ASTX727 for any individual subgroup.
L2123: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L2124: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.

L2141: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2142: While decitabine has carcinogenic potential, the reported neoplasms in the study were consistent with the elderly disease population including non-malignant neoplasms.
L2143: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L2144: In addition, FDA performed an analysis of secondary malignancies using data from the 120-day safety update. In the pooled SAS, there were 15/208 (7%) patients with a TEAE in the
L2145: Neoplasms benign, malignant and unspecified (incl cysts and polyps) SOC. This included 2 (1%) with squamous cell carcinoma, 2 (1%) with squamous cell carcinoma of skin, 2 (1%) with basal cell carcinoma, 2 (1%) with seborrheic keratosis, and one each of adenocarcinoma gastric, hypergammaglobulinaemia benign monoclonal, melanocytic naevus, pancreatic carcinoma metastatic, refractory cytopenia with unilineage dysplasia, small cell lung cancer, and squamous cell carcinoma of the tongue. The median time to first event was 148.5 days (range 12-1285) days. Given the short median time to onset, these malignancies are less likely to be related to treatment with ASTX727.
L2146: Excluding the cases of seborrheic keratosis, hypergammaglobulinaemia benign monoclonal, melanocytic naevus, and refractory cytopenia with unilineage dysplasia (the disease under study), the incidence of second malignancies was 10 (5%). Two patients with second malignancies of basal and squamous cell carcinoma had a prior history of skin cancer (basal carcinoma) and had relatively short drug exposure at the time of the event (Cycle 4 and Cycle 7). Another 4 patients had their second malignancy after &lt; 6 cycles of decitabine (Cycles 2, 3, 4, and 5). Therefore, the second malignancies observed are most likely unrelated to ASTX727.
L2147: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L2148: ## Data:
L2149: No pregnancies were reported in the clinical trials.
L2150: Based on its mechanism of action and findings in animals, decitabine (one of the active ingredients in the ASTX727 FDC tablet) can cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman. Decitabine alters DNA synthesis and is expected to result in adverse reproductive effects. In nonclinical studies with decitabine in mice and rats, decitabine was teratogenic, fetotoxic, and embryotoxic (Dacogen USPI 2019).
L2151: Due to the known adverse reproductive effects of decitabine, no reproductive or developmental toxicity studies have been conducted with ASTX727 or cedazuridine alone.
L2152: Pregnant and breast-feeding women were excluded from enrollment in ASTX727 clinical studies. ASTX727 or its components (cedazuridine and decitabine) have not been administered orally to women who were pregnant or breast-feeding in the ASTX727 clinical program and no information is available on the use of ASTX727 in pregnant women.
L2153: There are no data on the effects of oral administration of cedazuridine or decitabine or its metabolites in human milk, the effects on the breastfed child, or on milk production.
L2154: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2155: Based on findings from human data, animal studies, and the mechanism of action for decitabine, ASTX727 may cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman.
L2156: NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
L2157: Because many drugs are excreted in human milk, and because of the potential for serious adverse reactions from ASTX727 in a nursing child, advise lactating women to avoid breastfeeding during treatment with ASTX727 and for at least 1 week after the last dose.
L2158: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L2159: Lactating women are advised to avoid breastfeeding during treatment with ASTX727 and for at least 2 weeks after the last dose, consistent with the recommendation for IV decitabine.
