L2167: ## **Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound**
L2168: ## **Data:**
L2169: There were no reports of overdose.
L2170: There is no known antidote for overdosage with ASTX727 FDC tablets. Higher doses of decitabine may be associated with increased myelosuppression, including prolonged neutropenia and thrombocytopenia.
L2171: There is no evidence that ASTX727 is habit forming or could lead to dependence.
L2172: ASTX727 can be stopped abruptly with no withdrawal effects. Potential clinical benefit may be lost if ASTX727 is stopped too early. Once treatment is stopped, blood levels of decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine-epimer should be non-detectable within 24, 72, and 72 hours, respectively.
L2173: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2174: Overdose of ASTX727 should be managed with supportive care of expected adverse events of myelosuppression and infection.
L2175: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L2176: FDA confirms the Applicant's assessment.
L2177: ## 8.2.10. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L2178: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L2179: ## Data:
L2180: ASTX727 FDC tablets are not currently marketed in any country.
L2181: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2182: Postmarketing data from IV decitabine are included in ASTX727 proposed labelling.
L2183: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L2184: The Applicant added differentiation syndrome (DS) and interstitial lung disease (ILD) to the post-marketing experience section of the proposed drug label. DS is in the USPI for IV decitabine, but ILD is not. The Sponsor responded to a labeling query on 5/13/2020 indicating that ILD is included in the Canadian PM and the EMA SmPC of Dacogen. However, given that ILD was not in the USPI for IV decitabine, the IV decitabine label was updated via a labeling supplement to include the risk of ILD. Both DS and ILD are now included in the post-marketing experience section. Sweet's syndrome (acute febrile neutrophilic dermatosis), which is related to DS, is listed in the post-marketing section for IV decitabine, but Sweet's syndrome occurred in ASTX727 clinical trials (1%), so was included in Section 6.1.
L2185: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L2186: ## Data:
L2187: The safety profile of decitabine from the approved indication and long-term use in clinical setting both in MDS and off label in other myeloid malignancies have been extensively studied and reported in the literature together with substantial postmarketing experience globally. It is unlikely that ASTX727 use will result in additional safety concerns than what is already known for decitabine, considering the equivalent systemic exposure to IV decitabine and long-term safety assessment in the application (up to 29 cycles in Phase 2 and up to 35 cycles in Phase 1).
L2188: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2189: Use of ASTX727 is not expected to result in new safety concerns that were not already described for IV decitabine and ASTX727 NDA submission.
L2190: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L2191: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment but notes that it is unknown whether cedazuridine and/or the oral route of administration may contribute to certain adverse effects of ASTX727 that may yet be unknown.
L2192: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L2193: ## Data:
L2194: Decitabine in ASTX727 has known risks of cytotoxicity, mainly myelosuppression (hematologic toxicity), and infection consequences due to neutropenia; however, these complications are also common in the disease population. In addition, ASTX727 has a fixed dose of decitabine as opposed to IV decitabine, which is dosed based on BSA. ASTX727 also contains an NCE, the CDA inhibitor cedazuridine, and is dosed orally. This could result in added risks to those already known for IV decitabine, which are discussed below:
L2195: - As expected, ASTX727 was associated with common AEs of thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, anemia, and febrile neutropenia. The overall incidence of these AEs (all grades and Grade \ge 3 AEs) was generally similar between ASTX727 and IV decitabine in Cycle 1 or 2. Infection SAEs of pneumonia and sepsis were less common in both treatments, with pneumonia occurring at a similar incidence in the ASTX727 and IV decitabine cycles but with sepsis reported at a higher incidence for ASTX727. The slightly higher incidence of SAEs of sepsis may have been more related to baseline characteristics in Cycle 1 and 2 since the incidence was generally lower with ASTX727 in Cycles \ge 3 with continued treatment where patients may have been stabilized by treatment response. There was some imbalance in baseline disease characteristics between Sequence A (ASTX727 in Cycle 1) and B (IV decitabine in Cycle 1) in Phase 3. The risks of cytopenias and associated infections/bleeding are the same risks as the target disease of MDS and CMML (Steensma 2018; Parikh and Tefferi 2012). These complications are easily monitored with blood counts and manageable with the use of appropriate standard of care therapies (e.g., growth factors and anti-infectives) by physicians experienced in the treatment of this disease. ASTX727 treatment can also be delayed and/or dose reduced to manage these complications.
- Analyses of safety data by BSA quartiles did not show any notable safety profile differences between quartiles that was not also present for IV decitabine. In addition, analyses of exposure-safety for certain hematological AEs showed that incidence of the selected AEs were similar across the full range of decitabine exposures achieved with the fixed dose treatment with ASTX727.
- Analysis of GI disorders SOC showed no differences in the overall incidence of GI AEs between ASTX727 and IV decitabine. Notably, incidence of individual GI AEs of Grade \ge 3 was &lt;2% for ASTX727 and IV decitabine. No additional risk from the oral administration of ASTX727 was identified.
- Overview of the safety profile of ASTX727 did not identify a safety risk associated with cedazuridine. The relatively short and intermittent (5 days per month) CDA inhibition only had the desired effect of enabling oral delivery of decitabine with no other safety consequences apparent from the safety data. However, other nucleosides that are also metabolized by CDA should not be co-administered on the same day as cedazuridine.

L2214: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2215: IV decitabine is already approved for the treatment of MDS and CMML with a known positive benefit-risk profile. Based on the above data, ASTX727 is expected to provide similar benefits to those achieved with IV decitabine. ASTX727 treatment involves similar risks to those known for IV decitabine. There was no notable added risk observed as a result of cedazuridine treatment or the oral administration of ASTX727. The decitabine exposures from ASTX727 fixed-dose administration were largely overlapping with those of IV decitabine in individual patients). The fixed decitabine dose in ASTX727 did not result in added risk particularly for patients with smaller body weight/body size. The range of decitabine exposures across the different body weights were also largely overlapping (Figure 8) and exposure-safety analyses showed similar AE incidence across the range of decitabine exposures). The data support the conclusion that ASTX727 (cedazuridine 100 mg and decitabine 35 mg; Daily×5) is associated with a similar positive benefit-risk to that of the approved IV decitabine dose and regimen of 20 mg/m2 Daily×5 in patients with intermediate or high-risk MDS or CMML.
L2216: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L2217: FDA performed an updated analysis of the overall safety profile of ASTX727 based on data from the 120-day safety update. The on-treatment fatal TEARs listed are as per FDA adjudication.
L2218: ## **Major safety events in the pooled SAS and in Cycle 1 between ASTX727 and IV decitabine – 120 day safety update**

L2223: 3Within 30 days following the first dose of treatment
L2224: Overall, the pooled SAS demonstrated the already known safety profile of IV decitabine. The study population was monitored for deaths, SAEs, common AEs, and common laboratory tests.
L2225: A total of 16 (8%) of patients treated with ASTX727 died during or within 30 days after study treatment. Considering only the first cycle of therapy, on-treatment deaths were similarly low (1-2%) in patients treated with ASTX727 or IV decitabine. The most frequent cause of death was primary disease. Treatment-related fatal ARs included sepsis, septic shock, pneumonia, respiratory failure, cerebral hemorrhage, and sudden death. Treatment-related fatal ARs in the first cycle were due to septic shock, pneumonia, and respiratory failure in patients treated with ASTX727 and IV decitabine.
L2226: ASTX727 was generally well-tolerated. The most common TEARs (incidence ≥ 15% and ≥ 2% more frequent with ASTX727 compared to IV decitabine) during Cycle 1 were thrombocytopenia, fatigue, nausea, dyspnea, diarrhea, dizziness, headache, and leukopenia. The most frequent Grade ≥ 3 TEARs (incidence ≥ 5% and ≥ 2% more frequent with ASTX727 compared to IV decitabine) were thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, dyspnea, leukopenia, and sepsis. The most frequent serious AE during the first cycle was febrile neutropenia, which occurred at a lower rate with ASTX727 (8%) compared to IV decitabine (12%). However, the SAR of sepsis was more common with ASTX727 (6%) compared to IV decitabine (2%) during Cycle 1.
L2227: The most common TEAR during all cycles of ASTX727 was thrombocytopenia, with 62% incidence of all-grade and 54% incidence of Grade 3 or higher thrombocytopenia. Other ARs occurring in \ge 20\% of patients were neutropenia, fatigue, anemia, arthralgias, constipation, myalgias, mucositis, nausea, dyspnea, diarrhea, rash, febrile neutropenia, dizziness, edema, cough, headache, leukopenia, decreased appetite, URI, pneumonia, and transaminase increased. Additional Grade \ge 3 TEARs (\ge 10\%) included neutropenia, anemia, febrile neutropenia, leukopenia, pneumonia, and sepsis.
L2228: Discontinuation due to AR occurred in only 5% of patients in the pooled SAS. The most frequent ARs leading to discontinuation were febrile neutropenia (1%) and pneumonia (1%).
L2229: The important risks of myelosuppression and embryo-fetal toxicity are unchanged from IV decitabine based on the data presented in the submission.
L2230: Overall, given the historically poor outcomes of patients with MDS who are candidates for hypomethylating agent therapy, the level of toxicity observed with ASTX727 is acceptable for the clinical benefit observed. The toxicity profile is very similar to that observed with IV decitabine, with perhaps more myelosuppression, which can be managed with close monitoring and dose interruption or reduction, if required.
L2231: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L2232: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L2233: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L2234: The submitted clinical data from studies ASTX727-01-B (phase 2) and ASTX727-02 (phase 3) demonstrated:
L2235: - a CR rate of 18-21% with median duration of CR responses of 7.5 – 8.7 months. These results are similar to the ORRs (CR+PR) reported in the USPI for IV decitabine, which were 17% for the randomized trial and 16% for the single arm trial.
- 49-53% of patients dependent on transfusions at baseline achieved at least 56 days transfusion independence post-baseline and 63-64% of patients transfusion independent at baseline were able to maintain that transfusion independence for at least 56 days post-baseline.
L2236: Of note,
L2237: - both studies ASTX727-01-B (phase 2) and ASTX727-02 (phase 3) are considered as single arm trial in terms of efficacy assessment, therefore results of time to event endpoints are not interpretable.
L2238: 2) Study ASTX727-02 (phase 3) will be updated when all subjects complete a minimum of at least 12 months of follow-up.
L2239: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L2240: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L2241: The review team was able to verify or clarify the efficacy and safety endpoints as provided by the Applicant. The review team recommends approval of the NDA.

Full Prescribing Information
Indications and Usage	Included all French-American-British subtypes.	Revised indication to omit refractory anemia with excess blasts in transformation, because this subtype is now considered AML.
Dosage and Administration	...	Added a new subsection "important administration information" to include information about substitution and premedications, because this dosing-related information was considered sufficiently important for practitioners that it should precede the basic dosing information; this proposal is supported by the recommendations found in the Dosage and Administration guidance.
Included baseline testing and monitoring with recommended dosage.	Included this information with dosage modifications for adverse reactions to house all information regarding safety monitoring and adverse reactions together.
(b) (4)	Increased the maximum time to 4 weeks for consistency with intravenous decitabine.
L2261: (b) (4)	
Added instructions regarding to use the same or reduced dose following adequate resolution of these adverse reactions.
Added instructions to withhold for active or uncontrolled infection for consistency with intravenous decitabine.

Warnings and Precautions (W&P)	Included a W&P for myelosuppression.	Revised to describe the percentage of patients who needed a dose interruption and reduction or who permanently discontinued for myelosuppression to describe the tolerability of INQOVI.
Included W&P for embryo-fetal toxicity.	Removed (b) (4)
Adverse Reactions	(b) (4)	
Presented a pooled safety population for these 2 trials, because these trials included a similar study population.
Included serious adverse reactions, fatal adverse reactions, permanent discontinuation, dose interruptions and dose reductions to describe the tolerability of INQOVI.
Added a table to describe the laboratory abnormalities as reported in the lab dataset that worsened from baseline, because laboratory abnormalities are included in the definition of an adverse reaction as found in the

L2266: ## 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L2267: ## The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
L2268: No Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategy (REMS) is needed for the safe use of INQOVI for the proposed indication. The risks of INQOVI can be adequately managed in the post-market setting through product presentation and labeling.
L2269: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L2270: ## **The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:**
L2271: The following clinical pharmacology trials will be required:
L2272: ## **PMR**
L2273: - Submit the final report and datasets from a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to determine an appropriate starting dose of INQOVI in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment that may inform product labeling. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled; *Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Hepatic Function: Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing and Labeling*.
- Submit the final report and datasets from a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to determine an appropriate starting dose of INQOVI in patients with severe renal impairment and/or end-stage renal disease that may inform product labeling. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled; *Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Renal Function: Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing and Labeling*.
- Submit the final report with datasets from a thorough QT/QTc study to evaluate the effect of cedazuridine on QT interval prolongation that may further inform labelling. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the ICH E14 Guidances for Industry titled; *E14 Clinical Evaluation of QT/QTc Interval Prolongation and Proarrhythmic Potential for Non-Antiarrhythmic Drugs*, and *E14 Clinical Evaluation of QT/QTc Interval Prolongation and Proarrhythmic Potential for Non-Antiarrhythmic Drugs - Questions and Answers (R3)*.
L2274: ## **PMC:**
L2275: - Submit the final report and datasets from a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to determine the effect of food on decitabine and cedazuridine exposure when administered with INQOVI that may inform product labeling. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled; *Food-Effect Bioavailability and Fed Bioequivalence Studies*.

L2376: ## 19.4.2. Metabolism and Excretion
L2377: A Phase 1 open-label, non-randomized, 2-treatment period, fixed-sequence, mass balance study (E7727-01) of single doses of cedazuridine (E7727) was conducted in healthy subjects. During Period 1, subjects were administered 100 mg unlabeled E7727 orally and a microtracer dose of 100 µg 14C-E7727 IV. During Period 2, subjects were administered 100 mg 14C-E7727 orally. Blood and urine samples were collected up to 72 hours, and feces were collected in 24 hour intervals in Periods 1 and 2.
L2378: After a micro IV dose of E7727, 99% of total radioactivity was found in urine and &lt;1% in feces. Following oral dose of E7727, about 46% of total radioactivity (21% unchanged) was found in urine, and 51% (27%) in the feces. Table P provides PK parameters of E7727 in plasma.

L2382: In addition to parent cedazuridine (59% of total drug-related material), the cedazuridine-epimer (31%) and M266/1 metabolite (exchange of -OH by =O, ie, dehydrogenation, at 10%) were the other 2 major
L2383: entities present in plasma. As indicated in Table P, the systemic exposure to the epimer after oral administration in this study was approximately equal to the systemic exposure to cedazuridine. However, the exposure to the cedazuridine-epimer after IV administration was about 30% of that of cedazuridine.
L2384: ## 19.4.3. Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and In-Study Performance
L2385: The validated assay quantitation ranges were 0.5 to 200 ng/mL for decitabine, and 6 to 2310 ng/mL for cedazuridine (E7727), and 4 to 1460 ng/mL for cedazuridine epimer, with acceptable precision and accuracy. The validated stability assessments covered the sample handling and stability conditions during the analysis of clinical study samples. Refer to the Table Q for assay parameters during method validation.

L2400: ## 19.4.4. Pharmacometrics review report
L2401: ## 19.4.4.1. SUMMARY OF FINDINGS
L2402: ## 19.4.4.1.1. Key Review Questions
L2403: ASTX727 is a fixed dose combination of 100 mg of cedazuridine and 35 mg of decitabine. Decitabine is the active component that is thought to act through 1) incorporation into DNA causing apoptosis of proliferating cells and 2) inhibition of DNA methyltransferase which causes hypomethylation and consequently promotes cellular differentiation of future cancer cells. When taken orally, decitabine is rapidly metabolized by the liver's cytidine deaminase resulting in very low oral bioavailability. Cedazuridine is a potent inhibitor of cytidine deaminase and therefore increases decitabine oral bioavailability. Renal elimination is the major route of cedazuridine excretion. In the stomach, cedazuridine undergoes PH dependent conversion to its epimer (a less potent cytidine deaminase inhibitor) thus decreasing bioavailability of the potent epimer. Based on these disposition pathways, decitabine exposure is therefore dependent on factors influencing hepatic function, renal function and stomach PH. The Applicant evaluated potential covariates and developed population PK models that accounted for the effect of renal impairment and sex on cedazuridine clearance and rate of absorption respectively. In turn, cedazuridine affected decitabine hepatic clearance. The model also accounted for the effect of body size (height) on decitabine hepatic clearance (through empirical model of body size dependent hepatic and portal vein blood flow and volume) and sex on intravenous (IV) decitabine hepatic clearance and central volume. However, none of these covariates had a sufficiently large effect on clearance to justify dose adjustments. On the other hand, it is interesting that sex was identified as a covariate on clearance and absorption parameters while concomitant use of proton pump inhibitors (PPI) was not identified as a covariate to decitabine exposure. Based on this observation, the key pharmacometric review questions were:
L2404: - Do pharmacokinetic and exposure-vs-safety data support dose adjustment based on measures of body size (e.g body surface area or weight)?
- Do pharmacokinetic and exposure-vs-safety data support dose adjustment based on measures of renal impairment (e.g. creatinine clearance[CLCR])?
- Do pharmacokinetic data support concomitant use with proton pump inhibitors?
- Do pharmacokinetic and exposure-vs-safety data support dose adjustment based on biological sex?
L2405: ## 19.4.4.1.1.1. Do pharmacokinetic and exposure-vs-safety data support dose adjustment based on measures of body size (e.g body surface area or weight)?
L2406: No, dose adjustment is not needed based on body surface area (BSA) or weight (WT) for ASTX727. Although measures of body size (BSA and WT) were associated with decitabine exposure and with proportions of patients with any grade neutropenia, prediction from logistic regression model indicated only 5% difference in probability of any grade neutropenia between
L2407: lowest and highest BSA quartiles. This effect was not large enough to warrant dose adjustments.
L2408: ## **19.4.4.1.1.2. Do pharmacokinetic and exposure-vs-safety data support dose adjustment based on measures of renal impairment (e.g. creatinine clearance[CLCR])?**
L2409: No, dose adjustment is not needed for patients with mild and moderate renal impairment. Although renal function has a trend for association with decitabine exposure (p-value = 0.063) and with any grade thrombocytopenia (p-value = 0.06), the effects were not statistically significant (\alpha = 0.05) to warrant dose adjustments. Furthermore, CLCR was not associated with any grade neutropenia. ASTX727 is not recommended for use in patients with severe renal impairment, end stage renal disease and patients on dialysis since it was not studied in these patients.
L2410: ## **19.4.4.1.1.3. Do pharmacokinetic data support concomitant use with proton pump inhibitors?**
L2411: Yes, ASTX727 can be used concomitantly with PPI. Although decitabine exposure was higher in patients on concomitant PPI compared to other patients, the difference (less than 25%) does not support avoiding concomitant use of PPI with ASTX727.
L2412: ## **19.4.4.1.1.4. Do pharmacokinetic and exposure-vs-safety data support dose adjustment based on biological sex?**
L2413: No, there is no need for dose adjustments based on sex. Although male subjects had about 30% lower 5-days cumulative AUC compared to female subjects, no differences were observed when day 1, 2, and 5 AUCs were compared between the sexes separately. Furthermore, the association between sex and adverse events of any grade neutropenia (p-value = 0.1724 at treatment-cycle 2) and thrombocytopenia (p-value = 0.12) were not statistically significant. In totality, the difference between male and female AUCs and incidences of adverse events and do not warrant dose adjustment given that ASTX727 dosing will be down-titrated in subjects experiencing intolerable toxicity.
L2414: ## **19.4.4.1.2. Recommendations**
L2415: No dose adjustment is recommended for ASTX727 based on sex, BSA, WT or CLCR. However, female and patients with low BSA (&lt;1.97) and impaired renal function (CLCR &lt; 60 ml/minor category) should be monitored for cytopenia adverse events and dosing reduced as studied in phase 3 clinical trial.
L2416: ## **19.4.4.1.3. Label Statements**
L2417: ## **(12.2. Pharmacodynamics)**
L2418: Based on the exposure-response analyses, a relationship between an increase in 5-day cumulative daily decitabine exposure and a greater likelihood of some adverse reactions (e.g., any grade neutropenia, thrombocytopenia) was observed in clinical studies.
L2419: ## **(12.3. Pharmacokinetics/special populations)**
L2420: ## Decitabine exposure (AUC) increased with decreasing body surface area or body weight.
L2421: ## **19.4.4.2. Results of Applicant's Analysis**
L2422: ## **19.4.4.2.1. Applicants' population pharmacokinetics results.**
L2423: Data for PK analyses were obtained from phase 1 (ASTX727-01) and phase 2 (ASTX727-02) studies that were designed to compare the pharmacokinetics of ASTX727 to that of intravenous decitabine. In the phase 1 study, combinations of different doses of cedazuridine and decitabine were tested to identify optimal combination with comparable exposure to 20mg/Kg of IV decitabine. The optimal dose was compared to 20mg/Kg of IV decitabine in part-2 of the phase 1 study (ASTX727-01) to confirm dose-comparability and treatment outcomes. The phase 2 study was a bioequivalence and treatment-outcome study comparing optimal dose tested in phase 1B to 20mg/Kg of IV decitabine. Intensive PK sampling for characterization cedazuridine and decitabine PK were collected. The final dataset for PK analyses consisted of 18940 PK observations from 243 individuals. The patient population across all studies consisted of 168 males and 75 females, with a mean age of 70.3 years (with a range of 32.0-90.0 years), a mean weight of 83.1 kg (with a range of 41.0-158 kg), a mean height of 171 cm (with a range of 144-193 cm), a mean BSA of 1.97 m2 (with a range of 1.30-2.90 m2), a mean CrCL of 65.5 mL/min/1.73m2 (with a range of 27.7-156 mL/min/1.73m2), and a mean bilirubin of 13.2 umol/L (with a range of 3.0-51.3 umol/L)
L2424: The Applicant developed cedazuridine and decitabine population pharmacokinetics models sequentially. A cedazuridine semi-physiologic pharmacokinetic model was developed first (Figure 29); the estimated population PK parameters are given in Table 73. In brief, the model
L2425: ## 2.3. Reviewer's comments
L2426: The Applicant followed appropriate pharmaco-statistical principles for model development. However, the Applicant's final semi-physiologic PK model for IV decitabine under-predicts Cmax and AUCs.
L2427: Based on this, the reviewer performed additional model evaluation to look for any model misspecification (Figure 28).
L2428: After comparing observed versus model predicted AUC the reviewer found that the Applicant's model unacceptably underpredicted decitabine AUC (See Figure 28). An information request was sent to the Applicant to explain the identified underprediction of decitabine AUC. The Applicant acknowledges the underprediction and attributed it to
L2429: Due to this misspecification, the reviewer concludes that the Applicant's IV decitabine model failed to identify PK covariates. The Applicant did not assess impacts of covariates on oral decitabine clearance and distributions. Estimates obtained during IV decitabine model development were fixed when oral decitabine data were included for modeling. Based on this, results from the Applicant's model do not support labelling statements on dose adjustment in renal and hepatic impaired patient nor based on body surface area. Furthermore, predictions from the model cannot be used in exposure-vs-response or safety modeling.
L2430: ## 19.4.4.3. *Reviewer's Analysis*
L2431: ## 19.4.4.3.1. Introduction
L2432: The Applicant's semi-physiologic PK model underpredicted observed decitabine AUC. This can be attributed to structural model misspecifications particularly the un-estimated physiological parameters. This misspecification may cause inaccurate identification and characterization of effects covariates of PK parameters. Fortunately, due to intensive PK sampling, 5-day-AUC can be calculated from observed data and used to assess PK covariates.
L2433: Furthermore, identification of factors associated with treatment related adverse events can be extended by performing additional analyses e.g. assess factors associated with adverse events in cycle 1 and cycle 2 separately; and to use a sequential approach to building a GLM model for adverse events where by only potential predictors identified through univariate logistic regression analysis are included in the final multivariate model.
L2434: ## 19.4.4.3.2. Objectives
L2435: The reviewer performed additional PK and exposure-safety analyses to:
L2436: Identify intrinsic and extrinsic factors associated with decitabine AUC using intensively collected PK data
L2437: Identify predictors of neutropenia and thrombocytopenia in each treatment cycle separately.
L2438: ## 19.4.4.3.3. Methods
L2439: ## 19.4.4.3.3.1. *Data Sets*
L2440: Datasets for PK and exposure-safety analyses were constructed from ADAM datasets supplied by the Applicant i.e. adsl.xpt, lb.xpt, pc.xpt, and adae.xpt. For preparation of PK datasets, records on intensive PK sampling on day 1, 2 and 5 from both phase 1B (ASTX727-01-B) and phase 2 (ASTX727-02) studies were identified and used to calculate AUC between 0 – 8 hours (AUC0-8) for each day. AUCs were calculated using the linear trapezoidal method. For the decitabine PK dataset, cumulative five-day AUC was calculated by multiplying average of day 2 and day 5 AUC by 5 (5-day-AUC = (day2-AUC+day5-AUC)/2) while for IV decitabine 5-day-AUC was calculated by multiplying average of day 1 and day 5 AUC by 5 (5-day-AUC = (day1-AUC+day5-AUC)/2). Individuals with less than 5 PK observation in a day were removed from the dataset due to potentially unreliable AUC. Covariate information were obtained from adsl.xpt and lb.xpt datasets. Creatine clearance (CLCR) and estimated glomerular filtration rates (eGFR) were calculated using Cockcroft-Gault and MDRD equations respectively.
L2441: For exposure-safety datasets, dependent variables were identified from the AEHLT variable in the adae.xpt datasets. The NCYCSTD variable was used to identify treatment cycles, and 3 separate adverse events datasets were prepared for each treatment cycle. The pc.xpt datasets provided PK data for calculation of 5-day-AUC. The exposure and covariate datasets were joined to adverse events datasets to complete construction of analysis datasets.
L2442: ## **19.4.4.3.3.2. Software**
L2443: The R software for statistical computing (Version 3.5.3) was used for preparation of the analysis datasets, for graphical analyses and for linear and generalize linear modeling
L2444: ## **19.4.4.3.3.3. Models**
L2445: Univariate and multivariate linear and logistic regression models were used in the analyses. Before such analyses, collinearities among independent variables were assessed. In univariate analyses one independent variable was assessed for association with a dependent variable. For PK analysis, 5-day-AUC was log transformed to create a dependent variable. Independent variables with univariate p-values of &lt; 0.05 were included in the multivariate analysis regardless of collinearity among them to control for possible confounding effect. For exposure-safety analyses, variables which were collinear with AUC were not included in the multivariate analyses.
L2446: ## 19.4.4.3.4. Results
L2447: ## 19.4.4.3.4.1. *Extrinsic and intrinsic factors associated with decitabine AUC*
L2448: Figure 30 shows results from collinearity analyses among independent variables.
L2449: Graphical exploration and univariate analyses indicated that age, sex, weight, height, CLCR, and BSA were associated with oral decitabine 5-day-AUCs at p-values &lt; 0.05. A multivariate analysis identified both sex (p-value = 0.000082) and BSA (p-value = 0.020) to be the independent predictors of 5-day-AUC for oral decitabine (See Table 77). Table 77 indicates that male subjects (represented by intercept parameter) with age of 70 years, BSA of 1.73 m2 and CLCR of 90 mil/min would have about 30% lower 5-days-AUC compared to female patients of the same age, BSA, and CLCR.

The results from multivariate linear regression analyses are given in Table 78. These results indicate that only BSA is strongly associated with decitabine daily AUC after adjusting for sex, weight and height.

L2456: Concomitant use of proton pump inhibitors was assessed as a potential extrinsic factor impacting decitabine exposure. Data for concomitant drug use was obtained from *cm.xpt* dataset. The CMENRTPT variable was used to identify patients who were on ongoing PPI use and those who were not. Graphical and linear model analysis was used to determine if ongoing PPI use has impact on decitabine exposure. Figure 31 and **Table 79** shows that subjects on ongoing PPI use in the phase 2 study had 23.3% higher decitabine 5-day-AUC (P-value=0.009) compared to subjects not on PPI. Despite this significant trend the effect is considered small and is not consistent with results from the phase 1 study

L2461: ## **19.4.4.3.4.2. Factors associated with any grade neutropenias in phase 1B and 2 studies**
L2462: Graphical analyses for the relationship between any grade neutropenias in treatment-cycle 1 and different patient characteristics are given in Figure 32 and **Figure 33**. In univariate General Linear Model (GLM) analyses, none of the patient characteristics were associated with likelihood of any grade neutropenias.
L2463: **Figure 34 and Figure 35**, show results from graphical analyses for the relationship between any grade neutropenias in treatment-cycle 2 with different patient characteristics. Female subjects appear to higher proportions of any grade neutropenias compared to male subjects. However, in univariate GLM, sex was not a predictor of any grade neutropenias (p-value = 0.172), only day-5-AUC was associated with likelihood of any grade neutropenias (p-value = 0.0187). Nevertheless, in a multivariate GLM, independent variables included log-transformed 5-days-AUC, sex, and study (phase 1 or 2), treatment-sequence (Oral-&gt;IV or IV-&gt;oral) and interaction term between study and sequence. **Table 80** shows results from the multivariate logistic regression analysis. The multivariate analysis indicate that the likelihood of any grade neutropenia increased with increasing 5-days-AUC. Furthermore, subjects in phase 2 study had more likelihood of any grade neutropenias than in phase 1. Similarly, in treatment-cycle 2, subjects in phase 2 and in oral-&gt;IV sequence (i.e. receiving IV decitabine in cycle 2) had less likelihood of neutropenias compared to other study/sequence interactions.
L2464: **Figure 36** shows the proportions of patients with any grade neutropenias in treatment-cycle 3 for different categories of subject characteristics. The figure shows decreasing proportion of neutropenia with increasing body-surface area, and lower proportion with neutropenia in male subjects compared to female. In contrast, proportions of neutropenias seem to increase with higher 5-days-AUC and higher baseline neutrophil counts. However, in univariate analyses, none of the covariates are associated with neutropenia in treatment-cycle3 (p-values of 0.125, 0.449, 0.229, 0.165, for 5-days-AUC, BSA, baseline neutrophils, and sex). A multivariate analysis with 5-days-AUC, baseline neutrophils, study (phase 1 or 2), treatment-sequence (Oral-&gt;IV or IV-&gt;oral) and interaction term between study and sequence had a better fit than any other combination of independent variables. **Table 81** shows parameters of the multivariate model while **Figure 37** shows good agreement between observed and predicted proportions of neutropenia in different quantiles of 5-days-AUC and BSA for treatment cycle 3.
L2465: In conclusion, the results from these additional analyses do not warrant dose reduction based on the assessed patient factors.

L2471: Graphical and univariate GLM identified some covariates with trend for association with any grade thrombocytopenias in treatment cycles 1 and 2 (p-value &gt; 0.05 and p-value &lt;=0.20).

L2544: Kaplan-Meier survival curve showing Percent survival versus Day after Implant for the L1210 disseminated tumor mouse model. The legend indicates the following treatments: Vehicle, 100 mg/kg E7727, 0.4 mg/kg decitabine (PO), 100 mg/kg E7727 + 0.4 mg/kg decitabine (PO), 10 mg/kg E7727 + 0.4 mg/kg decitabine (PO), 1 mg/kg E7727 + 0.4 mg/kg decitabine (PO), and 0.4 mg/kg decitabine (IV). The Vehicle group shows the lowest survival, while the 100 mg/kg E7727 + 0.4 mg/kg decitabine (PO) group shows the highest survival, reaching approximately 90% survival by Day 25. The sample size is n=10.
L2545: Kaplan-Meier survival curve showing percent survival versus day after implant for the KG-1 disseminated tumor mouse model. The curve compares Vehicle control (n=10) with various doses of E7727 (cedazuridine) and decitabine (PO or IV). The legend lists the following treatments: Vehicle, 100 mg/kg E7727, 0.3 mg/kg decitabine (PO), 100 mg/kg E7727 + 0.3 mg/kg decitabine (PO), 10 mg/kg E7727 + 0.3 mg/kg decitabine (PO), 1 mg/kg E7727 + 0.3 mg/kg decitabine (PO), and 0.3 mg/kg decitabine (IV). The combination of 100 mg/kg E7727 + 0.3 mg/kg decitabine (PO) shows the highest survival rate, reaching approximately 90% survival by day 120, compared to 0% survival for the Vehicle control.

L2552: Figure A displays two plots related to the relationship between 5-day Cumulative Decitabine AUC and Complete Response (CR).
The left plot shows the Probability of Complete Response (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus Decitabine AUC Exposure (X-axis, 1000 to 3000 ng*hr/mL). The solid blue line represents a loess smooth of the original data, and the grey shaded region indicates a 95% Confidence Interval (CI) for the smooth. Error bars represent the 95% CI at quartiles of decitabine exposure. The P value is 0.72, indicating no significant relationship.
The right plot shows the number of Responders (n) and Complete Response (CR) percentage across four 5-day AUC Quartiles (ng.h/mL):

L2553: Figure B displays three exposure-response plots showing the relationship between 5-day cumulative decitabine exposure (AUC) and the proportion of patients experiencing adverse reactions (Grade Neutropenia or Grade Thrombocytopenia).
The top left plot, labeled "CYCLE 2: Both ASTX727 and IV Decitabine," shows the Proportion of patients with neutropenia versus 5 Day AUC (\text{h} \cdot \text{ng}/\text{mL}). Observed data points (black circles with error bars) and a predicted curve (red line) show a positive, sigmoidal relationship, indicating that higher AUC is associated with a higher proportion of patients experiencing neutropenia.
The top right plot, labeled "CYCLE 2: Both ASTX727 and IV Decitabine," shows the Proportion of patients with thrombocytopenia versus 5 Day AUC (\text{h} \cdot \text{ng}/\text{mL}). Observed data points and a predicted curve show a positive, sigmoidal relationship, indicating that higher AUC is associated with a higher proportion of patients experiencing thrombocytopenia.
The bottom plot, labeled "CYCLE 3: ASTX727 ONLY," shows the Proportion of patients with neutropenia versus 5 Day AUC (\text{h} \cdot \text{ng}/\text{mL}). Observed data points and a predicted curve show a positive, sigmoidal relationship, indicating that higher AUC is associated with a higher proportion of patients experiencing neutropenia.
In all plots, the X-axis ranges from 0 to 4000 \text{h} \cdot \text{ng}/\text{mL}, and the Y-axis ranges from 0.00 to 1.00. The predicted curves are fitted to the observed data points.

Bottom Right: Neutropenias combined (Cycles 1 and 2). The Y-axis is 5-day Cumulative AUC (0 to 3500). L2574: DOSE ESCALATION ALGORITHM
Subjects with IPSS Intermediate-1 or -2, or High-Risk MDS
DOSING
Cycle 1:
Day -3 (\pm 2 days)*: Single dose oral decitabine
Day 1: decitabine IV 20 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2
Days 2-5: cedazuridine + oral decitabine
Cycle 2:
Day -3 (\pm 2 days)*: Single dose cedazuridine
Days 1-5: cedazuridine + oral decitabine
Cycles \ge 3:
Days 1-5: cedazuridine + oral decitabine
Continue cedazuridine + oral decitabine until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity occurred, the subject withdrew consent or was withdrawn from study
decitabine AUC oral/IV > \text{approx } 1.10
decitabine AUC oral/IV < \text{approx } 0.90
When the target level of decitabine AUC after oral administration was within approximately 0.90-1.10 of IV decitabine AUC with no more than 1 DLT per 6 subjects, the cohort was expanded to reach up to 12-18 total subjects in the cohort to confirm the AUC range before opening the Dose Confirmation Stage. If the AUC was out of the desired range, or more than 1 DLT occurred per 6 subjects, the Data and Safety Review Committee could recommend further adjustment of the cedazuridine or decitabine dose in a new cohort of 3 subjects until the desired AUC range and safety were achieved in at least 3 subjects.

For 20/100, %AUCt is approximately 50 at DAY 2, 60 at DAY 5, and 60 at 5-DAY. For 20/60, %AUCt is approximately 30 at DAY 2, 40 at DAY 5, and 40 at 5-DAY. For 20/40, %AUCt is approximately 20 at DAY 2, 30 at DAY 5, and 30 at 5-DAY.
